pairs of free fermion oscillators
\{\psi^{a}_{m},\psi^{*}_{a,m}\>|\>1\leq a\leq n,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}
, which satisfy anti-commutation relations

\displaystyle\{\psi^{a}_{m},\psi^{*}_{b,m^{\prime}}\}=\delta^{a}_{b}\delta_{mm^{\prime}},\quad\{\psi^{a}_{m},{\psi}^{b}_{m^{\prime}}\}=0,\quad\{\psi^{*}_{a,m},\psi^{*}_{b,m^{\prime}}\}=0.

The algebra generated by these fermions with the above relations is denoted by
\mathrm{Cl}(n)
. The fermion Fock space
\mathcal{F}(n)
is a
\mathrm{Cl}(n)
-module generated by
\ket{\emptyset}
with relations
\psi^{*}_{a,m}\ket{\emptyset}=0
, for all
a,m
.

There is an algebra homomorphism from
U(\gl_{n}[z])
to a completion
\mathrm{Cl}(n)^{\wedge}
which is determined by

\displaystyle E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\psi^{a}_{\ell+m}\psi^{*}_{b,\ell}.

(4.29)

This algebra map induces a
\GL_{n}[z]
-module structure on the Fock space
\mathcal{F}(n)
, then the latter has eigenspace decomposition with respect to the action of
\mathbf{1}\otimes z^{0}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}(n)=\bigoplus_{N\geq 0}\mathcal{F}_{N}(n).

(4.30)

We note that
\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)
is the subspace of
\mathcal{F}(n)
spanned by elements of the form
{\psi}^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\cdots{\psi}^{a_{N}}_{m_{N}}\ket{\emptyset}
.

We can decompose
\mathcal{F}(n)
into a tensor product of its factors
\mathcal{F}(n)=\mathcal{F}(1)^{\otimes n}
, where the
a
-th tensor component
\mathcal{F}(1)
is the Fock space for the
a
-th pair
\{\psi^{a}_{m},\psi^{*}_{a,m}\>|\>m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}\}
. In view of this tensor decomposition, each direct summand on the right-hand-side of (
4.30
) decomposes as:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)=\bigoplus_{N_{1}+\cdots+N_{n}=N}\mathcal{F}_{N_{1}}(1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{F}_{N_{n}}(1).

(4.31)

We give a
\mathbb{Z}
-grading (i.e.
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant structure) on
\mathrm{Cl}(n)
by setting

\displaystyle\deg\psi^{a}_{p}=p,\quad\deg\psi^{*}_{b,q}=-q.

Then
\mathrm{Cl}(n)
is a graded algebra and
\mathcal{F}(n)
is a graded
\mathrm{Cl}(n)
-module. We note that
\deg(E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m})=m
in this grading, which is compatible with the natural grading on
\gl_{n}[z]
.

We can compute the
q
-character of
\mathcal{F}_{N}(1)
as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{ch}_{q}(\mathcal{F}_{N}(1))=\sum_{m_{1}>\cdots>m_{N}\geq 0}q^{\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{i}}=q^{\frac{N(N-1)}{2}}\prod_{i=1}^{N}\frac{1}{1-q^{i}}.

Using the decomposition (
4.31
) we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathrm{ch}_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\mathcal{F}_{N}(n))&=\sum_{N_{1}+\cdots+N_{n}=N}\prod_{l=1}^{n}\left(\mathbf{a}_{l}^{N_{l}}\mathrm{ch}_{q}(\mathcal{F}_{N_{l}}(1))\right)\\
\text{\small by \eqref{eq: character of Hilbert space k=1}}\quad&=\mathrm{ch}_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)).\end{split}

(4.32)

Proposition 4.8
.

The linear map
\mathfrak{f}:\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)
which maps the bases by

\displaystyle{\psi}^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\cdots{\psi}^{a_{N}}_{m_{N}}\ket{\emptyset}\mapstochar\rightarrow\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}(A^{a_{1}}X^{m_{1}})_{i_{1}}\cdots(A^{a_{N}}X^{m_{N}})_{i_{N}}\ket{\emptyset}

(4.33)

is a
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant isomorphism.

Proof.

It is straightforward to compute that
\mathfrak{f}
respects the
\mathbb{Z}
-grading and intertwines the
\gl_{n}[z]
-actions on
\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)
and
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)
respectively, so
\mathfrak{f}
is
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant. Let
L=\lfloor\frac{N}{n}\rfloor
and
r=N-nL
, then

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}({\psi}^{1}_{0}\cdots\psi^{r}_{0}\psi^{1}_{1}\cdots\psi^{n}_{1}\cdots{\psi}^{1}_{L}\cdots{\psi}^{n}_{L}\ket{\emptyset})

is a nonzero element in the ground states
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)_{0}

[
6
]
. By the
\gl_{n}[z]
-equivariant property of
\mathfrak{f}
and Corollary
5.4
,
\mathfrak{f}
is surjective. Since
\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)
has the same character as
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)
by (
4.32
),
\mathfrak{f}
is an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 4.2
.

The isomorphism
\mathfrak{f}
is not an isometry with respect to the natural Hermitian structures on
\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)
and on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)
. To see this, let us compute the commutator between
\mathbf{1}\otimes z=\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\psi^{a}_{\ell+1}\psi^{*}_{a,\ell}
and its Hermitian conjugate:

\displaystyle[(\mathbf{1}\otimes z)^{\dagger},\mathbf{1}\otimes z]=\left[\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}\psi^{a}_{\ell}\psi^{*}_{a,\ell+1},\;\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\psi^{b}_{m+1}\psi^{*}_{b,m}\right]=\psi^{a}_{0}\psi^{*}_{a,0}.

Meanwhile
\mathbf{1}\otimes z
acts on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)
as
\mathsf{t}_{0,1}
, and the commutator reads:

\displaystyle[\mathsf{t}_{0,1}^{\dagger},\mathsf{t}_{0,1}]=[\mathsf{t}_{1,0},\mathsf{t}_{0,1}]=\mathsf{t}_{0,0}=N.

The above example shows that
\mathfrak{f}
does not preserve the Hermitian conjugate of operator, thus
\mathfrak{f}
can not be an isometry.

Using the multiplication map
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
which is surjective by Corollary
5.5
, one obtain fermion Fock space presentation for general
k
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)^{\otimes k}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k).

(4.34)

This map has nontrivial kernel if
k\geq 2
, which is closely related to level-rank duality, see Section
5.3
and also
[
8
, 4.1]
.

5
Operators

5.1
\gl_{n}[z]
action on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)

We have already seen that the flavour symmetry
\GL_{n}
acts on
\mathcal{M}(N,n)
naturally. In the Quot scheme description
\mathcal{M}(N,n)\cong\mathrm{Quot}^{N}({\mathcal{O}}^{\oplus n}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})
, the
\GL_{n}
action is by the automorphism on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\oplus n}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
. It turns out that
{\mathcal{O}}^{\oplus n}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}}
admits a bigger automorphism group, denoted by
\GL_{n}[z]
.
\GL_{n}[z]
is the functor that associate a
\mathbb{C}
-scheme
S
to
\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S)
.
\GL_{n}[z]
is represented by a group ind-scheme by
[
50
, Lemma 4.1.4]
.

\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S)
acts on
{\mathcal{O}}^{\oplus n}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\times S}
naturally, and the action is obviously compatible with base change
S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
, thus there is a natural action of
\GL_{n}[z]
on
\mathrm{Quot}^{N}({\mathcal{O}}^{\oplus n}_{{\mathbb{A}}^{1}})
.
\GL_{n}[z]
acts on
\mathcal{E}_{\mathrm{univ}}
by pullback, so the action extends to the determinant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\det}
.

The Lie algebra of
\GL_{n}[z]
is the positive part of loop algebra of
\gl_{n}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\mathrm{Lie}(\GL_{n}[z])=\{g\in\GL_{n}[z](\Spec\>\mathbb{C}[\epsilon]/(\epsilon^{2}))\>|\>g\equiv\mathrm{id}\pmod{\epsilon}\}\cong\gl_{n}[z].

The
\GL_{n}[z]
action on
\mathcal{L}_{\det}
induces a
\gl_{n}[z]
-module structure on
\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\cong\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
. Moreover, for every
\vec{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
,
\GL_{n}[z]
acts on the fiber
h^{-1}(\vec{x})
, and the specialization map

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma(h^{-1}(\vec{x}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(\vec{x})})

is a
\gl_{n}[z]
-module map.

We note that the
\GL_{n}[z]
-action on
\mathcal{M}(N,n)
is induced from the following
\GL_{n}[z]
-action on
V(N,n)
. In fact,
V(N,n)
represents the following moduli data

\displaystyle V(N,n)(S)=\{(\mathcal{E},\varphi,\psi)\>|\>\mathcal{E}\in\mathrm{Coh}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S),\>\varphi\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S}^{\oplus n},\mathcal{E}),\>\psi:\mathcal{E}\cong\mathcal{O}_{S}^{\oplus N}\}/\mathrm{equivalence}.

Then
\GL_{n}(\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S)
acts on
V(N,n)(S)
via its natural action on
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}\times S}^{\oplus n}
, which is obviously compatible with base change
S^{\prime}\rightarrow S
. Thus there is a natural action of
\GL_{n}[z]
on
V(N,n)
, which induces a
\gl_{n}[z]
-module structure on
\mathbb{C}[V(N,n)]\cong\mathcal{H}_{N}(n)
.

Lemma 5.1
.

The
\gl_{n}[z]
-module structure on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n)
agrees with the one induced by the Lie algebra map
\rho:\gl_{n}[z]\rightarrow D(V(N,n))
such that

\displaystyle\rho(E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m})=A^{a}_{i}(X^{m})^{i}_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial A^{b}_{j}}.

(5.1)

Proof.

It is enough to work out the action of
E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m}\in\gl_{n}[z]
on
V(N,n)
. Let
(X,A)\in V(N,n)
, then
1+\epsilon E^{a}_{b}z^{m}\in\mathrm{Lie}(\GL_{n}[z])
acts on
(X,A)
by precomposing
A:\mathcal{O}^{\oplus n}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}
with the automorphism
1+\epsilon E^{a}_{b}z^{m}
. Since
A
is
\mathcal{O}^{\oplus n}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}
-module map, the action can be explicitly written as

\displaystyle(X,A)\mapstochar\rightarrow(X,A+\epsilon X^{m}\circ A\circ E^{a}_{b}).

Equivalently, the above formula of the action determines a tangent field
A^{a}_{i}(X^{m})^{i}_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial A^{b}_{j}}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Since the action of
\gl_{n}[z]
on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is the induced from the action of
\gl_{n}[z]
on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n)
by restriction to the subspace
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)=\mathcal{H}_{N}(n)^{\GL_{N},-k}
, we conclude that the
\gl_{n}[z]
-module structure of
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is the one induced from the Lie algebra map (
5.1
). As a corollary, we have the following

Proposition 5.2
.

The action of the central
\gl_{1}[z]\subset\gl_{n}[z]
on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is given by

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}\otimes z^{m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\text{ multiplication by }k\cdot\mathrm{Tr}(X^{m})\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}].

Proof.

Applying the moment map relation
\mu^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}(E^{i}_{j})+k\delta^{i}_{j}=0
to (
5.1
), we get

\displaystyle\rho(\mathbf{1}\otimes z^{m})

\displaystyle=k\cdot\mathrm{Tr}(X^{m})-(X^{m})^{i}_{j}X^{l}_{i}\frac{\partial}{\partial X^{l}_{j}}+(X^{m})^{i}_{j}X^{j}_{l}\frac{\partial}{\partial X^{i}_{l}}

\displaystyle=k\cdot\mathrm{Tr}(X^{m})-(X^{m+1})^{l}_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial X^{l}_{j}}+(X^{m+1})^{i}_{l}\frac{\partial}{\partial X^{i}_{l}}

\displaystyle=k\cdot\mathrm{Tr}(X^{m}).

∎

In particular, we see that the action of
\gl_{n}[z]
on the central fiber
\Gamma(h^{-1}(0),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(0)})
factors through
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z]\oplus\gl_{1}
.

We have seen that
\Gamma(h^{-1}(0),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(0)})\cong\Gamma(\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))
. According to
[
55
, 4.1]
,
\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}}
is a closed subvariety of a connected component
\Gr^{(N)}_{\GL_{n}}
of the affine Grassmannian
\Gr_{\GL_{n}}
which is the moduli ind-scheme of lattices
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{C}(\!(z)\!)^{\oplus n}
such that

\displaystyle\dim(\mathbb{C}[\![z]\!]^{\oplus n}/z^{M}\Lambda)=N+Mn,\quad\forall M\gg 0.

The
\GL_{n}[z]
-action on
\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}}
is the restriction of the natural
\GL_{n}[z]
-action on
\Gr^{(N)}_{\GL_{n}}
, and we have an induced
\gl_{n}[z]
-module map
5
5

5

\Gamma(\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))^{*}
is called an affine Demazure module of
\GL_{n}[z]

[
56
,
24
]
.

\displaystyle\Gamma(\Gr^{(N)}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))\rightarrow\Gamma(\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k)).

(5.2)

The follow well-known result is a Borel-Weil-Bott type theorem for the Kac-Moody Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(n)
.

Proposition 5.3

(Theorem 2.5.5 in
[
50
]
)
.

The action of
\mathfrak{gl}_{n}[z]
on
\Gamma(\Gr^{(N)}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))
extends to an action of
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}\oplus\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}
. Moreover,
\Gamma(\Gr^{(N)}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))
is dual to
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{-kN}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
, where
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})
is the level
k
integrable representation with highest weight
k\varpi_{n-r}
of
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)
and
\mathrm{Fock}_{-kN}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
is the Fock module of
\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}
of weight
-kN
. Here
r=N-\lfloor\frac{N}{n}\rfloor n
.

It is known that the lowest energy subspace
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})^{*}_{0}\subset L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})^{*}
is isomorphic to
L_{k\varpi_{r}}({\mathfrak{sl}}_{n})
(irreducible
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
module of highest weight
k\varpi_{r}
), and
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})^{*}
is generated from
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})^{*}_{0}
by the actions of
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z]
. Since
L_{k\varpi_{r}}({\mathfrak{sl}}_{n})
is an irreducible
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
-module, it follows that for every nonzero
v\in L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})^{*}_{0}
, the action map
U(\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z])\rightarrow U(\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z])\cdot v
is surjective onto
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})^{*}
.

According to
[
52
, Ch.XVIII]
, the map (
5.2
) is surjective. Moreover, (
5.2
) maps the lowest energy subspace
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})^{*}_{0}
isomorphically onto the lowest energy subspace
\Gamma(\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))_{0}\subset\Gamma(\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))
. Thus for every nonzero
v\in\Gamma(\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))_{0}
, the action map
U(\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z])\rightarrow U(\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z])\cdot v
is surjective onto
\Gamma(\bGr^{N\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))
. Using graded Nakayama lemma, one can extend the above surjectivity results to
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
, namely we have the following.

Corollary 5.4
.

For every nonzero
v\in\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
, the action map
U(\gl_{n}[z])\rightarrow U(\gl_{n}[z])\cdot v
is surjective onto
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
. In particular,
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is a cyclic
\gl_{n}[z]
-module.

Proof.

Denote by
M:=U(\gl_{n}[z])\cdot v
the subspace generated from
v
by the action of
\gl_{n}[z]
. As we have explained above,
M/\mathfrak{m}M\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)/\mathfrak{m}\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is surjective where
\mathfrak{m}
is the maximal ideal of
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}]
corresponding to the origin
0\in\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
. The action
\gl_{n}[z]
on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant, thus
M
is a graded subspace. It follows from graded Nakayama lemma that
M\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is surjective. We present the detail below for the convenience of reader.

Suppose that
M\neq\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
, then let
w\in\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\setminus M
be a homogeneous element such that its energy grading is smallest among
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\setminus M
. The existence of such element is a consequence of the fact that the energy grading of
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is bounded from below.

We claim that the image of
w
under the specialization map
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\rightarrow\Gamma(h^{-1}(0),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(0)})
can not be zero. Otherwise
w\in\mathfrak{m}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
. Write
w=\sum_{i}\alpha_{i}\cdot h_{i}
such that
\alpha_{i}
’s are homogeneous elements in
\mathfrak{m}
and
h_{i}
’s are homogeneous elements in
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
. Since
\alpha_{i}
has positive energy, then
h_{i}
must belongs to
M
by the choice of
w
. By the Proposition
5.2
, for every
\alpha_{i}
there exists
\beta_{i}\in U(\gl_{1}[z])
such that
\rho(\beta_{i})=\alpha_{i}
, thus
w\in M
, a contradiction. This proves our claim.

Next, let
\bar{w}
be the image of
w
in
\Gamma(h^{-1}(0),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(0)})
, and let
\bar{v}
be the image of
v
in the lowest energy subspace
\Gamma(h^{-1}(0),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(0)})_{0}
. According to our previous discussion, there exists
g\in U(\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z])
such that
\bar{w}=g\cdot\bar{v}
. We note that

\displaystyle\deg(w)=\deg(\bar{w})=\deg(g\cdot\bar{v})=\deg(g\cdot v),

thus
w-g\cdot v
is a homogeneous element in
\mathcal{H}(n,k)
which has the same degree with
w
. We also note that
w-g\cdot v\notin M
because
w\notin M
and
g\cdot v\in M
. Then according to our claim, the image of
w-g\cdot v
in
\Gamma(h^{-1}(0),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(0)})
is nonzero, this contradicts with
\bar{w}=g\cdot\bar{v}
. Therefore
M=\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
.
∎

Corollary 5.5
.

The multiplication map
\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det})^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})
is surjective.

Proof.

According to Corollary
5.4
, it suffices to show that the map between ground states

\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det})_{0}^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})_{0}

is nonzero. Using Remark
4.1
, it is equivalent to show that
\Gamma(F_{\lambda},\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{F_{\lambda}})^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\Gamma(F_{\lambda},\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{F_{\lambda}})
is nonzero. Consider the projections to the highest weight spaces

\displaystyle\Gamma(F_{\lambda},\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{F_{\lambda}})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{\{z^{\lambda}\}},\qquad\Gamma(F_{\lambda},\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{F_{\lambda}})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{\{z^{\lambda}\}}

which are both nonzero. The tensor product map for the fiber at
\{z^{\lambda}\}
is an isomorphism, i.e.
(\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{\{z^{\lambda}\}})^{\otimes k}\cong\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{\{z^{\lambda}\}}
. This implies that
\Gamma(F_{\lambda},\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{F_{\lambda}})^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\Gamma(F_{\lambda},\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{F_{\lambda}})
is nonzero, whence the corollary follows.
∎

Remark 5.1
.

Combine Corollary
5.5
and Proposition
4.8
, we see that
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is spanned by elements of form

\displaystyle\prod_{\alpha=1}^{k}\left[\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{1}}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}})_{i_{1}}\cdots(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N}}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{N}})_{i_{N}}\right]\ket{\emptyset},\quad\text{where }\;1\leq a^{(\alpha)}_{j}\leq n,\;m^{(\alpha)}_{j}\in\mathbb{N}.

(5.3)

5.2
DDCA action on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)

The deformed double current algebra (DDCA) was defined by Guay for type A in
[
34
]
and later defined for other types by Guay and Yang in
[
35
]
. In this paper we focus on the type A, and we use a slightly different version of DDCA, which was introduced in
[
9
]
. The relation between our definition and Guay’s original definition is pointed out in
[
9
, 2.5]
.

Definition 5.1

(see
[
9
, Definition 2.0.5]
)
.

The deformed double current algebra
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
is defined to be the
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{1},\epsilon_{2}]
-algebra generated by
\{\mathsf{T}_{p,q}(x),\mathsf{t}_{p,q}\>|\>x\in\gl_{n},(p,q)\in{\mathbb{N}}^{2}\}
with the relations (
A0
)-(
A4
) as follows.

\mathsf{T}_{r,s}(1)=\epsilon_{2}\mathsf{t}_{r,s},\;\mathsf{T}_{r,s}(ax+by)=a\mathsf{T}_{r,s}(x)+b\mathsf{T}_{r,s}(y),\;\forall(a,b)\in\mathbb{C}^{2},

(A0)

[\mathsf{T}_{0,0}(x),\mathsf{T}_{0,m}(y)]=\mathsf{T}_{0,m}([x,y]),\;[\mathsf{T}_{0,0}(x),\mathsf{t}_{0,m}]=0,

(A1)

\text{ for }p+q\leq 2,\begin{cases}&[\mathsf{t}_{p,q},\mathsf{T}_{r,s}(x)]=(sp-rq)\mathsf{T}_{p+r-1,q+s-1}(x),\\
&[\mathsf{t}_{p,q},\mathsf{t}_{r,s}]=(sp-rq)\mathsf{t}_{p+r-1,q+s-1},\end{cases}

(A2)

To write down (
A3
)-(
A4
), we introduce notation
\epsilon_{3}=-n\epsilon_{1}-\epsilon_{2}
, and

\mathsf{T}_{u,r,t,s}(x\otimes y):=\mathsf{T}_{u,r}(x)\mathsf{T}_{t,s}(y)

for
x,y\in\mathfrak{gl}_{n}
, and
\Omega:=E^{a}_{b}\otimes E^{b}_{a}\in\mathfrak{gl}_{n}^{\otimes 2}
, then

\begin{cases}&\begin{aligned} =&\mathsf{T}_{1,m}([x,y])-\frac{\epsilon_{3}m}{2}\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1}(\{x,y\})-n\epsilon_{1}\mathrm{tr}(y)\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1}(x)\\
&+\epsilon_{1}\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{j+1}{m+1}\mathsf{T}_{0,j,0,m-1-j}(([x,y]\otimes 1)\cdot\Omega)\\
&+\epsilon_{1}\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\mathsf{T}_{0,j,0,m-1-j}((x\otimes y-xy\otimes 1)\cdot\Omega)\end{aligned}\\
&[\mathsf{T}_{1,0}(x),\mathsf{t}_{0,m}]=m\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1}(x),\end{cases}

(A3)

\begin{split}&[\mathsf{t}_{3,0},\mathsf{t}_{0,m}]=3m\mathsf{t}_{2,m-1}+\frac{m(m-1)(m-2)}{4}(\epsilon_{1}^{2}-\epsilon_{2}\epsilon_{3})\mathsf{t}_{0,m-3}\\
&-\frac{3\epsilon_{1}}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{m-3}(j+1)(m-2-j)(\mathsf{T}_{0,j,0,m-3-j}(\Omega)+\epsilon_{1}\epsilon_{2}\mathsf{t}_{0,j}\mathsf{t}_{0,m-3-j}),\;(m\geq 3)\end{split}

(A4)

Remark 5.2
.

Let us take
x=E^{a}_{b}
,
y=E^{c}_{d}
in (
A3
), then the 2nd and the 3rd line of (
A3
) can be written explicitly as follows:

\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{j+1}{m+1}\mathsf{T}_{0,j,0,m-1-j}(([x,y]\otimes 1)\cdot\Omega)+\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\mathsf{T}_{0,j,0,m-1-j}((x\otimes y-xy\otimes 1)\cdot\Omega)\\
=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\mathsf{T}_{0,j}(E^{a}_{d})\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1-j}(E^{c}_{b})-\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{j+1}{m+1}\left(\delta^{a}_{d}\mathsf{T}_{0,j}(E^{c}_{f})\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1-j}(E^{f}_{b})+\delta^{c}_{b}\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1-j}(E^{a}_{f})\mathsf{T}_{0,j}(E^{f}_{d})\right).

(5.4)

We note that the relations (
A0
)-(
A4
) become linear after setting
\epsilon_{1}=0
. In fact,
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}/(\epsilon_{1})
is isomorphic to the universal enveloping algebra of the Lie algebra
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}^{\sim}
. Here
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})
is the algebra of
\epsilon_{2}
-differential operators on
\mathbb{C}
, i.e.
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\cong\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{2}]\langle x,y\rangle/([y,x]=\epsilon_{2})
.
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}^{\sim}
is defined to be the
\mathbb{C}[\epsilon_{2}]
-submodule of
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}[\epsilon^{\pm}]
generated by
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathfrak{sl}_{K}
and
\frac{1}{\epsilon_{2}}\cdot D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes 1
.
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}^{\sim}
is actually a Lie subalgebra of
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}[\epsilon^{\pm}]

[
9
, Lemma 2.0.2]
. We can regard
D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}^{\sim}
as a version of “double current algebra”, meaning that it is obtained from
\gl_{K}
by joining two variables
x
and
y
. Since
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
is a deformation of
U(D_{\epsilon_{2}}(\mathbb{C})\otimes\mathfrak{gl}_{K}^{\sim})
, this explains the terminology “deformed double current algebra”.

According to
[
22
,
9
]
, the deformed double current algebra
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
acts on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
via the following assignment:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{1}\mapstochar\rightarrow 1,\quad\epsilon_{2}\mapstochar\rightarrow k,\quad\mathsf{T}_{p,q}(E^{a}_{b})\mapstochar\rightarrow A^{a}\mathrm{Sym}(Y^{p}X^{q})B_{b},\quad\mathsf{t}_{p,q}\mapstochar\rightarrow\mathrm{Tr}\>\mathrm{Sym}(Y^{p}X^{q}).

(5.5)

Here
\mathrm{Sym}(\cdots)
means averaging over permutations, and we have omitted the dummy indices that are contracted, for example

\mathsf{T}_{1,1}(E^{a}_{b})\mapstochar\rightarrow\frac{1}{2}A^{a}_{i}Y^{i}_{j}X^{j}_{l}B^{l}_{b}+\frac{1}{2}A^{a}_{i}X^{i}_{j}Y^{j}_{l}B^{l}_{b}.

We note that
\{\mathsf{T}_{0,p}(E^{a}_{b})\>|\>1\leq a,b\leq n,p\in\mathbb{N}\}
generates a subalgebra
U(\gl_{n}[z])\subset\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
, and the action of
U(\gl_{n}[z])
agrees with the one defined by (
5.1
).

There is a natural grading on
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
given by
[
9
, (2.6)]

\displaystyle\deg(\mathsf{T}_{p,q}(E^{a}_{b}))=\deg(\mathsf{t}_{p,q})=q-p,\quad\deg(\epsilon_{1})=\deg(\epsilon_{2})=0.

(5.6)

Under this grading, the action of
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant.

\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
has a skew-linear anti-involution
\sigma:\mathsf{A}^{(n)}\cong\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
which is determined by

\displaystyle\sigma(\epsilon_{1})=\epsilon_{1},\quad\sigma(\epsilon_{2})=\epsilon_{2},\quad\sigma(\mathsf{T}_{p,q}(E^{a}_{b}))=\mathsf{T}_{q,p}(E^{b}_{a}),\quad\sigma(\mathsf{t}_{p,q})=\mathsf{t}_{q,p}.

(5.7)

Skew-linearity means
\sigma(c\cdot\mathfrak{a})=\bar{c}\cdot\sigma(\mathfrak{a})
for all
c\in\mathbb{C}
and all
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
. Anti-involution means that
\sigma(\mathfrak{a}\cdot\mathfrak{b})=\sigma(\mathfrak{b})\cdot\sigma(\mathfrak{a})
for all
\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{b}\in\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
and
\sigma^{2}=\mathrm{id}
.

Proposition 5.6
.

The Hermitian inner product
\langle\cdot|\cdot\rangle
on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is skew-invariant under the action of
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\langle\sigma(\mathfrak{a})\cdot w|{v}\rangle=\langle w|\mathfrak{a}\cdot v\rangle

(5.8)

for all
\ket{w},\ket{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
and all
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
.

Proof.

Let
\rho_{N}:\mathsf{A}^{(n)}\rightarrow\mathrm{End}(\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k))
be the algebra map determined by (
5.5
), then we see that

\rho_{N}(\sigma(\mathfrak{a}))=\rho_{N}(\mathfrak{a})^{\dagger}

for all
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
, whence the proposition follows.
∎

Corollary 5.7
.

\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is a simple module of
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that every nonzero element
\ket{w}\in\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
generates
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
via the
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
-action. First of all we notice that
\mathsf{t}_{1,1}
acts on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
as the energy grading operator (up to a constant shift). Let us we write
\ket{w}=\sum_{i\in I}\ket{w_{i}}
where
I
is a finite index set and each
\ket{w_{i}}
is homogeneous such that
\deg\ket{w_{i}}\neq\deg\ket{w_{j}}
whenever
i\neq j
, then
\{\ket{w_{i}}\}_{i\in I}
belongs to
\mathbb{C}[\mathsf{t}_{1,1}]\cdot\ket{w}
. Therefore we can assume that
\ket{w}
is homogeneous without loss of generality.

Since the Hermitian inner product
\langle\cdot|\cdot\rangle
on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is nondegenerate, there exists
\ket{w^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
such that
\langle w^{\prime}|w\rangle\neq 0
. Let us choose a nonzero
\ket{v_{0}}\in\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
, then by Corollary
5.4
, there exists
\mathfrak{a}\in U(\gl_{n}[z])\subset\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
such that
\ket{w^{\prime}}=\mathfrak{a}\ket{v_{0}}
. Without loss of generality, we can also take
\mathfrak{a}
to be homogeneous with respect to the grading (
5.6
). By the skew-invariance (
5.8
), we have

\displaystyle 0\neq\langle w^{\prime}|w\rangle=\langle v_{0}|\sigma(\mathfrak{a})|w\rangle.

Denote
\ket{v}:=\sigma(\mathfrak{a})|w\rangle
, then
\ket{v}
is homogeneous and
\langle v_{0}|v\rangle\neq 0
, which implies that
\ket{v}
belongs to the lowest energy subspace
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
, because
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
is orthogonal to
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{>0}
. According to Corollary
5.4
,
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)=U(\gl_{n}[z])\ket{v}
, thus
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)=\mathsf{A}^{(n)}\ket{w}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

It is shown in
[
9
, 2.8]
that there is a Yangian subalgebra
Y(\gl_{n})\subset\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
such that

\displaystyle Y(\gl_{n})\ni T^{a}_{b}(u)\mapstochar\rightarrow\delta^{a}_{b}+A^{a}\frac{1}{u-XY}B_{b}

(5.9)

when it acts on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
. Here
T^{a}_{b}(u)=\delta^{a}_{b}+\sum_{n\geq 0}T^{a}_{b;n}u^{-n-1},1\leq a,b\leq n
is the RTT generator of
Y(\gl_{n})
, i.e. it satisfies the RTT relation:

\displaystyle(u-v)[T^{a}_{b}(u),T^{c}_{d}(v)]=T^{c}_{b}(u)T^{a}_{d}(v)-T^{c}_{b}(v)T^{a}_{d}(u).

We note that the Yangian action on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
preserves the energy grading, i.e.

[Y(\gl_{n}),\mathsf{t}_{1,1}]=0
, thus every energy eigenspace
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)^{\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times},d}
of
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-weight
d
is a Yangian submodule.

Moreover,
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is a unitary module of
Y(\gl_{n})
, i.e. the Hermitian inner product
\langle\cdot|\cdot\rangle
on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is skew-invariant under the action of
Y(\gl_{n})
, i.e.

\displaystyle\langle T^{a}_{b}(u)\cdot w|{v}\rangle=\langle w|T^{b}_{a}(u)\cdot v\rangle

for all
\ket{w},\ket{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
. If
S\subset\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)^{\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times},d}
is a
Y(\gl_{n})
submodule, then its orthogonal complement
S^{\perp}
in
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)^{\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times},d}
is also a
Y(\gl_{n})
submodule by the unitarity. Therefore we have the following.

Corollary 5.8
.

\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is a semisimple module of the Yangian
Y(\gl_{n})
with finite dimensional simple constituent.

See Remark
8.6
for a characterization of simple
Y(\gl_{n})
-submodules of
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
. See also Theorem
9.3
.

5.3
Level-rank relation

Consider the tensor decomposition
\mathbb{C}^{kN}=\mathbb{C}^{N}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{k},\mathbb{C}^{kn}=\mathbb{C}^{n}\otimes\mathbb{C}^{k}
with
\GL_{k}
naturally acting on the second tensor components, then we have isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(kN,kn)^{\GL_{k}}\cong\mathcal{M}(N,n).

(5.10)

Let us denote
\mathcal{L}_{\det,k}
to be the determinant line bundle on
\mathcal{M}(kN,kn)
, then the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{\det,k}
to the
\GL_{k}
-fixed locus
\mathcal{M}(N,n)
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{\det,1}^{\otimes k}
. The restriction map gives a homomorphism:

\displaystyle\tau:\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,m)=\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(kN,kn),\mathcal{L}_{\det,k}^{\otimes m})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det,1}^{\otimes km})=\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,km),

(5.11)

which we shall call it the
level-rank map
.

Proposition 5.9
.

The level-rank map
\tau
in (
5.11
) is
\mathbb{Z}
-graded, surjective and
\gl_{n}[z]\oplus\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]
-equivariant, where
\gl_{n}[z]\oplus\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]
acts on
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,m)
via the inclusion
\gl_{n}[z]\oplus\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]\subset\gl_{kn}[z]
and
\gl_{n}[z]\oplus\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]
acts on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,km)
via the projection to the first component.

Proof.

Recall that the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\gl_{kn}[z]
-module structure on
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,m)
is induced from the
\GL_{kn}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
action on the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\det,k}^{\otimes m}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(kN,kn)
. We notice that the
\GL_{k}
fixed point locus
\mathcal{M}(kN,kn)^{\GL_{k}}
is
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-closed, and it is also fixed by
\GL_{k}[z]
such that
\SL_{k}[z]
acts on the restriction of determinant line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\det,1}|_{\mathcal{M}(kN,kn)^{\GL_{k}}}
trivially, thus (
5.11
) is
\mathbb{Z}
-graded and
\gl_{n}[z]\oplus\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]
-equivariant.

By the
\gl_{n}[z]
-equivariance of (
5.11
) and the cyclicity of
\gl_{n}[z]
-action on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,km)
(Corollary
5.4
), to show that (
5.11
) is surjective, it suffices to show that the induced map between ground states
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,m)_{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,km)_{0}
is nonzero. Using Remark
4.1
, we only need to show that the restriction map

\displaystyle\Gamma(\GL_{nk}\cdot\{z^{\lambda_{k}}\},\mathcal{L}_{\det,k}^{\otimes m}|_{\GL_{nk}\cdot\{z^{\lambda_{k}}\}})\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\Gamma(\GL_{n}\cdot\{z^{\lambda_{k}}\},\mathcal{L}_{\det,k}^{\otimes m}|_{\GL_{n}\cdot\{z^{\lambda_{k}}\}}).

is nonzero, where
\lambda_{k}=\varpi_{kr}+L\cdot\varpi_{kn}
is a coweight for
\GL_{kn}
. Notice that both sides in the above map have nontrivial image after further restriction to
\mathcal{L}_{\det,k}^{\otimes m}|_{\{z^{\lambda_{k}}\}}
, thus the above map is nontrivial, which proves the surjectivity of (
5.11
).
∎

Proposition
5.9
implies that the level-rank map
\tau
in (
5.11
) factors through a
\gl_{n}[z]
-equivariant surjective map

\displaystyle\bar{\tau}:\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,m)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,km),

(5.12)

where
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,m)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}
is the space of coinvariants, i.e.
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,m)/\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]\cdot\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,m)
. We shall call
\bar{\tau}
the
reduced level-rank map
.

5.3.1
The case of
m=1

Let us focus on the case when
m=1
which is closely related to the level-rank duality, as pointed out in
[
8
]
.

Consider the
\mathfrak{h}[z]
-coinvariant
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]}
where
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{sl}_{k}
is the Cartan subalgebra. In the view of isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(kN,kn)^{T}\cong\coprod_{N_{1}+\cdots+N_{k}=kN}\mathcal{M}(N_{1},n)\times\cdots\times\mathcal{M}(N_{k},n),

(5.13)

where
T\subset\GL_{k}
is the maximal torus, Corollary
4.7
implies that the restriction to
T
-fixed point locus induces an isomorphism between
\mathfrak{t}[z]
-modules:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)\cong\bigoplus_{N_{1}+\cdots+N_{k}=kN}\mathcal{H}_{N_{1}}(n,1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N_{k}}(n,1),

(5.14)

where
\mathfrak{t}\cong\mathbb{C}^{k}
is the Lie algebra of
T
. According to Proposition
5.2

\mathfrak{t}[z]
acts on
\mathcal{H}_{N_{1}}(n,1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N_{k}}(n,1)
by

\displaystyle(t_{1},\cdots,t_{k})\otimes z^{r}\mapstochar\rightarrow(t_{1}\mathrm{Tr}(X^{r}_{1}),\cdots,t_{k}\mathrm{Tr}(X^{r}_{k}))\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N_{1})}\times\cdots\times\mathbb{A}^{(N_{k})}].

In particular
\mathfrak{t}
acts on
\mathcal{H}_{N_{1}}(n,1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N_{k}}(n,1)
with weight
(N_{1},\cdots,N_{k})
, therefore the
\mathfrak{h}
-coinvariant
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}}
is
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)
. Further taking
z\mathfrak{h}[z]
-covariant amounts to taking quotient of
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)^{\otimes k}
by the action of

\displaystyle\{\mathrm{Tr}(X^{r}_{i})-\mathrm{Tr}(X^{r}_{i+1})\>|\>1\leq i<k,r\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}\},

which is nothing but taking tensor product with respect to the base ring
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}]
. Therefore we obtain the following:

Proposition 5.10
.

The isomorphism (
5.14
) induces a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded isomorphism between
\gl_{n}[z]
-modules:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]}\cong\underbrace{\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[A^{(N)}]}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[A^{(N)}]}\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)}_{k\text{ copies}}.

(5.15)

By the isomorphism (
5.15
) and Lemma
3.6
,
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]}
is a locally free module of finite rank on
\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
. We note that there is a natural surjective map
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}
whose composition with
m=1
case in (
5.12
) gives a surjective map

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]}\cong\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[A^{(N)}]}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[A^{(N)}]}\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k).

(5.16)

Since the above map is induced from the diagonal embedding
\mathcal{M}(N,n)\hookrightarrow\mathcal{M}(N,n)^{\times k}\subset\mathcal{M}(kN,kn)^{T}
, we see that it is nothing but the tensor multiplication map
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
.

Using the fermion presentation
\mathfrak{f}:\mathcal{F}_{kN}(kn)\cong\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)
in Section
4.6
, we can explicitly write down
\tau\circ\mathfrak{f}:\mathcal{F}_{kN}(kn)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
as follows. Under the fixed point decomposition
5.14
, the relevant component
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)
is identified with the
\mathfrak{h}
-invariant subspace
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)^{\mathfrak{h}}
, where
\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{sl}_{k}
is the Cartan subalgebra. Correspondingly
\mathcal{F}_{kN}(kn)^{\mathfrak{h}}\cong\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)
, where the isomorphism is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)\ni\bigotimes_{\alpha=1}^{k}\left({\psi}^{a^{(\alpha)}_{1},\alpha}_{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}}\cdots{\psi}^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N},\alpha}_{m^{(\alpha)}_{N}}\ket{\emptyset}\right)\mapstochar\rightarrow\prod_{\alpha=1}^{k}{\psi}^{a^{(\alpha)}_{1},\alpha}_{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}}\cdots{\psi}^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N},\alpha}_{m^{(\alpha)}_{N}}\ket{\emptyset}\in\mathcal{F}_{kN}(kn)^{\mathfrak{h}}.

Here
({\psi}^{a,\alpha}_{m})_{1\leq a\leq n,1\leq\alpha\leq k,m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}}
are the modes for
n\times k
fermion fields. Since the map
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is the multiplication map, we obtain the following.

Proposition 5.11
.

The composition of the maps
\mathcal{F}_{kN}(kn)\overset{\mathfrak{f}}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)\overset{\tau}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is given by

\tau\circ\mathfrak{f}\left(\prod_{\alpha=1}^{k}{\psi}^{a^{(\alpha)}_{1},\alpha}_{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}}\cdots{\psi}^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N_{\alpha}},\alpha}_{m^{(\alpha)}_{N_{\alpha}}}\ket{\emptyset}\right)\\
=\tau\left(\epsilon^{i^{(1)}_{1}\cdots i^{(k)}_{N_{k}}}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{k}\left[(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{1},\alpha}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}})_{i^{(\alpha)}_{1}}\cdots(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N_{\alpha}},\alpha}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{N_{\alpha}}})_{i^{(\alpha)}_{N_{\alpha}}}\right]\ket{\emptyset}\right)\\
=\begin{cases}\prod_{\alpha=1}^{k}\left[\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{1}}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}})_{i_{1}}\cdots(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N}}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{N}})_{i_{N}}\right]\ket{\emptyset}&,\text{ if }\forall\alpha,N_{\alpha}=N,\\
0&,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(5.17)

Corollary 5.12
.

\forall k\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
,
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}
is generated by global sections, i.e. the natural map between sheaves

\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}(N,n)}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}

is surjective.

Proof.

As we have seen in the above, the
k
-th tensor power map

\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det})^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})

is surjective, so it is enough to show that
\mathcal{L}_{\det}
is generated by global sections. Let
T=\mathbb{C}^{\times n}\subset\GL_{n}
be the maximal torus, then
\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}(N,n)}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\det}
is
T
-equivariant, so the support of its cokernel is
T
-invariant, therefore we need to show that fibers of
\mathcal{L}_{\det}
at every
T
-fixed point are generated by global sections. By (
4.24
)
\mathcal{M}(N,n)^{T}
is an affine variety, so the natural map

\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n)^{T},\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{\mathcal{M}(N,n)^{T}})\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{M}(N,n)^{T}}\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{\mathcal{M}(N,n)^{T}}

is surjective. By Proposition
4.5
, the restriction map
\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det})\rightarrow\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n)^{T},\mathcal{L}_{\det}|_{\mathcal{M}(N,n)^{T}})
is an isomorphism. This finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 5.13
.

The reduced level-rank map (
5.12
) in the case
m=n=1:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{kN}(k,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(1,k)

(5.18)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Consider the composition of surjective maps
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(k,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{kN}(k,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(1,k)
. It is enough to show that
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(k,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(1,k)
is an isomorphism. In fact,
\mathcal{H}_{N}(1,k)
(in particular
\mathcal{H}_{N}(1,1)
) is a rank one free module over
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}]
, then it follows that (
5.16
) is an isomorphism.
∎

Corollary 5.14
.

The reduced level-rank map (
5.12
) in the case
m=N=1:

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{k}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}(n,k)

(5.19)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

We notice that the natural map
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}
factors through

\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]\rtimes S_{k}}\cong\left(\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[A^{(N)}]}\cdots\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[A^{(N)}]}\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)\right)^{S_{k}}.

Take
N=1
and the right-hand-side of the above isomorphism becomes
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{1}]\otimes S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}
, where
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z]
acts on
S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}
via evaluation map
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z]\overset{z=0}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
. On the other hand,
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n,k)
is also isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{1}]\otimes S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}
as a
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{1}]\otimes U(\mathfrak{sl}_{n}[z])
-module. Thus the surjective map
\mathcal{H}_{k}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{h}[z]\rtimes S_{k}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}(n,k)
is an isomorphism, which implies that
\mathcal{H}_{k}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{1}(n,k)
is an isomorphism.
∎

In general, we do not know if the map
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is isomorphism or not. Nevertheless, we can show that it becomes an isomorphism after localization. Namely, let

\displaystyle\mathrm{Disc}=\prod_{i<j}(x_{i}-x_{j})^{2}\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}]

(5.20)

be the discriminant, then the nonvanishing locus of
\mathrm{Disc}
is
\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}
, i.e.
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}][\mathrm{Disc}^{-1}]=\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}]
.

Theorem 5.15
.

If
m=1
, then the reduced level-rank map (
5.12
) becomes an isomorphism after localizing
\mathrm{Disc}
, i.e. we have isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}[\mathrm{Disc}^{-1}]\cong\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)[\mathrm{Disc}^{-1}],

(5.21)

for all
k,n,N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is a free module over
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}]
, it suffices to show that the fiber of map
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
at
\vec{x}=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{N})\in\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
is an isomorphism whenever
\vec{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}
.

Denote the fibers of
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)
and
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}
at
{\vec{x}}
by
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\vec{x}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z],\vec{x}}
respectively. Applying
\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]
-coinvariant to the natural map
\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\vec{x}}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z],\vec{x}}
and we get:

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\vec{x}}\right]_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z],\vec{x}}.

It is enough to show that the surjective map
\left[\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\vec{x}}\right]_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{\vec{x}}
is an isomorphism.

Using the isomorphism (
4.10
) and Proposition
3.3
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\vec{x}}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\Gamma(\bGr^{k\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{kn},x_{i}},\mathcal{O}(1)),\quad\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{\vec{x}}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\Gamma(\bGr^{\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},x_{i}},\mathcal{O}(k)).

Here
\bGr^{k\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{kn},x_{i}}
and
\bGr^{\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},x_{i}}
the affine Schubert varieties of the affine Grassmannians supported at
x_{i}
. The action of
\mathfrak{sl}[z]
on
\Gamma(\bGr^{k\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{kn},x_{i}},\mathcal{O}(1))
factors through a Lie algebra quotient
\mathfrak{sl}[z]\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{sl}[z]/(z-x_{i})^{r}
for some
r
. We note that the Lie algebra map

\displaystyle\mathfrak{sl}[z]\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{N}\mathfrak{sl}[z]/(z-x_{i})^{r}

is surjective since
x_{i}
are distinct. It follows that

\displaystyle\left[\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\vec{x}}\right]_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}=\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}\Gamma(\bGr^{k\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{kn},x_{i}},\mathcal{O}(1))_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}.

(5.22)

We notice that
\Gamma(\bGr^{k\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{kn},x_{i}},\mathcal{O}(1))_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}
can be identified with
\left[\mathcal{H}_{k}(kn,1)_{x_{i}}\right]_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}
. By Corollary
5.14
, the coinvariant space
\left[\mathcal{H}_{k}(kn,1)_{x_{i}}\right]_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{H}_{1}(n,k)_{x_{i}}
, and the latter is isomorphic to
\Gamma(\bGr^{\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},x_{i}},\mathcal{O}(k))
. This implies that the map
\left[\mathcal{H}_{kN}(kn,1)_{\vec{x}}\right]_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{\vec{x}}
is an isomorphism. This finishes the proof.
∎

6
Wave Functions

Restricted to the open subset
\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}\subset\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
consisting of
N
disjoint points in
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, the fibers of Hilbert-Chow morphism
h:\mathcal{M}(N,n)\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
are isomorphic to
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N}
. Moreover, the restriction of
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}
to the fiber
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N}
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}(k)^{\boxtimes N}
. So for every
\vec{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}
, the fiber
h_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})|_{\vec{x}}
is isomorphic to
\Gamma(\mathbb{P}^{n-1},\mathcal{O}(k))^{\otimes N}\cong(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes N}
.

Denote by
\mathfrak{W}
the composition of the the restriction map

\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\rightarrow\Gamma\left(h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}})}\right)

and the base change map

\Gamma\left(h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}})}\right)\rightarrow\Gamma\left(h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}})}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}_{\mathrm{disj}}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}]

Since both maps are injective,
\mathfrak{W}
is a embedding

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}:\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}]\otimes(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes N}.

(6.1)

We call
\mathfrak{W}
the wave function presentation map, and for
\ket{v}\in\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
we call
\mathfrak{W}(\ket{v})
the wave function of
\ket{v}
.

Let us write down
\mathfrak{W}
explicitly. We diagonalize
X=H\mathrm{diag}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{N})H^{-1}
, then define
y^{a}_{i}=(AH)^{a}_{i}
. The coordinate on
\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}
is then
\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{N}\}
, and the
i
-th copy of
S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}
is represented by homogeneous polynomials in
\{y^{1}_{i},\cdots,y^{n}_{i}\}
of degree
k
. Every element in
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
can be presented as
f(X,A)\ket{\emptyset}
where
f(X,A)
is a
\GL_{N}
semi-invariant polynomial, the wave function
\mathfrak{W}(f(X,A)\ket{\emptyset})
is the function

\displaystyle f(\mathrm{diag}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{N}),y)\in\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}]\otimes(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes N}.

(6.2)

Example 6.1
.

If
k=1
, then we can write down the wave function of the image of the fermion Fock space presentation in Proposition
4.8
:

\begin{split}\mathfrak{W}\circ\mathfrak{f}({\psi}^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\cdots{\psi}^{a_{N}}_{m_{N}}\ket{\emptyset})&=\mathfrak{W}(\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}(A^{a_{1}}X^{m_{1}})_{i_{1}}\cdots(A^{a_{N}}X^{m_{N}})_{i_{N}}\ket{\emptyset})\\
&=\det(x_{i}^{m_{j}}y^{a_{j}}_{i})_{ij}.\end{split}

(6.3)

More generally, using the multiplication map
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,1)^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
which is surjective by Corollary
5.5
, one can always present the wave function by a product of functions of form
\det(x_{i}^{m_{j}}y^{a_{j}}_{i})_{ij}
, see
[
8
, 3.2]
.

6.1
Calogero representation of DDCA

According to
[
9
, Lemma A.12]
, the Calogero representation of the deformed double current algebra
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
is an algebra map from
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
to the twisted differential operator algebra
D^{k}((\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}})
. The latter naturally acts on
\Gamma((\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}},\mathcal{O}(k)^{\boxtimes N})\cong\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}]\otimes(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes N}
. Moreover, the construction of the map
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}\rightarrow D^{k}((\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}})
implies that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{W}:\Gamma(\mathcal{M}(N,n),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\rightarrow\Gamma((\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}},\mathcal{O}(k)^{\boxtimes N})

intertwines with the
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
-actions. Therefore we can present the
\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
-action on
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
using the Calogero representation
[
9
, (A.14)]
:

\displaystyle\mathsf{t}_{2,0}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Delta^{-1}\partial_{i}^{2}\Delta-2\sum_{i<j}^{N}\frac{\Omega_{ij}+k}{(x_{i}-x_{j})^{2}},\qquad\mathsf{T}_{0,n}(E^{a}_{b})\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{i=1}^{N}E^{a}_{b,i}x_{i}^{n}.

(6.4)

Here
E^{a}_{b,i}
is the
a
-th row
b
-th column elementary matrix
E^{a}_{b}\in\gl_{n}
, which acts on
i
-th
S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}
as
k
-th symmetric power of vector representation
\mathbb{C}^{n}
.
\Omega_{ij}=E^{a}_{b,i}E^{b}_{a,j}
is the quadratic Casimir of
ij
sites.
\Delta
is the Vandermonde factor

\displaystyle\Delta=\prod_{i>j}^{N}(x_{i}-x_{j}).

(6.5)

Remark 6.1
.

Using the Calogero representation (
6.4
), the Calogero-like Hamiltonian in
[
8
, (3.8)]
with
B=1
can be written as

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{H}}=-\mathsf{t}_{2,0}+2\mathsf{t}_{1,1}.

(6.6)

Since
\{\mathsf{t}_{2,0},\mathsf{t}_{1,1},\mathsf{t}_{0,2}\}
is an
\mathfrak{sl}_{2}
-triple, the Hamiltonian
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
is triangular on the
\mathsf{t}_{1,1}
-eigenvectors. This provides an alternative proof of
[
8
, (3.20),(3.21)]
.

Remark 6.2
.

As we have discussed in Section
5.2
, there is a Yangian subalgebra
Y(\gl_{n})\subset\mathsf{A}^{(n)}
such that
[Y(\gl_{n}),\mathsf{t}_{1,1}]=0
, so
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
admits a Yangian action which preserves the energy grading. Moreover, the Calogero Hamiltonian (
6.6
) can be written as
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}=e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{ad}_{\mathsf{t}_{2,0}}}(2\mathsf{t}_{1,1})
, so
\tilde{\mathcal{H}}
commutes with Yangian algebra
e^{-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{ad}_{\mathsf{t}_{2,0}}}(Y(\gl_{n}))
. This confirms a conjecture in
[
8
]
that the Calogero Hamiltonian (
6.6
) admits a Yangian symmetry.

Lemma 6.1
.

The Calogero representation of the operator
H_{\mathrm{CS}}:=\mathrm{Tr}((XY)^{2})
is a higher-spin analog of Calogero-Sutherland Hamiltonian
:

\displaystyle H_{\mathrm{CS}}=\sum_{i=1}^{N}\Delta^{-1}(x_{i}\partial_{i})^{2}\Delta-2\sum_{i<j}^{N}\frac{x_{i}x_{j}(\Omega_{ij}+k)}{(x_{i}-x_{j})^{2}}-(N-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\partial_{i}-\frac{N(N-1)(2N-1)}{6}.

(6.7)

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of
[
9
, Lemma A.1.2]
. We diagonalize
X=H\mathrm{diag}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{N})H^{-1}
. Define
\overline{XY}^{i}_{j}:=:(H^{-1}XYH)^{i}_{j}:
where the normal ordering means
H
and
X
are put at the right of
Y
(the ordering of
H
and
X
does not matter since they commute), that is
\overline{XY}^{i}_{j}=Y^{l}_{s}(H^{-1})^{i}_{t}X^{t}_{l}H^{s}_{j}
. Using
[
9
, (A.17)]
6
6

6

The variables
X
and
Y
in this paper are denoted by
Y
and
X
in
[
9
, Appendix A]
respectively.
we compute that

\displaystyle\overline{XY}^{i}_{j}=\begin{cases}\frac{x_{i}}{x_{j}-x_{i}}u^{a}_{j}v^{i}_{a}&,\text{ if }i\neq j,\\
x_{i}\partial_{i}&,\text{ if }i=j,\end{cases}\qquad\text{where }u^{a}_{i}=(AH)^{a}_{i},\;v^{j}_{a}=(H^{-1}B)^{j}_{a}.

(6.8)

On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\overline{XY}^{i}_{j}\overline{XY}^{j}_{i}=\mathrm{Tr}((XY)^{2})-(H^{-1})^{i}_{l}[(XY)^{l}_{m},H^{m}_{p}]\overline{XY}^{p}_{i}+(H^{-1})^{i}_{l}[(XY)^{l}_{q},H^{t}_{i}](XY)^{q}_{t}.

(6.9)

Using
[
9
, (A.21))]
, we have

\displaystyle[(XY)^{a}_{b},H^{c}_{d}]=\sum_{e\neq d}^{N}\frac{x_{d}}{x_{d}-x_{e}}H^{a}_{d}H^{c}_{e}(H^{-1})^{e}_{b}.

(6.10)

Plug (
6.10
) into (
6.9
), and we get

\displaystyle\overline{XY}^{i}_{j}\overline{XY}^{j}_{i}=\mathrm{Tr}((XY)^{2})-\sum_{p=1}^{N}\sum_{q\neq p}^{N}\frac{x_{p}+x_{q}}{x_{p}-x_{q}}\overline{XY}^{p}_{p}.

(6.11)

Plug (
6.8
) to the above equation, and we get

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}((XY)^{2})=\sum_{i=1}^{N}(x_{i}\partial_{i})^{2}-2\sum_{i<j}^{N}\frac{x_{i}x_{j}}{(x_{i}-x_{j})^{2}}u^{a}_{i}v^{j}_{a}u^{b}_{j}v^{i}_{b}-(N-1)\sum_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}\partial_{i}+2\sum_{i\neq j}\frac{x_{i}^{2}}{x_{i}-x_{j}}\partial_{i}.

(6.12)

According to
[
9
, Lemma A.1.1]
, the
i
-th
\gl_{n}
generators are defined by
E^{a}_{b,i}=u^{a}_{i}v^{i}_{b}
(
i
is not summed). It is straightforward to compute that
u^{a}_{i}v^{j}_{a}u^{b}_{j}v^{i}_{b}=\Omega_{ij}+k
, thus the right-hand-side of above equation is equal to (
6.7
).
∎

6.2
Ground states

Let
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k}:=s^{*}h_{*}(\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})
be the locally free sheaf on
\mathbb{A}^{N}
, where
s:\mathbb{A}^{N}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
is the symmetrization map. According to Proposition
3.3
,
\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k}\cong\tilde{\pi}_{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
. Here
\tilde{\pi}:\overline{\Gr}^{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{N}
is the structure map of Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian, and
\mathcal{O}(k)
is the
k
-th tensor power of determinant line bundle
\mathcal{O}(1)
on Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian.

Let
\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}
be the open subset in
\mathbb{A}^{N}
where
N
points are distinct, then
\tilde{\pi}:\overline{\Gr}^{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}
is a trivial
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N}
-fibration. Therefore we have a canonical isomorphism

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k}|_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}}\otimes(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n})^{\otimes N}.

(6.13)

Remark 6.3
.

The isomorphism (
6.13
) composed with the embedding
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)=\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})^{S_{N}}\hookrightarrow\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k}|_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}})
gives rise to the wave function map (
6.1
).

Since Hilbert-Chow map
h:\mathcal{M}(N,n)\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
is flat (Corollary
3.4
), by the flat base change theorem we have

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})\cong\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{N}].

(6.14)

The above isomorphism is
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant, where
\GL_{n}[z]
action on the left-hand-side is induced by its action on the Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian
\overline{\Gr}^{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
, and
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
action on the left-hand-side is induced by the scaling of
\mathbb{A}^{1}
with weight
-1
. Note that the permutation group
S_{N}
naturally acts on the second tensor component of
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}]}\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{N}]
, and
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
is the symmetric part, i.e.
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)=\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})^{S_{N}}
.

Although
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})
differs from
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
, the difference disappears when restricted to lowest
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-weight components. Namely, let
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}
be the
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-eigenspace of
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})
with smallest
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-weight, then we have the following.

Lemma 6.2
.

(
6.14
) induces an isomorphism
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}\cong\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
.

Proof.

This follows from the fact that
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{N}]
is non-negatively graded and its degree zero component is
\mathbb{C}
.
∎

Consider the full Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian
\Gr_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
. The connected components of
\Gr_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
are labelled by
N
-tuple of integers
(m_{1},\cdots,m_{N})\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
such that
\Gr^{(m_{1},\cdots,m_{N})}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
is characterized by its fiber over a point
\vec{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}
being isomorphic to
\prod_{i=1}^{N}\Gr^{(m_{i})}_{\GL_{n}}
. Here
\Gr^{(m_{i})}_{\GL_{n}}
is the
m_{i}
-th connected component of
\Gr_{\GL_{n}}
which is the moduli ind-scheme of lattices
\Lambda\subset\mathbb{C}(\!(z)\!)^{\oplus n}
such that

\displaystyle\dim(\mathbb{C}[\![z]\!]^{\oplus n}/z^{M}\Lambda)=m_{i}+Mn,\quad\forall M\gg 0.

\overline{\Gr}^{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
is contained in the connected component
\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
where
\mathbf{1}
is the vector in
\mathbb{Z}^{N}
whose components are
1
.

Let
\tilde{\pi}^{\mathbf{1}}:\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{N}
be the structure map of the Beilinson-Drinfeld Grassmannian, and we define the following sheaf on
\mathbb{A}^{N}
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}:=\tilde{\pi}^{\mathbf{1}}_{*}\mathcal{O}(k).

(6.15)

Since there is a natural
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-action on
\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
such that
\mathcal{O}(k)
is equivariant, and such action restricts to the
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-action on
\overline{\Gr}^{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
, we have the restriction map

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}\rightarrow\tilde{\pi}_{*}\mathcal{O}(k)=\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k},

(6.16)

which is
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant by construction. Applying the result in
[
52
, Ch.XVIII]
to each fiber of
\tilde{\pi}^{\mathbf{1}}
, we see that the map (
6.16
) is surjective. Moreover (
6.16
) induces isomorphism between ground states, namely we have the following.

Proposition 6.3
.

Let
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}
be the
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-eigenspace of
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})
with the smallest
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-weight, then (
6.16
) induces isomorphism

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}\cong\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}.

(6.17)

Lemma 6.4
.

There exists a set of finite rank locally free sheaves
\mathcal{V}_{m}
on
\mathbb{A}^{N}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) with surjective maps
\mathcal{V}_{m+1}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{m}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
m\end{subarray}}{\lim}\>\mathcal{V}_{m}.

Moreover, for any
\vec{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{N}
let
\mathcal{V}_{m,\vec{x}}
be the fiber of
\mathcal{V}_{m}
at
\vec{x}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\vec{x}),\mathcal{O}(k))\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
m\end{subarray}}{\lim}\>\mathcal{V}_{m,\vec{x}}.

Proof.

It is known that
\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}\cong\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\PGL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{N}}\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
as ind-schemes.
\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
is inductive limit of finite flat schemes over
\mathbb{A}^{N}
. In fact,

\displaystyle\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbb{C}^{\times},\mathbb{A}^{N}}\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\\
m\end{subarray}}{\lim}\Spec_{\mathbb{A}^{N}}\left(\bigoplus_{i=0}^{m}\mathrm{Sym}^{i}(\mathcal{F}_{m})\right),\quad\mathcal{F}_{m}=\Hom_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}}}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}(m\cdot D)/\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}}),

where
D
is the divisor on
\mathbb{A}^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}=\Spec\mathbb{C}[x_{1},\cdots,x_{N}]\times\Spec\mathbb{C}[z]
given by equation
\prod_{i=1}^{N}(z-x_{i})
. The inductive limit is induced by the projection
\mathcal{F}_{m+1}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{m}
. Since
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}(m\cdot D)/\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}}
is locally free of rank
m-1
on
\mathbb{A}^{N}
, we see that
\mathcal{F}_{m}
and
\bigoplus_{i=0}^{m}\mathrm{Sym}^{i}(\mathcal{F}_{m})
are locally free of finite rank on
\mathbb{A}^{N}
. By the flat base change theorem, to prove the lemma for
\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}=\tilde{\pi}_{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
, it suffices to prove the analogous statement for
\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}(k)
, i.e. there exists a set of finite rank locally free sheaves
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{m}
on
\mathbb{A}^{N}
(
m\in\mathbb{N}
) with surjective maps
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{m+1}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{m}
, such that

\displaystyle\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}_{*}\mathcal{O}(k)\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
m\end{subarray}}{\lim}\>\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{m},\quad\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}^{\prime-1}(\vec{x}),\mathcal{O}(k))\cong\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
m\end{subarray}}{\lim}\>\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{m,\vec{x}},\forall\vec{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{N}.

It is known that
\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\PGL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
is direct limit of Beilinson-Drinfeld Schubert varieties:

\displaystyle\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\PGL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\\
\lambda\end{subarray}}{\lim}\bGr^{\lambda,\cdots,\lambda}_{\PGL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}},

where
\lambda
runs through all dominant coweights of
\PGL_{n}
such that
\lambda-\omega_{1}
is in the root lattice of
\PGL_{n}
.
\bGr^{\lambda,\cdots,\lambda}_{\PGL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
is contained in
\bGr^{\lambda^{\prime},\cdots,\lambda^{\prime}}_{\PGL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
if and only if
\lambda\leq\lambda^{\prime}
in the Bruhat order. Let
\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda}
be the restriction of
\tilde{\pi}^{\prime}
to
\bGr^{\lambda,\cdots,\lambda}_{\PGL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
, then
\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda}
is proper and flat
[
24
, 1.2.4]
, thus the derived pushforward
R\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is a perfect complex with positive tor amplitudes in
D^{b}_{\mathrm{coh}}(\mathbb{A}^{N})
. We note that
\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda}^{-1}(0)\cong\bGr^{N\lambda}_{\PGL_{n}}
, and according to
[
52
, Ch.XVIII]
we have
H^{i}(\bGr^{N\lambda}_{\PGL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))=0
for all
i>0
. It follows that
R^{i}\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda*}\mathcal{O}(k)
vanishes in an open neighborhood
U
of
\{0\}\in\mathbb{A}^{N}
for all
i>0
. By the
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariance of
\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda}
,
U
is
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-closed, thus
U=\mathbb{A}^{N}
. This implies that
R^{i}\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda*}\mathcal{O}(k)=0
for all
i>0
and
\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is locally free of finite rank and its fiber at
\vec{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{N}
is canonically isomorphic to
\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda}^{-1}(\vec{x}),\mathcal{O}(k))
. Let us define
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{m}=\tilde{\pi}_{(mn+1)\omega_{1}*}\mathcal{O}(k)
.

Finally, it remains to show that the transition map
\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda^{\prime}*}\mathcal{O}(k)\rightarrow\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda*}\mathcal{O}(k)
is surjective whenever
\lambda^{\prime}\geq\lambda
in Bruhat order. By the
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariance of the map
\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda^{\prime}*}\mathcal{O}(k)\rightarrow\tilde{\pi}_{\lambda*}\mathcal{O}(k)
, it suffices to show that the map between fibers at zero is surjective, i.e.
\Gamma(\bGr^{N\lambda^{\prime}}_{\PGL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))\rightarrow\Gamma(\bGr^{N\lambda}_{\PGL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))
is surjective. The surjectivity for the latter is proven in
[
52
, Ch.XVIII]
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.3
.

Since
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})\rightarrow\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})
is surjective, it induces surjective map between ground states, i.e.
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}
. Thus it suffices to show that
\dim\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}=\dim\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}
.

By the lemma
6.4
and by the
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-localization on
\mathbb{A}^{N}
, the
q
-character of
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})
is

\displaystyle\mathrm{ch}_{q}(\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}))=\frac{1}{(1-q)^{N}}\mathrm{ch}_{q}(\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(0),\mathcal{O}(k))).

We note that
\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(0)\cong\Gr^{(N)}_{\GL_{n}}
. According to
[
50
, Theorem 2.5.5]
,
\Gamma(\Gr^{(N)}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))
is dual to
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kN}
, where
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})
is the level
k
integrable representation with highest weight
k\varpi_{n-r}
of
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)
and
\mathrm{Fock}_{kN}
is the Fock module of
\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}
of weight
kN
. Then we have

\displaystyle\dim\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}=\dim\Gamma(\Gr^{(N)}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))_{0}=\dim L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})^{*}_{0}=\dim L_{k\varpi_{r}}({\mathfrak{sl}}_{n})=\dim\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{0}.

According to Lemma
6.2
, we have
\dim\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}=\dim\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}
. This proves the proposition.
∎

6.2.1
Conformal blocks

Fix
\ell
distinct points
y_{1},\cdots,y_{\ell}
in
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, and attach each point
y_{i}
a positive integer
d_{i}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}d_{i}=N
. Then we can regard
\mathbf{y}=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}d_{i}y_{i}
as an element in
\mathbb{A}^{N}
. The fiber of
\tilde{\pi}
at
\mathbf{y}
is isomorphic to
\prod_{i=1}^{\ell}\Gr^{(d_{i})}_{\GL_{n}}
, and the restriction of
\mathcal{O}(k)
to
\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\mathbf{y})
is the tensor product of
\mathcal{O}(k)
on each component
\Gr^{(d_{i})}_{\GL_{n}}
. Applying Proposition
5.3
to
\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\mathbf{y})
and we obtain the following.

Proposition 6.5
.

There is a vector space isomorphism
\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}),\mathcal{O}(k))^{*}\cong\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\ell}M_{i}
, where
M_{i}\cong L_{k\varpi_{n-r_{i}}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{-kd_{i}}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
,
r_{i}=d_{i}-\lfloor\frac{d_{i}}{n}\rfloor n
. The action of
\gl_{n}[z]
on
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\ell}M_{i}
is via the Lie algebra map
\gl_{n}[z]\rightarrow\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}\widehat{\gl}(n)_{k}
, where the
i
-th
\widehat{\gl}(n)_{k}
is the central extension

\displaystyle 0\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\widehat{\gl}(n)_{k}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\gl_{n}(\!(z-y_{i})\!)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow 0.

Consider the vertex algebra
V_{k}(\gl_{n})
associated to affine Lie algebra
\widehat{\gl}(n)_{k}
. We interpret the vector space
M_{i}
as a
V_{k}(\gl_{n})
-module inserted at
y_{i}\in\mathbb{A}^{1}\subset\mathbb{P}^{1}
. In
[
38
, 13.1.7]
, the space of
modified conformal blocks
is defined as the Lie algebra invariant

\displaystyle C^{0}_{V_{k}(\gl_{n})}(\mathbb{P}^{1},(y_{i}),(M_{i}))_{i=1}^{\ell}:=\left(\bigotimes_{i=1}^{\ell}M_{i}^{*}\right)^{\mathfrak{g}^{0}_{\vec{y}}},

(6.18)

where
\mathfrak{g}^{0}_{\vec{y}}
is the Lie subalgebra of
\gl_{n}[z,\frac{1}{z-y_{i}}]_{1\leq i\leq\ell}
consisting of those elements
f(z)
such that
f(\infty)=0
. By Proposition
6.5
, the space of modified conformal blocks
C^{0}_{V_{k}(\gl_{n})}(\mathbb{P}^{1},(y_{i}),(M_{i}))_{i=1}^{\ell}
is naturally isomorphic to
\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}),\mathcal{O}(k))^{\mathfrak{g}^{0}_{\vec{y}}}
.

Theorem 6.6
.

For any element
\phi\in\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}
, its restriction to the fiber at
\mathbf{y}
, denoted by
\phi_{\mathbf{y}}\in\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}),\mathcal{O}(k))
, belongs to the subspace of modified conformal blocks. I.e.
\phi_{\mathbf{y}}
is
\mathfrak{g}^{0}_{\vec{y}}
invariant.

Proof.

Define the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
to be the Lie subalgebra of
\gl_{n}[z,\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}]_{1\leq i\leq N}
consisting of those elements
f(z)
such that
f(\infty)=0
. We note that
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
acts on
\gl_{n}[z,\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}]_{1\leq i\leq N}
by assigning weight
1
to
z
and
-1
to
\frac{1}{z-x_{i}}
, so
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
is negatively weighted with respect to the
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-action.
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
naturally acts on
\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
, and such action lifts to an action of
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
on
\mathcal{O}(k)
, which endows
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})
a
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
-module structure. We note that
\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}),\mathcal{O}(k))
is quotient
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
-module of
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})
, and
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
acts on
\Gamma(\tilde{\pi}^{-1}(\mathbf{y}),\mathcal{O}(k))
via the evaluation map
\mathfrak{g}^{0}\twoheadrightarrow\mathfrak{g}^{0}_{\vec{y}}

\displaystyle x_{i}\mapstochar\rightarrow y_{j},\text{ for }\sum_{u=1}^{j-1}d_{u}<i\leq\sum_{v=1}^{j}d_{v}.

Therefore, the theorem will automatically follow from the following statement:

\displaystyle\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}\subset\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})^{\mathfrak{g}^{0}}.

This is true because the
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
-action on
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})
is
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant, and
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
is negatively graded, so
\mathfrak{g}^{0}
takes
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}
to lower
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-weight components which must be zero.
∎

6.2.2
Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov equation

It is known that the Beilinsen-Drinfeld Grassmannians
\{\Gr_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}\}_{N\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}}
forms a factorization space over the Ran space of
\mathbb{A}^{1}

[
38
, 20.3.5]
, and
\mathcal{O}(1)
is a factorizable line bundle
[
38
, 20.4.1]
. Then it follows from the theory of factorization space that
\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}=\tilde{\pi}_{*}^{\mathbf{1}}\mathcal{O}(k)
naturally inherits a
D
-module structure from the factorzation structure
[
38
, 20.4.1]
, i.e. it is equipped with a flat connection
\nabla:\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}\rightarrow\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}}
.

Proposition 6.7
.

Any ground state of
\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})
is a flat section of
\nabla
, i.e.

\displaystyle\nabla(\phi)=0,\;\forall\phi\in\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}.

Proof.

\nabla
is
\mathbb{C}_{q}^{\times}
-equivariant by construction. Let
\nabla_{i}\in\End(\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})
be the action of
i
-th tangent vector
\partial_{i}
on
\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}
, then
\nabla_{i}
decrease the energy grading by one, hence it annihilates the ground states.
∎

When restricted to the open subset
\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}\subset\mathbb{A}^{N}
, we can give a trivialization of
\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}
using the Proposition
6.5
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}|_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}}\otimes\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}^{*},\quad M_{i}=L_{k\varpi_{n-1}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{-k}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}).

(6.19)

According to
[
38
, 13.3.3]
, for any point
\vec{x}=(x_{1},\cdots,x_{N})\in\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}
the space of modified conformal blocks
C^{0}_{V_{k}(\gl_{n})}(\mathbb{P}^{1},(x_{i}),(M_{i}))_{i=1}^{N}
is isomorphic to
\left(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}\right)^{\otimes N}
. Moreover, we can vary the point
\vec{x}
and the space of modified conformal blocks forms a sub-bundle

\displaystyle C^{0}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x},\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}\right)\subset\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}|_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}},

whose fiber at a point
\vec{x}
is
C^{0}_{V_{k}(\gl_{n})}(\mathbb{P}^{1},(x_{i}),(M_{i}))_{i=1}^{N}
. According to the construction in
[
38
, 13.3.3]
the isomorphism
C^{0}_{V_{k}(\gl_{n})}(\mathbb{P}^{1},(x_{i}),(M_{i}))_{i=1}^{N}\cong\left(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}\right)^{\otimes N}
is induced by restricting a linear function
\tau\in\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}^{*}
to the ground states of
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}
, and the latter is dual to
\left(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}\right)^{\otimes N}
. We can translate the above construction into the geometric language: the dual of ground states of
\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}
is naturally identified with
\Gamma((\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N},\mathcal{O}(k)^{\boxtimes N})
where
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{N}
is the fiber of
\bGr^{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},\mathbb{A}^{N}}
at
\vec{x}
, and the restriction-to-ground-states map is exactly the natural quotient map
\Gamma(\Gr^{\mathbf{1}}_{\GL_{n},\vec{x}},\mathcal{O}(k))\twoheadrightarrow\Gamma(\bGr^{\omega_{1},\cdots,\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n},\vec{x}},\mathcal{O}(k))
. Varying
\vec{x}\in\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}
and we obtain the following.

Proposition 6.8
.

The projection
\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}\twoheadrightarrow\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k}
induces an isomorphism between vector bundles

\displaystyle C^{0}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x},\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}\right)\cong\mathcal{E}_{N}^{n,k}|_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}}

(6.20)

A key property of the sub-bundle
C^{0}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x},\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}\right)\subset\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k}|_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}}
is that it is a sub D-module
[
38
, 13.3.7.(2)]
, i.e. the connection
\nabla
preserves
C^{0}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x},\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}\right)
. The restriction of
\nabla
to
C^{0}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x},\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}\right)
is known as the Knizhnik-Zamolodchikov connection
[
57
]
. Using the isomorphism (
6.20
) and the trivialization (
6.13
), we obtain trivialization
C^{0}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x},\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}\right)\cong\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}}\otimes\left(S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}\right)^{\otimes N}
. The coordinate form of connection
\nabla
on
C^{0}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x},\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}\right)
in this trivialization is the following:

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}=\partial_{i}-\frac{1}{k+n}\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{\Omega^{\mathfrak{sl}_{n}}_{ij}}{x_{i}-x_{j}}-\frac{1}{kn}\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{k^{2}}{x_{i}-x_{j}},

(6.21)

where the first summation term corresponds to the module
L_{k\varpi_{n-1}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})
which is worked out in
[
38
, 13.3.8]
and the second summation term corresponds to the module
\mathrm{Fock}_{-k}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
which is worked out in
[
38
, 13.2.6]
. Here
\Omega^{\mathfrak{sl}_{n}}_{ij}
is the quadratic Casimir for
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
.
\Omega^{\mathfrak{sl}_{n}}_{ij}
equals to
\Omega_{ij}-\frac{k^{2}}{n}\mathrm{Id}
when acting on
S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}\otimes S^{k}\mathbb{C}^{n}
, so we can rewrite (
6.21
) as

\displaystyle\nabla_{i}=\partial_{i}-\frac{1}{k+n}\sum_{j\neq i}\frac{\Omega_{ij}+k}{x_{i}-x_{j}}.

(6.22)

By Theorem
6.6
the restriction of any ground state
\phi\in\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{N},\mathcal{V}_{N}^{n,k})_{0}
to the open locus
\mathbb{A}^{N}_{\mathrm{disj}}
is a section of the sub-bundle
C^{0}_{k}\left(\mathbf{x},\bigotimes_{i=1}^{N}M_{i}\right)
, and by the Proposition
6.7
the coordinate form of
\phi
satisfies the KZ equation
\nabla(\phi)=0
where
\nabla
is the KZ connection in (
6.22
). Combine Remark
6.3
, Lemma
6.2
, and Proposition
6.3
, we see that the wave functions of ground states in
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
satisfy the KZ equation. We summarize the result as follows.

Corollary 6.9
.

Ground states wave functions solve the KZ equation, i.e.

\displaystyle\nabla(\phi)=0,\;\forall\phi\in\mathfrak{W}(\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{0}),

where
\nabla
is the KZ connection (
6.22
).

The result that the Chern-Simons matrix model ground states wave functions satisfy KZ equation is not new, see
[
6
, 5.1]
for a derivation in the case when
N
is divisible by
n
and
[
8
, Appendix A.2]
for a proof in general. The previous proofs are computational and involve careful analysis on explicit formulae of the ground states. The new ingredient here is that we provide a geometric proof which essentially boils down to the simple fact that ground states have the lowest
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
weights among all physical states. Our method does not require explicit formulae of the ground states.

7
Conformal Limit, Part I: Transition Maps

In this section, we define and study the conformal limit (
N\rightarrow\infty
) of the Hilbert spaces of the matrix model.

To begin with, we introduce a natural transition map
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
. We will define this map in two ways: geometrically and algebraically, which turn out to be equivalent construction. The outcome of the constructions is a
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-equivariant map
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\otimes\chi_{N}^{\otimes k}
where
\chi_{N}
is certain character of
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
which plays the role of retaining equivariance. And we shall define the conformal limit Hilbert space as
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-finite subspace of the inverse limit of
\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
equipped with twisted
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-actions.

7.1
Geometric construction of the transition map

Consider the closed embedding

\displaystyle\iota_{N}:\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})\hookrightarrow\Quot^{N+n}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})

(7.1)

which maps a subsheaf
\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
of codimension
N
to the subsheaf
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(-[0])\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
which has codimension
N+n
. Here
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}(-[0])
is the ideal sheaf corresponding to the origin
0\in\mathbb{A}^{1}
.

Lemma 7.1
.

The pullback of the determinant line bundle
\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}
is
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-equivariantly isomorphic to
\mathcal{L}_{\det}\otimes\chi_{N}
, where
\chi_{N}
is the equivariant line bundle which is the pullback of the character

\displaystyle\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times},\quad(g[z],t)\mapstochar\rightarrow t^{N}\cdot\det g[0],

(7.2)

along the projection from
\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})
to a point.

Proof.

Consider the line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{-1}\otimes\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}
