, we claim that it is (non-equivariantly) isomorphic to the structure sheaf of
\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})
. Let
D\subset\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
be the prime divisor given by the equation
\prod_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}=0

7
7

7

The equation
\prod_{i=1}^{N}x_{i}=0
on
\mathbb{A}^{N}
cuts out
N
hyperplanes, and the symmetric group acts on
N
irreducible components transitively, therefore its image in
\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
is irreducible, i.e.
D
is a prime divisor.
. The preimage of
\mathbb{A}^{(N)}\setminus D
along the Hilbert-Chow map
h:\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{(N)}
is the moduli of codimensional
N
subsheaves
\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
such that
\{0\}
is not in the support of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}/\mathcal{E}
. Therefore the fiber of
\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}
at a point
(\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})
in
h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}\setminus D)
is
\det(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}/\mathcal{E})\otimes\det(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}|_{\{0\}})
. It follows that
\left(\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{-1}\otimes\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}\right)|_{h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}\setminus D)}
is isomorphic to the structure sheaf tensoring the line
\det(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}|_{\{0\}})
which is non-equivariantly isomorphic to the structure sheaf, thus
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{-1}\otimes\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}
is isomorphic to a divisor supported on
h^{-1}(D)
. Since
h
is flat by Corollary
3.4
,
h^{-1}(D)
is a divisor. Note that the restriction of
h
to
h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}\setminus\Delta)
is a locally trivial fibration with fibers isomorphic to
(\mathbb{P}^{n-1})^{\times N}
, where
\Delta
is the diagonal divisor, therefore
h^{-1}(D)
is prime because the intersection
D\cap(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}\setminus\Delta)
is nonempty. As a result
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{-1}\otimes\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}\cong\mathcal{O}_{\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})}(m\cdot h^{-1}(D))
for some
m
. Since
\mathcal{O}_{\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})}(h^{-1}(D))\cong h^{*}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{(N)}}(D)
and
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{(N)}}(D)
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{(N)}}
, this proves our claim.

Since
\Gamma(\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}),\mathcal{O}_{\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})})^{\times}=\Gamma(\mathbb{A}^{(N)},\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{(N)}})^{\times}=\mathbb{C}^{\times}
, the equivariant structure of the trivial line bundle
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{-1}\otimes\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}
must be pullback of a character
\chi:\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
along the projection from
\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n})
to a point. It remains to find
\chi
. To this end, we only need to find the action of
\chi
on
\GL_{n}[z]
and on
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
separately. For
\GL_{n}[z]
, we notice that
\left(\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{-1}\otimes\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}\right)|_{h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}\setminus D)}
is
\GL_{n}[z]
-equivariantly isomorphic to
\mathcal{O}_{h^{-1}(\mathbb{A}^{(N)}\setminus D)}\otimes\det(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}|_{\{0\}})
, thus
\chi:\GL_{n}[z]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is the determinant representation
\chi(g[z])=\det g[0]
. For
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
, we look at the fiber of
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{-1}\otimes\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}
at a
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-fixed point, say
\oplus_{i=1}^{n}z^{\lambda_{i}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus_{n}}
(
\sum_{i=1}^{n}\lambda_{i}=N
), then the fiber of
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{-1}\otimes\iota_{N}^{*}\mathcal{L}_{\det}
at this point is
\otimes_{i=1}^{n}z^{\lambda_{i}}\mathcal{O}_{\{0\}}
which has
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-weight
N
. Thus
\chi:\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}^{\times}
is given by the
N
-th power map. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 7.2
.

For any positive integer
k
, the induced map on global sections

\iota_{N}^{*}:\Gamma(\Quot^{N+n}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\rightarrow\Gamma(\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\otimes\chi_{N}^{\otimes k}

is surjective.

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of the surjectivity statement in Proposition
5.9
. By Lemma
7.1

\iota_{N}^{*}
is a
\gl_{n}[z]
-module map, and by Corollary
5.4
both
\Gamma(\Quot^{N+n}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})
and
\Gamma(\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\otimes\chi^{\otimes k}
are generated from a ground state by the action of
\gl_{n}[z]
, thus we only need to show that
\iota_{N}^{*}(v)\neq 0
for some ground state
v
. By Remark
4.1
, the restriction
\Gamma(\Quot^{N+n}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\rightarrow\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{\{z^{\lambda}\}}
maps the
\gl_{n}
highest weight vector of
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)_{0}
isomorphically onto
\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k}|_{\{z^{\lambda}\}}
. Here
\lambda_{i}=\lfloor\frac{N+n-i}{n}\rfloor,1\leq i\leq n
, and
\{z^{\lambda}\}
corresponds to the subsheaf
\oplus_{i=1}^{n}z^{\lambda_{i}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
. Thus the image of the
\gl_{n}
highest weight vector of
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)_{0}
is nonzero in
\Gamma(\Quot^{N}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}),\mathcal{L}_{\det}^{\otimes k})\otimes\chi_{N}^{\otimes k}
. This finishes the proof.

∎

The transition map
\iota_{N}^{*}:\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)\rightarrow\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)\otimes\chi_{N}^{\otimes k}
is compatible with the map (
5.10
). Namely, we have the following commutative diagram of embeddings of moduli spaces:

(7.3)

where the vertical arrows maps a subsheaf
\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}
to
\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus k}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus n}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus k}=\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}}^{\oplus kn}
. Note that

\displaystyle j_{N}^{*}\chi_{kN}=\chi_{N}^{\otimes k}.

Therefore we obtain a commutative diagram of maps between global sections of line bundles:

(7.4)

and all the maps in the above diagram are
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-equivariant.

7.2
Algebraic construction of the transition map

Before we introduce the algebraic construction of transition map which looks unnatural from the first glance, it is instructive to explain why a innocent-looking construction does not work.

Let us choose a splitting
\mathbb{C}^{N+n}=\mathbb{C}^{N}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{n}
which induces an embedding
V(N,n)\hookrightarrow V(N+n,n)
where
V(N,n)
is defined in the beginning of Section
3
. The restriction-to-subspace maps polynomial function ring
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]
surjectively onto
\mathbb{C}[V(N,n)]
. Let us take the semi-invariant subspace
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]^{\GL_{N+n},-k}
, which is by definition our Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)
, and we claim that its image in
\mathbb{C}[V(N,n)]
is zero. In fact, the restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[V(N,n)]
is
\GL_{N}\times\GL_{n}
equivariant, where
\GL_{N}\times\GL_{n}
action on
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]
is induced from diagonal embedding
\GL_{N}\times\GL_{n}\hookrightarrow\GL_{N+n}
and
\GL_{N}\times\GL_{n}
action on
\mathbb{C}[V(N,n)]
is induced from projection to the first component. Since
\GL_{n}
acts on
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]^{\GL_{N+n},-k}
via the character
g\mapstochar\rightarrow\det(g)^{-k}
, the image of
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]^{\GL_{N+n},-k}
in
\mathbb{C}[V(N,n)]
must be zero. So the naive restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[V(N,n)]
does not work.

To fix the issue, we consider the following vector space

\displaystyle\widetilde{V}(N,n):=\End(\mathbb{C}^{N})\oplus\Hom(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C}^{N+n}),

(7.5)

which embeds into
V(N+n,n)=\End(\mathbb{C}^{N+n})\oplus\Hom(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C}^{N+n})
by splitting
\mathbb{C}^{N+n}=\mathbb{C}^{N}\oplus\mathbb{C}^{n}
. The notation for linear coordinates on
\widetilde{V}(N,n)
is that

\displaystyle X\in\End(\mathbb{C}^{N}),\quad A\in\Hom(\mathbb{C}^{N},\mathbb{C}^{n}),\quad\eta\in\Hom(\mathbb{C}^{n},\mathbb{C}^{n}),

(7.6)

with components
(X^{i}_{j})_{1\leq i,j,\leq N}
,
(A^{a}_{i})_{1\leq a\leq n,1\leq i\leq N}
,
(\eta^{a}_{\ell})_{1\leq a\leq n,N+1\leq\ell\leq N+n}
.

Let us define a
\gl_{n}[z]
-action on
\widetilde{V}(N,n)
by

\displaystyle E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m}\mapstochar\rightarrow A^{a}_{i}(X^{m})^{i}_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial A^{b}_{j}}+\delta_{m=0}\eta^{a}_{\ell}\frac{\partial}{\partial\eta^{b}_{\ell}},

(7.7)

which exponentiates to a
\GL_{n}[z]
-action. We define the
\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-grading on
\widetilde{V}(N,n)
by
\deg(X)=1
,
\deg(A)=\deg(\eta)=0

Lemma 7.3
.

The restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
is
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-equivariant, where the
\GL_{n}[z]
-action on
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]
is the standard one (
5.1
) and the
\GL_{n}[z]
-action on
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
is given by (
7.7
).

Proof.

Straightforward computation.
∎

Lemma 7.4
.

The image of
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)=\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]^{\GL_{N+n},-k}
under the restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
is

\displaystyle\det(X)^{k}\cdot\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k},

(7.8)

where
\GL_{N}
acts on
X
by adjoint action and acts on
A
by dual fundamental representation.

Proof.

Let us denote
G_{1}
(resp.
G_{2}
) to be the
\GL_{N}
(resp.
\GL_{n}
) factor inside the diagonal embedding
\GL_{N}\times\GL_{n}\subset\GL_{N+n}
. Then for
i=1,2
,
G_{i}
acts on the image of
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]^{\GL_{N+n},-k}
under the restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
by the character
g\mapstochar\rightarrow\det(g)^{-k}
. The subspace of
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
on which
G_{2}
acts by the character
g\mapstochar\rightarrow\det(g)^{-k}
is
\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\mathbb{C}[X,A]
. The subspace of
\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\mathbb{C}[X,A]
on which
G_{1}
acts by the character
g\mapstochar\rightarrow\det(g)^{-k}
is
\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
. Thus the image lies in the subspace
\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
. Since
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
is
\gl_{n}[z]
-equivariant and both
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]^{\GL_{N+n},-k}
and
\det(X)^{k}\cdot\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
are generated from a ground state by
\gl_{n}[z]
-action (Corollary
5.4
), it is enough to show that there exists a ground state in
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]^{\GL_{N+n},-k}
whose image in
\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
is of the form
\det(X)^{k}\cdot\det(\eta)^{k}
times a ground state in
\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
. Using the explicit presentation (
4.18
), we can check that

\displaystyle\ket{1,\cdots,n,a_{1},\cdots,a_{N}}\mapstochar\rightarrow\det(X)^{k}\cdot\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\ket{a_{1},\cdots,a_{N}}.

(7.9)

This finishes the proof.
∎

We note that the
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-action on
\det(X)^{k}\cdot\det(\eta)^{k}
is exactly given by the character
\chi_{N}
, which agrees with the geometric construction. We claim that the map
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)\twoheadrightarrow\det(X)^{k}\cdot\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
constructed in Lemma
7.4
agrees with
\iota_{N}^{*}:\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)\twoheadrightarrow\chi_{N}^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
in the previous subsection, upon a choice of isomorphism
\det(X)\cdot\det(\eta)\cong\chi_{N}
. In fact, both
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)
and
\chi_{N}^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
are generated by ground states, so any
\GL_{n}[z]\rtimes\mathbb{C}^{\times}_{q}
-equivariant map between them is uniquely determined by the
\gl_{n}
-equivariant map between the ground state
\mathcal{H}_{N+n}(n,k)_{0}
and
\chi_{N}^{\otimes k}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
. Since the ground states are irreducible
\gl_{n}
-modules by Remark
4.1
, any two such maps are differed by a scalar multiple according to Schur’s lemma. The ambiguity of choosing a scalar multiple is fixed by choosing an isomorphism
\det(X)\cdot\det(\eta)\cong\chi_{N}
.

Definition 7.1
.

We define the twisted Hilbert space
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
to be the Hermitian vector space
{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
together with
shifted energy grading
given by the eigenvalues of

\displaystyle X^{i}_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial X^{i}_{j}}-\frac{k}{2}L(L-1)n-krL,\;\text{where }L=\lfloor\frac{N}{n}\rfloor,\;r=N-Ln.

(7.10)

We equip
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
with the following graded
\gl_{n}[z]
-action:

\displaystyle E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m}\mapstochar\rightarrow A^{a}_{i}(X^{m})^{i}_{j}\frac{\partial}{\partial A^{b}_{j}}-\delta_{m=0}\delta^{a}_{b}kL.

(7.11)

We define the transition map
p_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
to be
\det(X)^{-k}\cdot\det(\eta)^{-k}
multiplying with the restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]^{\GL_{N+n},-k}\rightarrow\det(X)^{k}\cdot\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
in the Lemma
7.4
. We define a section
\sigma_{N}
of the projection
p_{N}
to be the composition

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ker(p_{N})^{\perp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(p_{N}|_{\ker(p_{N})^{\perp}}\right)^{-1}}

(7.12)

Here we have used the fact that
p_{N}|_{\ker(p_{N})^{\perp}}
is isomorphism.

Remark 7.1
.

The ground states
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
has degree
0
with respect to the energy grading (
7.10
). Moreover,
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
is an irreducible
\gl_{n}
- module of highest weight
k\varpi_{r}
with respect to the action via taking
m=0
in (
7.11
).

We shall write the energy grading decomposition as

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d},

(7.13)

where
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}
is the eigenspace of the operator (
7.10
) with eigenvalue
d
.

Proposition 7.5
.

The projection
p_{N}
and the section
\sigma_{N}
preserve the energy grading on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
. Moreover,
p_{N}
is
\gl_{n}[z]
-equivariant.

Proof.

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
is isomorphic to
\det(A)^{-kL}\cdot\mathcal{H}_{N}(n,k)
as
\gl_{n}[z]
-module, therefore
p_{N}
is
\gl_{n}[z]
-equivariant by Lemma
7.3
. Since
p_{N}
maps
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k)_{0}
to
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{0}
, and both
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k)
and
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
are generated from ground states by
\gl_{n}[z]
-actions which respects the energy grading, thus
\gl_{n}[z]
-equivariance of
p_{N}
implies that
p_{N}
respects the energy grading. The Hermitian inner product on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
respects the grading, i.e.

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}\ket{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d^{\prime}}}=0,\;\text{whenever }d\neq d^{\prime}.

Therefore
\ker(p_{N})^{\perp}
is a graded subspace, i.e.
\ker(p_{N})^{\perp}=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\ker(p_{N})^{\perp}\cap\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}
, whence
\sigma_{N}
respects the energy grading.
∎

As we have discussed in the previous subsection, the transition map
p_{N}
is compatible with the map
\tau_{N}
defined in (
5.11
), i.e. we have a commutative diagram:

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

(7.14)

This can also be directly checked using the explicit formula of
p_{N}
given below.

7.2.1
Explicit formula of
p_{N}

By Remark
5.1
, every element in
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k)
is a linear combination of following elements

\displaystyle\ket{(a^{(\alpha)}_{j}),(m^{(\alpha)}_{j})}=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{k}\left[\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N+n}}(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{1}}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}})_{i_{1}}\cdots(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N+n}}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{N+n}})_{i_{N+n}}\right]\ket{\emptyset}.

(7.15)

Without loss of generality, let us assume that
\forall\alpha
,
m^{(\alpha)}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq m^{(\alpha)}_{N+n}
. Then we have

\displaystyle p_{N}\left(\ket{(a^{(\alpha)}_{j}),(m^{(\alpha)}_{j})}\right)=\delta\times\prod_{\alpha=1}^{k}\left[\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{1}}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{1}-1})_{i_{1}}\cdots(A^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N}}X^{m^{(\alpha)}_{N}-1})_{i_{N}}\right]\ket{\emptyset},

(7.16)

where the factor
\delta
is given by

\displaystyle\delta=\prod_{\alpha=1}^{k}\left[\epsilon^{a^{(\alpha)}_{N+1}\cdots a^{(\alpha)}_{N+n}}_{12\cdots n}\prod_{\ell=N+1}^{N+n}\delta_{m^{(\alpha)}_{\ell}=0}\right].

(7.17)

We note that if
\delta\neq 0
then it automatically follows that
m^{(\alpha)}_{j}\geq 1
for all
\alpha
and all
j\leq N
, so that (
7.16
) makes sense.

7.2.2
Fermion Fock space construction in the case of
k=1

When
k=1
, we can similarly define a shifted version of the fermion Fock space
\mathcal{F}_{N}(n)
in Section
4.6
. Namely, let
L=\lfloor\frac{N}{n}\rfloor
,
r=N-Ln
, and define
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)
to be the wedge space

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)=\bigwedge^{N}[\psi^{a}_{m}\>|\>a\in\{1,\cdots,n\},m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq-L}].

(7.18)

The energy grading on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)
is such that

\displaystyle\deg(\psi^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\psi^{a_{N}}_{m_{N}})=\frac{nL(L+1)}{2}+\sum_{i=1}^{N}m_{i}.

(7.19)

The space of ground states
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)_{0}
has degree
0
, and we write the energy grading decomposition

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)_{d}.

(7.20)

We equip
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)
with the following graded
\gl_{n}[z]
-action:

\displaystyle E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{\ell\geq\max\{-L,-m\}}\psi^{a}_{\ell+m}\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{b}_{\ell}}-\sum_{-L\leq\ell<-m}\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{b}_{\ell}}\psi^{a}_{\ell+m}.

(7.21)

Then the linear map
\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,1)
which is given by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}_{N}(\psi^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\psi^{a_{N}}_{m_{N}})=\epsilon^{i_{1}i_{2}\cdots i_{N}}(A^{a_{1}}X^{m_{1}+L})_{i_{1}}\cdots(A^{a_{N}}X^{m_{N}+L})_{i_{N}}\ket{\emptyset}

(7.22)

is a graded
\gl_{n}[z]
-module isomorphism.

Define
p^{\mathfrak{f}}_{N}:=\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}^{-1}_{N}\circ p_{N}\circ\widetilde{\mathfrak{f}}_{N+n}:\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N+n}(n)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)
to be the transition map for the shifted fermion Fock spaces. Explicitly, let
\psi^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\psi^{a_{N+n}}_{m_{N+n}}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N+n}(n)
be an element, and we assume that
m_{1}\geq\cdots\geq m_{N+n}
without loss of generality, then we have

\displaystyle p^{\mathfrak{f}}_{N}(\psi^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\wedge\cdots\wedge\psi^{a_{N+n}}_{m_{N+n}})=\delta^{\prime}\times\psi^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\cdots\psi^{a_{N}}_{m_{N}},

(7.23)

where the factor
\delta^{\prime}
is given by

\displaystyle\delta^{\prime}=\epsilon^{a_{N+1}\cdots a_{N+n}}_{12\cdots n}\prod_{\ell=N+1}^{N+n}\delta_{m_{\ell}=-L-1}.

(7.24)

7.3
Definition of the conformal limit of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)

Definition 7.2
.

Fix an integer
r\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
, we define the charge
r
conformal limit Hilbert space
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
to be the graded vector space which is degree-wise completion of the inverse system
\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k),p_{nL+r}\right\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d},\;\text{where }\;\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\>\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d}.

(7.25)

Here we use
p_{N}
in Definition
7.1
to define the inverse limit. Moreover, we denote the natural projection from the limit to a finite stage by
p^{\infty}_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
.

Remark 7.2
.

Since the transition maps are graded
\gl_{n}[z]
-module maps, it follows that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
inherits a graded
\gl_{n}[z]
-module structure such that
p^{\infty}_{N}
are graded
\gl_{n}[z]
-module morphisms.

Lemma 7.6
.

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}
is finite dimensional for all
n,k,d,r
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that for fixed
n,k,d
, there exists
C(n,k,d)\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\dim\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}\leq C(n,k,d)
for all
N
. Since the multiplication map
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,1)^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
is surjective, it is enough to prove the lemma for
k=1
. Using Corollary
4.6
, we have graded vector space isomorphism

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,1)\cong\bigoplus_{N_{1}+\cdots+N_{n}=N}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N_{1}}(1,1)\otimes\cdots\otimes\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N_{n}}(1,1),

(7.26)

therefore it suffices to prove the lemma for
n=k=1
. As explained in Example
4.1
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(1,1)
is isomorphic to
\mathbb{C}[\mathbb{A}^{(N)}]
as graded vector space. Thus
\dim\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(1,1)_{d}\leq p(d)
, where
p(d)
is the number of partitions of
d
. So we take
C(1,1,d)=p(d)
, and take

\displaystyle C(n,k,d)=\sum_{d=\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{k}d_{i,j}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}\prod_{j=1}^{k}p(d_{i,j}).

(7.27)

This finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 7.7
.

The inverse system in (
7.25
) stabilizes for
L\gg 0
, i.e.
p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d}
are isomorphisms for
L\gg 0
and fixed
n,k,d
.

Proof.

Since
\dim\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d}\leq\dim\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}<\infty
for all
L
, there exists
M\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\dim\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d}=\dim\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nM+r}(n,k)_{d}\text{ for all }L\geq M,

whence
p_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{(n+1)L+r}(n,k)_{d}}
are isomorphisms for all
L\geq M
. This implies that
p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}}
are isomorphisms for
L\geq M
.
∎

Another corollary is that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
can be reconstructed using the direct system
\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k),\sigma_{N}\right\}
. Namely, let us define
\sigma^{\infty}_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
to be the
M\rightarrow\infty
limit of the system of maps

\displaystyle\sigma_{N+nM}\circ\cdots\circ\sigma_{N+n}\circ\sigma_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+nM+n}(n,k).

By construction,
\sigma^{\infty}_{N}
is a graded homomorphism, and it is a section of
p^{\infty}_{N}
, i.e.
p^{\infty}_{N}\circ\sigma^{\infty}_{N}=\mathrm{Id}
. Moreover,
\sigma^{\infty}_{N+n}\circ\sigma_{N}=\sigma^{\infty}_{N}
, and this induces a graded homomorphism

\displaystyle\sigma^{(r)}_{\infty}:\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\\
L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\>\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k).

(7.28)

Proposition 7.8
.

\sigma^{(r)}_{\infty}
in (
7.28
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Since
p^{\infty}_{N}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}}
is isomorphism for
N\gg 0
, the same is true for
\sigma^{\infty}_{N}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}}
. Taking direct limit, we see that
\sigma^{(r)}_{\infty}
is an isomorphism on degree-
d
component, therefore
\sigma^{(r)}_{\infty}
is an isomorphism.
∎

The tensor multiplication map
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,1)^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is well-defined as the
L\rightarrow\infty
limit of the system of tensor multiplication maps
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,1)^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
, since the latter are compatible with transition maps. Similarly, the level-rank map
\tau^{(r)}_{\infty}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(kr)}_{\infty}(kn,1)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is well-defined as the
L\rightarrow\infty
limit of the system of level-rank maps
\tau_{nL+r}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{k(nL+r)}(kn,1)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
, which are compatible with transition maps by (
7.14
).

Proposition 7.9
.

The tensor multiplication map
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,1)^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
and the level-rank map
\tau^{(r)}_{\infty}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(kr)}_{\infty}(kn,1)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
are surjective.

Proof.

To show the surjectivity of tensor multiplication map, it is enough to show that it is degree-wise surjective, i.e. for every fixed
d
,

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{d=d_{1}+\cdots+d_{k}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{k}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,1)_{d_{i}}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}

is surjective. We choose
N
such that
\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,1)_{d^{\prime}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,1)_{d^{\prime}}
and
\mathfrak{p}^{\infty}_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}
are isomorphisms for all
d^{\prime}\leq d
. Then the result follows from the surjectivity of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,1)^{\otimes k}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
. The proof of surjectivity of the level-rank map is similar and we omit the detail.
∎

Proposition 7.10
.

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is a cyclic
\gl_{n}[z]
-module. Namely,
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is generated from a ground state
\ket{v}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{0}
by
\gl_{n}[z]
-action.

Proof.

We need to show that
U(\gl_{n}[z])\otimes\ket{v}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is surjective. Since this map is graded, it is enough to show that
U(\gl_{n}[z])_{d}\otimes\ket{v}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}
is surjective for all
d
. Let us fix
d
and choose
N
such that
p^{\infty}_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}
is isomorphism, then the result follows from the surjectivity of
U(\gl_{n}[z])\otimes p^{\infty}_{N}(\ket{v})\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
(Corollary
5.4
).
∎

Recall the character of a graded
\gl_{n}
module defined in (
4.15
), and according to our construction of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
and Theorem
4.3
, we have

\displaystyle\ch_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k))=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\mathfrak{A}^{-kL}q^{-\frac{k}{2}L(L-1)n-krL}\chi_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\overline{\Gr}^{(nL+r)\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))\prod_{i=1}^{nL+r}\frac{1}{1-q^{i}}.

Here
\mathfrak{A}=\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{a}_{i}
. Notice that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{-kL}q^{-\frac{k}{2}L(L-1)n-krL}\chi_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\overline{\Gr}^{(nL+r)\omega_{1}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k))=\chi_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\overline{\Gr}^{(nL+r)\omega_{1}-L\omega_{n}}_{\GL_{n}},\mathcal{O}(k)),

thus we have

\displaystyle\ch_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k))=\chi_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\overline{\Gr}^{(r)}_{\GL_{n},\mathrm{red}},\mathcal{O}(k))\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{i}}.

Here
\overline{\Gr}^{(r)}_{\GL_{n},\mathrm{red}}
is the
r
-th connected component of affine Grassmannian
\overline{\Gr}^{(r)}_{\GL_{n}}
endowed with reduced scheme structure. According to
[
50
, Theorem 2.5.5]
,
\chi_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\overline{\Gr}^{(r)}_{\GL_{n},\mathrm{red}},\mathcal{O}(k))
is dual to
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})
, where
L_{k\varpi_{n-r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})
is the level
k
integrable representation with highest weight
k\varpi_{n-r}
of
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)
. Therefore we get

\displaystyle\ch_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k))=\ch_{q,\mathbf{a}}(L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}))\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{i}}.

(7.29)

If we interpret
\prod_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-q^{i}}
as the character of a Fock space, then
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
has the same character as
L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}
. In the next section we will show that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is an integrable
\widehat{\gl}(n)
-module of level
k
and is isomorphic to
L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\gl}(n)_{k})
.
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is already a cyclic
\gl_{n}[z]
module, and we will see in the next section that the annihilation operators in
\widehat{\gl}(n)
are conformal limits of certain rescaling of operators
\mathsf{T}_{m,0}(E^{a}_{b})
in (
5.5
).

7.3.1
Semi-infinite wedge construction in the case of
k=1

In the case of
k=1
, our matrix model reduces to the spin Calogero-Sutherland model, and Uglov constructed its conformal limit via the semi-infinite wedge
[
58
,
41
]
. We briefly recall his construction here.

Definition 7.3
.

Fix an integer
r\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
, we define the charge
r
conformal limit fermion Fock space
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
to be the graded vector space which is degree-wise completion of the inverse system
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{nL+r}(n),p^{\mathfrak{f}}_{nL+r}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)_{d},\;\text{where }\;\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)_{d}:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\>\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{nL+r}(n)_{d}.

(7.30)

By construction,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
is isomorphic to
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,1)
via the isomorphism
\mathfrak{f}_{\infty}
which is the limit of isomorphisms
\{\mathfrak{f}_{nL+r}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
.

\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
has a basis

\begin{split}&\psi^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\wedge\psi^{a_{2}}_{m_{2}}\wedge\cdots,\quad(m_{1}\geq m_{2}\geq\cdots,\text{ and }a_{i}<a_{j}\text{ when }m_{i}=m_{j}),\\
&m_{j}=\lfloor\frac{r-j}{n}\rfloor\text{ when }j\gg 0.\end{split}

(7.31)

The energy grading on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
is such that

\displaystyle\deg(\psi^{a_{1}}_{m_{1}}\wedge\psi^{a_{2}}_{m_{2}}\wedge\cdots)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}m_{j}-\lfloor\frac{r-j}{n}\rfloor.

(7.32)

The space of ground states
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)_{0}
is spanned by

\begin{split}&\psi^{a_{1}}_{0}\wedge\cdots\wedge\psi^{a_{r}}_{0}\wedge\psi^{1}_{-1}\wedge\cdots\wedge\psi^{n}_{-1}\wedge\psi^{1}_{-2}\wedge\cdots\wedge\psi^{n}_{-2}\wedge\cdots.\end{split}

(7.33)

The graded
\gl_{n}[z]
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)
is given by:

\displaystyle E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{\ell\geq-m}\psi^{a}_{\ell+m}\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{b}_{\ell}}-\sum_{\ell<-m}\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{b}_{\ell}}\psi^{a}_{\ell+m}.

(7.34)

If we allow
m
in the above equation to be negative, then we get a graded
\widehat{\gl}(n)
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}_{N}(n)
. For the convenience of later discussions, we swap the positive and negative modes to align with VOA conventions.

Proposition 7.11

(
[
39
, Lecture 9]
)
.

The assignment

\displaystyle J^{a}_{b,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\sum_{\ell\geq 0}\psi^{a}_{\ell}\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{b}_{\ell+m}}-\sum_{\ell<0}\frac{\partial}{\partial\psi^{b}_{\ell+m}}\psi^{a}_{\ell}

(7.35)

defines a graded
\widehat{\gl}(n)_{1}
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n):

\displaystyle[J^{a}_{b,p},J^{c}_{d,q}]=\delta^{c}_{b}J^{a}_{d,p+q}-\delta^{a}_{d}J^{c}_{b,p+q}+kp\delta_{p+q,0}\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{c}_{b}.

(7.36)

Moreover,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
is isomorphic to
L_{\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\gl}(n)_{1})
, the
r
-th fundamental representation of
\widehat{\gl}(n)_{1}
, with respect to the above action. In particular,
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
is irreducible and integrable.

Note that
J^{a}_{b,-m}=E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m}
for all
m\geq 0
. The above proposition is the starting point of our discussion on the conformal limit of operators acting on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
, which we will carried out in detail in the next section.

8
Conformal Limit, Part II: Operators

8.1
Level-rank duality and
\widehat{\gl}(n)
module structure

As we explained in the last section, the
\gl_{n}[z]
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,1)\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
extends to an integrable
\widehat{\gl}(n)_{1}
-action, such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,1)
is isomorphic to the
r
-th fundamental representation
L_{\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\gl}(n)_{1})
.

Now consider the semi-infinite wedge space
\widetilde{\mathcal{F}}^{(kr)}_{\infty}(kn)
, which is isomorphic to
L_{\varpi_{kr}}(\widehat{\gl}(kn)_{1})
. Since
\widehat{\gl}(kn)_{1}
contains subalgebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(k)_{n}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}\oplus\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}
in a diagonal manner,
L_{\varpi_{kr}}(\widehat{\gl}(kn)_{1})
decomposes accordingly into direct sum of tensor products of irreducible modules of
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(k)_{n}
,
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}
, and
\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}
. The following level-rank duality result is due to I. Frenkel, see also
[
59
, Theorem AFF]
.

Theorem 8.1

(
[
60
, Theorem 1.6]
)
.

As an
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(k)_{n}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}\oplus\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}
-module,

\displaystyle L_{\varpi_{kr}}(\widehat{\gl}(kn)_{1})\cong\bigoplus_{\lambda}L_{\lambda}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(k)_{n})\otimes L_{\lambda^{\mathrm{t}}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}),

(8.1)

where the summation is taken for all
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{k})\in\mathbb{Z}^{k}
such that
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\lambda_{i}=kr
and
\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{k}\leq n
. Here
\lambda^{\mathrm{t}}
is the transpose of
\lambda
in the sense of a Maya diagram
[
59
]
.

By the proposition
7.9
, there exists a graded surjective
\gl_{n}[z]
-module map
\tau^{(r)}_{\infty}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(kr)}_{\infty}(kn,1)\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
. Since the
\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]
subalgebra of
\gl_{kn}[z]
acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
trivially,
\tau^{(r)}_{\infty}
factors through the
\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]
-coinvariant, i.e.

\displaystyle\bar{\tau}^{(r)}_{\infty}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(kr)}_{\infty}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}\twoheadrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k).

(8.2)

Proposition 8.2
.

The map
\bar{\tau}^{(r)}_{\infty}
in (
8.2
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Since
\bar{\tau}^{(r)}_{\infty}
is surjective, it is enough to show that two sides of
\bar{\tau}^{(r)}_{\infty}
have the same character. The character of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is computed in (
7.29
), which equals to the character of
L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}
. In view of the direct sum decomposition of (
8.1
), the only summand which is nonzero after taking
\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]
-coinvariant is the one corresponding to
\lambda=(n,\cdots,n)
. In fact, we have

\displaystyle L_{\lambda}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(k)_{n})_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]}=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C},&\text{if }\forall(i,j),\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{j},\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

It follows that

\displaystyle\ch_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(kr)}_{\infty}(kn,1)_{\mathfrak{sl}_{k}[z]})=\ch_{q,\mathbf{a}}(L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock})=\ch_{q,\mathbf{a}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)).

This finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 8.3
.

The
\gl_{n}[z]
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
extends to an
\widehat{\gl}(n)_{k}
-action, such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is isomorphic to
L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
. In particular,
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is irreducible and integrable.

Definition 8.1
.

We denote the generators of
\widehat{\gl}(n)_{k}
acting on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
by
\{J^{a}_{b,m}\>|\>1\leq a,b\leq n,m\in\mathbb{Z}\}
such that
\forall m\geq 0
,
J^{a}_{b,-m}=E^{a}_{b}\otimes z^{m}
where the latter is
\gl_{n}[z]
generator. In this convention
J^{a}_{b,>0}\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{0}=0
. We denote

\displaystyle J^{a}_{b,m}=\bar{J}^{a}_{b,m}+\frac{\delta^{a}_{b}}{n}\alpha_{m},

(8.3)

where
\bar{J}^{a}_{b,m}
is the traceless part (i.e.
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}
generators), and
\alpha_{m}
is the trace part (i.e.
\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}
generators).

We will show that the annihilation operators
J^{a}_{b,m},(m>0)
arises from the matrix model operators
\mathsf{T}_{m,0}(E^{a}_{b})
via certain scaling limit, see Theorem
8.31
.

8.2
Y(\gl_{n})
action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)

Consider the assignment

\begin{split}&\widetilde{T}^{a}_{b}(u)\mapstochar\rightarrow\left[\delta^{a}_{b}+A^{a}\frac{1}{u+(k+n)L-XY}B_{b}\right]\frac{\left(1+\frac{u}{n+k}\right)_{L}}{\left(1+\frac{u+k}{n+k}\right)_{L}},\\
&\text{where }L=\lfloor\frac{N}{n}\rfloor,\;r=N-nL.\\
&\text{Here we use the Pochhammer symbol notation $(x)_{a}=\frac{\Gamma(x+a)}{\Gamma(x)}$}.\end{split}

(8.4)

Note that
\widetilde{T}^{a}_{b}(u)
in (
8.4
) is obtained from
{T}^{a}_{b}(u)
in (
5.9
) by spectral parameter shift
u\mapstochar\rightarrow u+(k+n)L
followed by multiplying a function
f(u)
, therefore (
8.4
) gives rise to a Yangian algebra
Y(\gl_{n})
-action on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
, i.e.
\widetilde{T}^{a}_{b}(u)
satisfies the RTT relation

\displaystyle(u-v)[\widetilde{T}^{a}_{b}(u),\widetilde{T}^{c}_{d}(v)]=\widetilde{T}^{c}_{b}(u)\widetilde{T}^{a}_{d}(v)-\widetilde{T}^{c}_{b}(v)\widetilde{T}^{a}_{d}(u).

Definition 8.2
.

Fix
r\in\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
. Write
\widetilde{T}^{a}_{b}(u)=\delta^{a}_{b}+\sum_{m\geq 0}\widetilde{T}^{a}_{b;m}u^{-m-1}
. We denote the Yangian generator
\widetilde{T}^{a}_{b;m}
which acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
by
\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}
.

Theorem 8.4
.

p_{N}
and
\sigma_{N}
are
Y(\gl_{n})
-module maps with respect to the
Y(\gl_{n})
-actions defined by (
8.4
), i.e.

\displaystyle p_{N}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b}(u)=\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b}(u)\circ p_{N},\quad\sigma_{N}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b}(u)=\prescript{L+1}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b}(u)\circ\sigma_{N}.

(8.5)

Remark 8.1
.

Since the Hermitian inner product
\langle\cdot|\cdot\rangle
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
is skew-invariant under the action of
Y(\gl_{n})
, i.e.

\displaystyle\langle\widetilde{T}^{a}_{b}(u)\cdot w|{v}\rangle=\langle w|\widetilde{T}^{b}_{a}(u)\cdot v\rangle

for all
\ket{w},\ket{v}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
, then
p_{N}
being
Y(\gl_{n})
-module map implies
\sigma_{N}
being
Y(\gl_{n})
-module map. In fact, suppose
p_{N}
is a
Y(\gl_{n})
-module map, then
\ker(p_{N})
is
Y(\gl_{n})
-submodule of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k)
, thus
\ker(p_{N})^{\perp}
is also a
Y(\gl_{n})
-submodule of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k)
, therefore
\sigma_{N}
is a
Y(\gl_{n})
-module map by construction. So we only need to prove that
p_{N}
is a
Y(\gl_{n})
-module map.

Remark 8.2
.

Let us recall
{T}^{a}_{b}(u)
defined in (
5.9
):
{T}^{a}_{b}(u)=\delta^{a}_{b}+A^{a}\frac{1}{u-XY}B_{b}
, then it is elementary to see that

\displaystyle p_{N}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b}(u)=\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b}(u)\circ p_{N}\;\Longleftrightarrow\;p_{N}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{T}^{a}_{b}(u)=\frac{u+k}{u}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{T}^{a}_{b}(u-n-k)\circ p_{N}.

(8.6)

Therefore it suffices to prove the equation for
{T}^{a}_{b}(u)
in (
8.6
) in order to prove that
p_{N}
is a
Y(\gl_{n})
-module map.

Remark 8.3
.

Recall that for
m\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, the quantum minor
A_{m}(u)
is defined as

\displaystyle A_{m}(u):=\mathrm{qdet}\>T^{a}_{b}(u)_{1\leq a,b\leq m}=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{m}}\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)\prod_{1\leq i\leq m}^{\rightarrow}T^{\sigma(i)}_{i}(u-i+1).

(8.7)

It is well-known that
Y(\gl_{n})
is generated by
\{T^{a}_{b;0}\>|\>1\leq a,b\leq n\}
and coefficients of
\{A_{m}(u)\>|\>1\leq m\leq n\}
. Then, in order to prove the equation for
{T}^{a}_{b}(u)
in (
8.6
), it is enough to show that

\displaystyle p_{N}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{T}^{a}_{b;0}=\left(\prescript{L}{}{T}^{a}_{b;0}+k\delta^{a}_{b}\right)\circ p_{N},

(8.8)

and that

\displaystyle p_{N}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{A}_{m}(u)=\frac{(u-m+1+k)_{m}}{(u-m+1)_{m}}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{A}_{m}(u-n-k)\circ p_{N},\quad(1\leq m\leq n).

(8.9)

Since
T^{a}_{b;0}=\mathsf{T}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})
, (
8.8
) follows from Proposition
7.5
and the definition of
\gl_{n}[z]
action (
7.11
).

Now, Remark
8.1
,
8.2
, and
8.3
reduce the proof of Theorem
8.4
to the proof of equation (
8.9
), which is the main goal of the rest of this subsection.

Observation.
Let us consider the
(n+N)\times(n+N)
matrix of operators:

\mathsf{E}:=\quad\begin{pNiceArray}

(8.10)

then
\mathsf{E}
satisfies the
\gl_{n+N}
commutation relations:

\displaystyle[\mathsf{E}^{\mu}_{\nu},\mathsf{E}^{\rho}_{\sigma}]=\delta^{\rho}_{\nu}\mathsf{E}^{\mu}_{\sigma}-\delta^{\mu}_{\sigma}\mathsf{E}^{\rho}_{\nu},\quad(1\leq\mu,\nu,\rho,\sigma\leq n+N).

\mathsf{E}
acts on
\mathbb{C}[V(N,n)]=\mathbb{C}[X,A]
naturally, so we consider the following operator acting on
\mathbb{C}[X,A]
:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}^{a}_{b}(u):=\delta^{a}_{b}+\sum_{s\geq 1}\frac{(\mathsf{E}^{s})^{a}_{b}}{u^{s}},\quad(1\leq a,b\leq n),

(8.11)

i.e.
\mathfrak{T}(u)
is the restriction of
(1-\mathsf{E}/u)^{-1}
to the upper
n\times n
block.
\mathfrak{T}(u)
commutes with
(XY)^{i}_{j}
,
(YX)^{i}_{j}
, and
(BA)^{i}_{j}
, so
\mathfrak{T}(u)
commutes with the moment map
\mu^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}(\gl_{N})
defined in (
4.5
), therefore
\mathfrak{T}(u)
’s action on
\mathbb{C}[X,A]
leaves
\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
invariant.

Lemma 8.5
.

When restricted to
\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
, we have the following equation between operators:

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}^{a}_{b}(u)=T^{a}_{b}(u-n-k).

(8.12)

Proof.

First of all, it is straightforward to compute that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{T}^{a}_{b}(u)=\delta^{a}_{b}+A^{a}\frac{1}{u-YX-BA}B_{b}.

(8.13)

To relate the right-hand-side of the above equation to
T^{a}_{b}(u)
, we notice that for a family of operator
\{\mathcal{O}^{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
transforming in the
\gl_{N}
vector representation
8
8

8

This means that
[\mu^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}(E^{i}_{j}),\mathcal{O}^{\ell}]=\delta^{\ell}_{j}\mathcal{O}^{i}
.
, we have

\displaystyle[\mu^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}(E^{i}_{j}),\mathcal{O}^{j}]=N\mathcal{O}^{i}.

Using the definition of
\mu^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}
, we have
\mu^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}(E^{i}_{j})=(XY)^{i}_{j}-(YX)^{i}_{j}-(BA)^{i}_{j}+(N+n)\delta^{i}_{j}
. Since
\mu^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}(E^{i}_{j})=-k\delta^{i}_{j}
when acting on
\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
, we then have

\displaystyle(XY)^{i}_{j}\mathcal{O}^{j}+(n+k)\mathcal{O}^{i}=\left((YX)^{i}_{j}+(BA)^{i}_{j}\right)\mathcal{O}^{j}.

(8.14)

Therefore we have

\displaystyle A^{a}\frac{1}{u-YX-BA}B_{b}

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq 0}u^{-s-1}A^{a}(YX+BA)^{s}B_{b}

by (
8.14
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\geq 0}u^{-s-1}A^{a}(XY+n+k)^{s}B_{b}

\displaystyle=A^{a}\frac{1}{u-n-k-XY}B_{b}.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Definition 8.3
.

Define
\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}
to be the lower
(n+N-m)\times(n+N-m)
block of
\mathsf{E}
:

\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}=\qquad\begin{pNiceArray}

In particular
\mathsf{E}=\prescript{}{0}{\mathsf{E}}
. We define the Capelli determinant of
\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}
to be

\displaystyle C_{m}(u):=\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n+N-m}}\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)\prod_{1\leq i\leq n+N-m}^{\rightarrow}\left((u+i+m-n-N)\delta^{\sigma(i)}_{i}-\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}^{\sigma(i)}_{i}\right)

(8.15)

Remark 8.4
.

If
m\leq n
, then
C_{m}(u)
commutes with
(XY)^{i}_{j}
,
(YX)^{i}_{j}
, and
(BA)^{i}_{j}
, so
C_{m}(u)
commutes with the moment map
\mu^{*}_{\mathbb{C}}(\gl_{N})
defined in (
4.5
), therefore
C_{m}(u)
’s action on
\mathbb{C}[X,A]
leaves
\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
invariant.

Remark 8.5
.

Coefficients of
C_{m}(u)
commutes with
\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}
, i.e. they are central elements in the universal enveloping algebra
U(\gl_{n+N-m})
generated by
\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}

[
61
]
. Moreover, if
V_{\mu}
is an irreducible
\gl_{n+N-m}
module with highest weight
\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{n+N-m})\in\mathbb{Z}^{n+N-m}
, then
C_{m}(u)
acts on
V_{\mu}
as the scalar
[
61
]
:

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n+N-m}(u-\mu_{i}+i+m-n-N).

(8.16)

Proposition 8.6

(
[
61
, Theorem 2.33]
)
.

For
1\leq m\leq n
, we have identity:

\displaystyle\mathrm{qdet}\>\left(1+\frac{\prescript{}{0}{\mathsf{E}}}{u}\right)\cdot\mathrm{qdet}\>\mathfrak{T}^{a}_{b}(-u+n+N-1)_{1\leq a,b\leq m}=\mathrm{qdet}\>\left(1+\frac{\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}}{u}\right).

(8.17)

Here the quantum determinant
\mathrm{qdet}\left(1+\frac{\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}}{u}\right)
is defined by

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma\in S_{n+N-m}}\mathrm{sgn}(\sigma)\prod_{1\leq i\leq n+N-m}^{\rightarrow}\left(\delta^{\sigma(i)}_{i}+\frac{\prescript{}{m}{\mathsf{E}}^{\sigma(i)}_{i}}{u-i+1}\right).

(8.18)

Corollary 8.7
.

When restricted to
\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N},-k}
, we have the following equation between operators:

\displaystyle A_{m}(u-n-k)=\frac{C_{m}(u-m)}{C_{0}(u)}(u-m+1)_{m}.

(8.19)

Proof.

Plug (
8.15
) into (
8.17
), then use Lemma
8.5
.
∎

Therefore, to study how
A_{m}(u)
transforms under the projection map
p_{N}
, it suffices to study how
C_{m}(u)
transforms under
p_{N}
. We state the result as follows.

Theorem 8.8
.

Fix
r\in\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
and
m\in\{0,\cdots,n\}
. We denote the Capelli determinant
C_{m}(u)
which acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
by
\prescript{L}{}{C}_{m}(u)
. Then we have

\displaystyle p_{N}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{C}_{m}(u)=(u+1-n)_{n}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{C}_{m}(u-n-k)\circ p_{N}.

(8.20)

Assume Theorem
8.8
for now, and we can deduce (
8.9
) as follows:

\displaystyle p_{N}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{A}_{m}(u)

by (
8.19
)

\displaystyle=(u+1+n+k-m)_{m}\cdot p_{N}\circ\frac{\prescript{L+1}{}{C}_{m}(u+n+k-m)}{\prescript{L+1}{}{C}_{0}(u+n+k)}

by (
8.20
)

\displaystyle=(u+1+n+k-m)_{m}\cdot\frac{(u+k+1-m)_{n}}{(u+k+1)_{n}}\cdot\frac{\prescript{L}{}{C}_{m}(u-m)}{\prescript{L}{}{C}_{0}(u)}\circ p_{N}

\displaystyle=(u+k+1-m)_{m}\cdot\frac{\prescript{L}{}{C}_{m}(u-m)}{\prescript{L}{}{C}_{0}(u)}\circ p_{N}

by (
8.19
)

\displaystyle=\frac{(u+k+1-m)_{m}}{(u+1-m)_{m}}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{A}_{m}(u-n-k)\circ p_{N}.

This proves Theorem
8.4
. The remaining of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
8.8
.

8.2.1
Decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)

Let us write
X,A
in the form of a
(N+n)\times N
matrix:

†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
pNiceArray (nicematrix)

(8.21)

The above matrix transforms as vector
\otimes
dual vector representation with respect to the
\GL_{N+n}\times\GL_{N}
action, where the generator of
\gl_{N+n}
is the matrix
\mathsf{E}
defined in (
8.10
) and the generator of
\gl_{N}
is
-A^{a}_{j}B^{i}_{a}-X^{l}_{j}Y^{i}_{l}
,
(1\leq i,j\leq N)
. Therefore, by the Schur-Weyl duality we have the following decomposition of polynomial ring
\mathbb{C}[X,A]
into simple
\GL_{N+n}\times\GL_{N}
-modules
9
9

9

Let
V
,
W
be two vector spaces, then by the Schur-Weyl duality we have
(V\otimes W^{*})^{\otimes d}=V^{\otimes d}\otimes W^{*\otimes d}=\bigoplus_{\lambda,\mu}V_{\lambda}\otimes S_{\lambda}\otimes W_{\mu}^{*}\otimes S_{\mu}
, where
V_{\lambda}
(resp.
W_{\mu}
) is the irreducible
\GL(V)
-module (resp.
\GL(W)
-module) with highest weight given by Young diagram
\lambda
(resp.
\mu
), and
S_{\lambda}
,
S_{\mu}
are irreducible
S_{d}
-module given by Young diagrams
\lambda
,
\mu
respectively. Then
\mathrm{Sym}^{d}(V\otimes W^{*})=\bigoplus_{\lambda,\mu}V_{\lambda}\otimes W_{\mu}^{*}\otimes(S_{\lambda}\otimes S_{\mu})^{S_{d}}
, where
S_{d}
acts diagonally, thus
\mathrm{Sym}^{d}(V\otimes W^{*})=\bigoplus_{\lambda}V_{\lambda}\otimes W_{\lambda}^{*}
. Applying the above argument to
V=\mathbb{C}^{N+n}
and
W=\mathbb{C}^{N}
gives (
8.22
).
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[X,A]=\bigoplus_{\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}}V^{N+n}_{\lambda}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda})^{*},

(8.22)

where
V^{N+n}_{\lambda}
(resp.
V^{N}_{\lambda}
) is the irreducible
\GL_{N+n}
-module (resp.
\GL_{N}
-module) with highest weight
\lambda
. Consider the “diagonal” subgroup
\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}}\subset\GL_{N+n}\times\GL_{N}
which is generated by
X^{i}_{l}Y^{l}_{j}-A^{a}_{j}B^{i}_{a}-X^{l}_{j}Y^{i}_{l}
,
(1\leq i,j\leq N)
. Then according to the definition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
, we have
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)=\mathbb{C}[X,A]^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}
, therefore we get a decomposition:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)=\bigoplus_{\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}}\left(V^{N+n}_{\lambda}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}.

(8.23)

Definition 8.4
.

We define the subspace
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda):=\left(V^{N+n}_{\lambda}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}
in (
8.23
).

Definition 8.5
.

For a tuple of integers
\mu=(\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{N})\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
, we define
\mu^{\shortdownarrow}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
such that
\mu^{\shortdownarrow}_{i}=\mu_{i}-k
, (
1\leq i\leq N
). Similarly we define
\mu^{\shortuparrow}\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
such that
\mu^{\shortuparrow}_{i}=\mu_{i}+k
, (
1\leq i\leq N
).

Lemma 8.9
.

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)\neq 0
if and only if
\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}\in\mathbb{N}^{N}
and
\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,N-n\}
,
\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda_{i+n}+k
.

Proof.

V^{N+n}_{\lambda}
decomposes further into irreducible
\GL_{n}\times\GL_{N}
modules:

\displaystyle V^{N+n}_{\lambda}=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=(\nu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\nu_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\\
\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\end{subarray}}M^{\lambda}_{\nu,\mu}\otimes V^{n}_{\nu}\otimes V^{N}_{\mu}.

(8.24)

Here
M^{\lambda}_{\nu,\mu}
is the multiplicity space which has dimension given by the Littlewood-Richardson coefficient
c^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}
. Therefore we have

\begin{split}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)&=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\nu=(\nu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\nu_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\\
\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\end{subarray}}M^{\lambda}_{\nu,\mu}\otimes V^{n}_{\nu}\otimes\Hom_{\GL_{N}}(V^{N}_{\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}},V^{N}_{\mu})\\
&\cong\begin{cases}\bigoplus_{\nu=(\nu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\nu_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}}M^{\lambda}_{\nu,\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}}\otimes V^{n}_{\nu}&,\text{ if }\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}\in\mathbb{N}^{N},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}\end{split}

(8.25)

Assume that
\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}\in\mathbb{N}^{N}
, then we have

\displaystyle\dim\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)=\sum_{\nu=(\nu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\nu_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}}c^{\lambda}_{\nu\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}}\dim V^{n}_{\nu}=s_{\lambda/\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}}(1,\cdots,1),

(8.26)

where
s_{\lambda/\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})
is the
n
-variable skew Schur function of the shape
\lambda/\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
. Thus
\dim\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)
equals to the number of semi-standard skew Young tableaux with shape
\lambda/\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
and entries
\in\{1,\cdots,n\}

[
62
]
. The latter number is nonzero if and only if there is no column in
\lambda/\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
with length greater than
n
, which is equivalent to that
\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,N-n\}
,
\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda_{i+n}+k
.
∎

Remark 8.6
.

We note that
\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}}
commutes with operators
\mathfrak{T}^{a}_{b}(u)
defined in (
8.11
). Then it follows from Lemma
8.5
that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)
is a
Y(\gl_{n})
-submodule of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
, where RTT generators of
Y(\gl_{n})
acts as
T^{a}_{b}(u)=\delta^{a}_{b}+A^{a}\frac{1}{u-XY}B_{b}
. In fact,
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)
is a special case of a class of irreducible
Y(\gl_{n})
-modules
\{V_{\omega}\>|\>\omega\text{ is a skew Young diagram}\}
constructed by Nazarov-Tarasov
[
63
]
, see also
[
64
]
. We identify that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)\cong V_{\lambda/\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}}(k-N)^{*}
.

Definition 8.6
.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}
be a Young diagram of length
N
. We say that
\lambda
is
admissible
if and only if it satisfies the conditions in Lemma
8.9
, that is,
\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}\in\mathbb{N}^{N}
and
\forall i\in\{1,\cdots,N-n\}
,
\lambda_{i}\geq\lambda_{i+n}+k
. When
N\geq n
, we say that
\lambda
is
cuttable
if and only
\lambda
is admissible and
\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}_{N-n+1}=0
. When
\lambda
is cuttable, we denote its
cut

\lambda^{\natural}:=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N-n})\in\mathbb{N}^{N-n}
.

Remark 8.7
.

If
\lambda
is cuttable then
\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
can be regarded as an element in
\mathbb{N}^{N-n}
, and in fact
\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}=\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow}
.

Lemma 8.10
.

There is a one to one correspondence:

\displaystyle\text{cuttable Young diagrams of length }N+n\>\overset{1:1}{\longleftrightarrow}\>\text{admissible Young diagrams of length }N.

(8.27)

The map from the left-hand-side to the right-hand-side is given by
\lambda\mapstochar\rightarrow\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow}
.

The proof of the above lemma is straightforward and we omit it.

Consider the following block decomposition of the
(A,X)
matrix of size
(N+2n)\times(N+n)
defined in (
8.21
):

†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
pNiceArray (nicematrix)

(8.28)

Here
X=(X_{1},X_{2})
is the corresponding block decomposition of the matrix
X
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[X_{1},X_{2},A,\eta]^{\GL_{N+n}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}\subseteq\mathbb{C}[X_{1},A]^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}\otimes\mathbb{C}[X_{2},\eta]^{\GL_{n}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k},

where
\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}}\times\GL_{n}^{\mathrm{diag}}\hookrightarrow\GL_{N+n}^{\mathrm{diag}}
as block diagonal matrices:

\begin{pNiceArray}\subset\GL_{N+n}

The polynomial ring
\mathbb{C}[X_{1},X_{2},A,\eta]
is equipped with natural action of
\GL_{N+2n}\times\GL_{N}\times\GL_{n}
. Here
\left(\begin{subarray}{c}A\\
X_{1}\end{subarray}\right)
transforms as vector under
\GL_{N+2n}
, as dual vector under
\GL_{N}
, and trivially under
\GL_{n}
, and
\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\eta\\
X_{2}\end{subarray}\right)
transforms as vector under
\GL_{N+2n}
, as dual vector under
\GL_{n}
, and trivially under
\GL_{N}
. Using the Schur-Weyl duality, we can decomposes
\mathbb{C}[X_{1},X_{2},A,\eta]
as

\begin{split}\mathbb{C}[X_{1},X_{2},A,\eta]&=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime}=(\lambda^{\prime}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda^{\prime}_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\\
\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\end{subarray}}V^{N+2n}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{*}\otimes V^{N+2n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\otimes(V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\\
&=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N+n})\in\mathbb{N}^{N+n}\\
\lambda^{\prime}=(\lambda^{\prime}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda^{\prime}_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\\
\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{n})\in\mathbb{N}^{n}\end{subarray}}M^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{N+2n}_{\lambda}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{*}\otimes(V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}.\end{split}

(8.29)

Here
M^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
is the multiplicity space.

Definition 8.7
.

We define the subspace

\displaystyle\mathcal{V}_{N+n}(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime}):=\left(V^{N+2n}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}\otimes\left(V^{N+2n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\otimes(V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{n}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}

(8.30)

We have inclusion

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(\lambda)\subset\bigoplus_{c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0}\mathcal{V}_{N+n}(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime}),

(8.31)

where
c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime\prime}}
is the Littlewood-Richardson coefficient.

Now consider the restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
defined in Section
7.2
.

Lemma 8.11
.

Under the restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
,

\displaystyle\text{image of }\mathcal{V}_{N+n}(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime})\subseteq\begin{cases}\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda^{\prime})&,\text{ if $\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(k^{n})$ and $\lambda^{\prime}$ is admissible},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(8.32)

Proof.

The restriction map sets the last
n
rows of
X_{1}
and the whole
X_{2}
to zero, thus the image of
\mathcal{V}_{N+n}(\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime})
is contained in

\displaystyle\left(V^{N+n}_{\lambda^{\prime}}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}\otimes V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\otimes\left((V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{n},-k}.

(8.33)

The
\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}}
semi-invariant is
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda^{\prime})
, and

\displaystyle\left((V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{n},-k}\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}&,\text{ if $\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(k^{n})$},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus the vector space (
8.33
) is nontrivial if and only if
\lambda^{\prime}
is admissible (by Lemma
8.9
) and
\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(k^{n})
. Moreover, when
\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(k^{n})
,
V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\otimes\left((V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{n},-k}
is one-dimensional and it is generated by
\det(\eta)^{k}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 8.12
.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N+n})\in\mathbb{N}^{N+n}
. Then we have

\displaystyle p_{N}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(\lambda))\subseteq\begin{cases}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow})&,\text{ if $\lambda$ is cuttable},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(8.34)

Proof.

Let us assume that
\lambda
is admissible, otherwise
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(\lambda)=0
by Lemma
8.9
and (
8.34
) trivially holds. In view of (
8.31
) and the definition of
p_{N}
(Definition
7.1
), Lemma
8.11
implies that

\displaystyle p_{N}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(\lambda))\subseteq\sum_{c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime},(k^{n})}\neq 0}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda^{\prime\shortdownarrow}),

where the
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda^{\prime})
in Lemma
8.11
becomes
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda^{\prime\shortdownarrow})
in the above because we need to multiply
\det(X)^{-k}
to get
p_{N}
. We claim there exists
\lambda^{\prime}
that makes
c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime},(k^{n})}
nonzero if and only if
\lambda
is cuttable and
\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda^{\natural}
. If
c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime},(k^{n})}\neq 0
, then
\lambda^{\prime}\subseteq\lambda
, in fact
\lambda^{\prime}\subseteq\lambda^{\natural}
because
\lambda^{\prime}
has length
N
. On the other hand,
\lambda
being admissible implies that
\lambda_{i}\geq k
for all
N<i\leq N+n
. Therefore
|\lambda|-|\lambda^{\prime}|-|(k^{n})|\geq 0
, and the equality holds if and only if
\lambda_{i}=k
for all
N<i\leq N+n
and
\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda^{\natural}
. So we see that
c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime},(k^{n})}\neq 0
implies that
\lambda
is cuttable and
\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda^{\natural}
. Conversely, for a cuttable
\lambda
,
c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\natural},(k^{n})}=1
. This proves our claim, and the proposition follows from the claim.
∎

8.2.2
Branching

For
m\in\{1,\cdots,n\}
, let us consider the block-diagonal subgroup
\GL_{m}\times\GL_{n+N-m}\subset\GL_{n+N}
:

†
†
nicematrix-placeholder:
pNiceArray (nicematrix)

(8.35)

We can replace the
\GL_{n+N}
by this subgroup in the decomposition (
8.22
), and get

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[X,A]=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\\
\nu=(\nu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\nu_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\end{subarray}}M^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu}\otimes V^{n+N-m}_{\mu}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda})^{*},

(8.36)

where
M^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}
is the multiplicity space. Taking

\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}}
semi-invariant, we get a decomposition:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)=\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\\
\nu=(\nu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\nu_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}\\
\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\end{subarray}}M^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu}\otimes\left(V^{n+N-m}_{\mu}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}.

(8.37)

Definition 8.8
.

We define the subspace
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right):=\bigoplus_{\nu}M^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu}\otimes\left(V^{n+N-m}_{\mu}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}
in (
8.37
).

Compare Definition
8.8
with Definition
8.4
, we find:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)=\bigoplus_{\mu}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right).

Let us describe the pair of Young diagrams
(\lambda,\mu)
such that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)
is nonzero.

Definition 8.9
.

Let us fix
m,N\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}
, and for a pair of integer tuples
\lambda,\mu\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
, we write
\lambda\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu
if and only if for all
i\in\{1,\cdots,N\}
,
\lambda_{i}\geq\mu_{i}\geq\lambda_{i+m}
(we set
\lambda_{i}=0
if
i\notin\{1,\cdots,N\}
).

Remark 8.8
.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N})
and
\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{N})
be Young diagrams of length
N
, then
\lambda\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu
if and only if
\mu\subseteq\lambda
and the skew Young diagram
\lambda/\mu
has no column with length greater than
m
, equivalently the
m
-variable skew Schur function
s_{\lambda/\mu}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m})
is nonzero.

Remark 8.9
.

If
\lambda\overset{p}{\searrow}\mu\overset{q}{\searrow}\nu
, then
\lambda\overset{p+q}{\searrow}\nu
.

Lemma 8.13
.

\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)\neq 0
if and only if
\lambda
is admissible and
\lambda\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
.

Proof.

If
\lambda
is not admissible, then
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\lambda)=0
by Lemma
8.9
, whence
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)=0
, so we may assume that
\lambda
is admissible in the following. We observe that
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)\neq 0
if and only if
\left(V^{n+N-m}_{\mu}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}\neq 0
and
\bigoplus_{\nu}M^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu}\neq 0
. Using the same argument as the proof of Lemma
8.9
, the former holds if and only if
\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}\subseteq\mu
and
s_{\mu/\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{n-m})\neq 0
, which is equivalent to
\mu\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
. Using the identity

\displaystyle s_{\lambda/\mu}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m})=\sum_{\nu=(\nu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\nu_{m})\in\mathbb{N}^{m}}c^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}s_{\nu}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m}),

we see that
\bigoplus_{\nu}M^{\lambda}_{\nu\mu}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu}\neq 0
if and only if
s_{\lambda/\mu}(x_{1},\cdots,x_{m})\neq 0
, which is equivalent to
\lambda\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu
. This finishes the proof.
∎

We collect some combinatorial facts in Lemma
8.14
and
8.15
.

Lemma 8.14
.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N})
and
\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{N})
be Young diagrams of length
N
, and assume that
\lambda
is cuttable and
\lambda\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
, then

\begin{split}&\mu_{i}=k,\;\forall N-n<i\leq N-m,\text{ and}\\
&\mu_{i}=0,\;\forall N-m<i.\end{split}

(8.38)

Proof.

Since
\lambda
is cuttable and
\lambda\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu
, then for all
N-n<i\leq N-m
,
\lambda_{i}=k\geq\mu_{i}\geq k=\lambda_{i+m}
, this forces
\mu_{i}=k
. Since
\lambda
is cuttable and
\mu\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
, then for all
i>N-m
,
\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}_{i-n+m}=0\geq\mu_{i}
, this forces
\mu_{i}=0
.
∎

Lemma 8.15
.

Let us fix a cuttable
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}^{N+n}
, then there is a one to one correspondence:

\displaystyle\{\mu\in\mathbb{N}^{N+n}\>|\>\lambda\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}\}\>\overset{1:1}{\longleftrightarrow}\>\{\nu\in\mathbb{N}^{N}\>|\>\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow}\overset{m}{\searrow}\nu\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow\shortdownarrow}\}.

(8.39)

The map from the left-hand-side to the right-hand-side is given by
\mu\mapstochar\rightarrow\mu^{\natural\shortdownarrow}
.

Proof.

Let us give the inverse map: starting from a
\nu\in\mathbb{N}^{N}
such that
\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow}\overset{m}{\searrow}\nu\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow\shortdownarrow}
, we append the rectangular Young diagram
(k^{n-m})
to the right of
\nu^{\shortuparrow}
. This is the inverse map of
\mu\mapstochar\rightarrow\mu^{\natural\shortdownarrow}
due to Lemma
8.14
.
∎

In view of the decomposition (
8.29
), we can replace the
\GL_{N+2n}
action by the subgroup
\GL_{m}\times\GL_{N+2n-m}
action, where the latter is the given in the block matrix (
8.35
) with
N
replaced by
N+n
. The associated decomposition reads:

\mathbb{C}[X_{1},X_{2},A,\eta]=\left(\bigoplus_{\lambda^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},\nu^{\prime}}M^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\nu^{\prime}\mu^{\prime}}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu^{\prime}}\otimes V^{N+2n-m}_{\mu^{\prime}}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{*}\right)\\
\otimes\left(\bigoplus_{\lambda^{\prime\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime},\nu^{\prime\prime}}M^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}\mu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{N+2n-m}_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes(V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\right).

(8.40)

Definition 8.10
.

We define the subspace

\mathcal{V}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime}\\
\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}\right):=\left(\bigoplus_{\nu^{\prime}}M^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\nu^{\prime}\mu^{\prime}}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu^{\prime}}\otimes\left(V^{N+2n-m}_{\mu^{\prime}}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}\right)\\
\otimes\left(\bigoplus_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}M^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}\mu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes\left(V^{N+2n-m}_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes(V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{n}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}\right).

(8.41)

We have inclusion

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)\subset\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime}\lambda^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0\\
c^{\mu}_{\mu^{\prime}\mu^{\prime\prime}}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\mathcal{V}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime}\\
\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}\right).

(8.42)

Lemma 8.16
.

Under the restriction map
\mathbb{C}[V(N+n,n)]\rightarrow\mathbb{C}[\widetilde{V}(N,n)]
, the image of
\mathcal{V}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime}\\
\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}\right)
is contained in

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\det(\eta)^{k}\cdot\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime}\\
\mu^{\prime}\end{subarray}\right)&,\text{ if $\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(k^{n})$ and $\mu^{\prime\prime}=(k^{n-m})$ and $\lambda^{\prime}$ is admissible and }\lambda^{\prime}\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu^{\prime}\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\prime\shortdownarrow},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(8.43)

Proof.

The restriction map sets the last
n
rows of
X_{1}
and the whole
X_{2}
to zero, thus the image of
\mathcal{V}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime},\lambda^{\prime\prime}\\
\mu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime\prime}\end{subarray}\right)
is contained in

\left(\bigoplus_{\nu^{\prime}}M^{\lambda^{\prime}}_{\nu^{\prime}\mu^{\prime}}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu^{\prime}}\otimes\left(V^{N+n-m}_{\mu^{\prime}}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda^{\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}},-k}\right)\\
\otimes\left(\bigoplus_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}M^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}\mu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{n-m}_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes\left((V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{n},-k}\right).

(8.44)

The tensor component involving
\GL_{N}^{\mathrm{diag}}
semi-invariant is
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime}\\
\mu^{\prime}\end{subarray}\right)
, and we have

\displaystyle\left((V^{n}_{\lambda^{\prime\prime}})^{*}\right)^{\GL_{n},-k}\cong\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}&,\text{ if $\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(k^{n})$},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

Thus the vector space (
8.44
) is nontrivial only if
\lambda^{\prime}
is admissible and
\lambda^{\prime}\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu^{\prime}\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\prime\shortdownarrow}
(by Lemma
8.13
) and
\lambda^{\prime\prime}=(k^{n})
. Let us assume that the above conditions are satisfied, then the vector space
M^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}\mu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{n-m}_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}
is the
(\nu^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})
branch of
V^{n}_{(k^{n})}
(
k
-th power of determinant representation) into
\GL_{m}\times\GL_{n-m}
representations, which is nontrivial if an only if
\nu^{\prime}=(k^{m})
and
\mu^{\prime}=(k^{n-m})
. When
\mu^{\prime}=(k^{n-m})
,
\bigoplus_{\nu^{\prime}}M^{\lambda^{\prime\prime}}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}\mu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{m}_{\nu^{\prime\prime}}\otimes V^{n-m}_{\mu^{\prime\prime}}
is one-dimensional and it is generated by
\det(\eta)^{k}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Proposition 8.17
.

Let
\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N+n})
and
\mu=(\mu_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\mu_{N+n})
be Young diagrams of length
N+n
, and assume that
\lambda
is admissible and
\lambda\overset{m}{\searrow}\mu\overset{n-m}{\searrow}\lambda^{\shortdownarrow}
, then

\displaystyle p_{N}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right))\subseteq\begin{cases}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow}\\
\mu^{\natural\shortdownarrow}\end{subarray}\right)&,\text{ if $\lambda$ is cuttable},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(8.45)

Proof.

Let us assume that
\lambda
is cuttable, otherwise
p_{N}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right))\subseteq p_{N}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\lambda\right))=0
by Proposition
8.12
. In view of (
8.42
) and the definition of
p_{N}
(Definition
7.1
), Lemma
8.16
implies that

\displaystyle p_{N}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right))\subseteq\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime},(k^{n})}\neq 0\\
c^{\mu}_{\mu^{\prime},(k^{n-m})}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime\shortdownarrow}\\
\mu^{\prime\shortdownarrow}\end{subarray}\right),

where the
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime}\\
\mu^{\prime}\end{subarray}\right)
in Lemma
8.16
becomes
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\prime\shortdownarrow}\\
\mu^{\prime\shortdownarrow}\end{subarray}\right)
in the above because we need to multiply
\det(X)^{-k}
to get
p_{N}
. According to the proof of Proposition
8.12
, the only
\lambda^{\prime}
that makes
c^{\lambda}_{\lambda^{\prime},(k^{n})}
nonzero is
\lambda^{\prime}=\lambda^{\natural}
.

If
c^{\mu}_{\mu^{\prime},(k^{n-m})}\neq 0
, then
\mu^{\prime}\subseteq\mu
, in fact
\mu^{\prime}\subseteq\mu^{\natural}
because
\mu^{\prime}
has length
N
. Therefore we have

\displaystyle|\mu|-|\mu^{\prime}|-|(k^{n-m})|

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{i=N+1}^{N+n-m}\mu_{i}-k(n-m)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=N+1}^{N+n-m}(\mu_{i}-k)

by Lemma
8.14

\displaystyle=0.

The equality holds if and only if
\mu^{\prime}=\mu^{\natural}
. So we conclude that the unique
\mu^{\prime}
that makes
c^{\mu}_{\mu^{\prime},(k^{n-m})}
nonzero is
\mu^{\prime}=\mu^{\natural}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

8.2.3
Proof of Theorem
8.8

According to Remark
8.5
,
\prescript{L+1}{}{C}_{m}(u)
acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray})
as the scalar

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{2n+N-m}(u-\mu_{i}+i+m-2n-N).

(8.46)

By Lemma
8.14
,
\mu_{i}=0
for
i>n+N-m
, so (
8.46
) equals to

\displaystyle(u-n+1)_{n}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n+N-m}(u-\mu_{i}+i+m-2n-N).

(8.47)

According to Remark
8.5
,
\prescript{L}{}{C}_{m}(u)
acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow}\\
\mu^{\natural\shortdownarrow}\end{subarray}\right)
as the scalar

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{n+N-m}(u-\mu^{\natural\shortdownarrow}_{i}+i+m-n-N).

(8.48)

Compare (
8.47
) and (
8.48
), and we compute that

\displaystyle p_{N}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{C}_{m}(u)\bigg\rvert_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)}

\displaystyle=(u-n+1)_{n}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n+N-m}(u-\mu_{i}+i+m-2n-N)\cdot p_{N}\bigg\rvert_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)}

\displaystyle=(u-n+1)_{n}\cdot\prod_{i=1}^{n+N-m}((u-n-k)-\mu^{\natural\shortdownarrow}_{i}+i+m-n-N)\cdot p_{N}\bigg\rvert_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)}

by Proposition
8.17

\displaystyle=(u-n+1)_{n}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{C}_{m}(u-n-k)\circ p_{N}\bigg\rvert_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}\left(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray}\right)}.

In other words, the equation (
8.20
) holds when restricted to the subspace
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray})
for a cuttable
\lambda
. On the other hand, if
\lambda
is not cuttable, then according to Proposition
8.17

p_{N}
maps
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray})
to zero, thus the equation (
8.20
) trivially holds on the subspace
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray})
. Since
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)=\bigoplus_{\lambda,\mu}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\\
\mu\end{subarray})
, the equation (
8.20
) holds on the whole space
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
. This concludes the proof of Theorem
8.8
.

Definition 8.11
.

Fix
r\in\{1,\cdots,n-1\}
. Define the inverse limit of the system
\{\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
which acts on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
by
\prescript{\infty}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}
.

8.3
Conformal limit of operators with bounded degree

Definition 8.12
.

Let
V=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}V_{d}
be a non-negatively graded vector space. A linear operator
\mathcal{O}\in\End(V)
is said to be of bounded degree if there exists
C\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\forall d\in\mathbb{N}
,
\mathcal{O}(V_{d})\subset V_{\leq d+C}
, where

\displaystyle V_{\leq m}:=\bigoplus_{i\leq m}V_{i}.

It is easy to see that finite linear combinations and compositions of bounded degree linear operators are again bounded degree linear operators.

Definition 8.13
.

Fix
r\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
. Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\in\End(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k))\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators of uniformly bounded degree, i.e. there exists
C\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\forall d\in\mathbb{N}
and
\forall L\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d})\subset\bigoplus_{i\leq d+C}\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{i}.

We say that
\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}
exists if and only if there exists linear operator
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}\in\End(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k))
with bounded degree such that
\forall v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
the following holds:

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert=0.

(8.49)

Here
\lVert\cdot\rVert
is a norm on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
. If the above holds, we say that the conformal limit of
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
is
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}
, and write
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}
.

Remark 8.10
.

The above definition does not depend on the choice of norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert
. Suppose that
\lVert\cdot\rVert^{\prime}
is another norm on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
, then for every
d\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C_{d}>1
such that
\forall v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
, the following inequality holds:

\displaystyle C_{d}^{-1}\lVert v\rVert<\lVert v\rVert^{\prime}<C_{d}\lVert v\rVert.

This is because
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
is a finite dimensional vector space (Lemma
7.6
), and all norms on a finite dimensional vector space are equivalent. According to the assumption on the boundedness of degrees of
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}
, we have
\forall v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
, there exists
d\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\forall L\in\mathbb{N}
,
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
, therefore
