\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert=0\Longleftrightarrow\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert^{\prime}=0.

There is an equivalent definition of conformal limit, see the next lemma.

Lemma 8.18
.

Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\in\End(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k))\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators of uniformly bounded degree, and let
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}\in\End(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k))
be a linear operator with bounded degree, then

\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}
if and only if
\forall d\in\mathbb{N}
and
\forall\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
, there exists
M\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\right)(\mathbb{B}_{d})\subseteq\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{B}_{d+C}\text{ holds for all }L\geq M,

(8.50)

where
\mathbb{B}_{i}
is the unit ball in
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq i}
with respect to the norm
\lVert\cdot\rVert
, i.e.

\mathbb{B}_{i}=\{v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq i}:\lVert v\rVert\leq 1\}.

Proof.

The “if" direction is obvious, let us show the “only if" direction. Define
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}:=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\in\End(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k))
. By our assumption,
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}
has uniformly bounded degree, i.e.
\exists C\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d})\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d+C}
holds for all
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Moreover,
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}(v)\rVert=0
for all
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
. Now fix an arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d+C}
is represented by a matrix of finite size, then point-wise convergence
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}\rightarrow 0
implies that every matrix component converges zero, thus the matrix norm
\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}\rVert
defined by

\displaystyle\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}\rVert:=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\\
\rVert v\rVert=1\end{subarray}}\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}(v)\rVert,

converges to zero. This proves the (
8.50
).
∎

Here we list some elementary properties of conformal limit.

Lemma 8.19
.

Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators of uniformly bounded degree, then its conformal limit is unique if it exists.

Proof.

Suppose that there are two linear operator
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{1},\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}\in\End(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k))
of bounded degrees, such that
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}
and
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}
simultaneously holds. For arbitrary
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}(v)-\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}(v)\rVert

\displaystyle=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\right)-\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\right)\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert+\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert

\displaystyle=0.

Thus
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}(v)=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}(v)
. Since
v
is arbitrary, we conclude that
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{1}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}_{2}
.
∎

Lemma 8.20
.

Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators of homogeneous degree
d
, and assume that
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}
, then
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}
is homogeneous of degree
d
.

Proof.

For arbitrary
f\in\mathbb{N}
, let us take arbitrary
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{f}
, then by our assumption
\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{f+d}
for all
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Since a finite dimensional subspace in a normed
\mathbb{C}
-vector space is closed, the limit

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert=0

implies that
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}(v)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{f+d}
. This finishes the proof.
∎

Lemma 8.21
.

Suppose that
\{\alpha_{L}\in\mathbb{C}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of complex numbers such that
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\alpha_{L}=\alpha
, then

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}{\alpha_{L}\cdot\mathrm{Id}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)}=\alpha\cdot\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)}.

(8.51)

Proof.

Take arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}
for
L\gg 0
by Proposition
7.7
. For arbitrary
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
, we have

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\alpha v-\alpha_{L}\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\alpha v-\alpha_{L}v\rVert=0.

∎

Lemma 8.22
.

Suppose that
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
are two collections of uniformly bounded degree linear operators, and assume that
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}
and
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}
, where
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}
and
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}
are bounded degree linear operators. Then
\forall\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\left(\alpha\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}+\beta\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\right)=\alpha\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}+\beta\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}},\quad\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\left(\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\right)=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}},

(8.52)

Proof.

The first statement (linearity of limit) is straightforward, and we focus on the second statement. Let us fix
D\gg 0
so that it bounds the degrees of
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
,
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}
, and
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}
. Let
d\in\mathbb{N}
and
\epsilon\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
be arbitrary. Let us take
R\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
such that

\displaystyle(\star)\quad\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}(\mathbb{B}_{d})\subseteq R\cdot\mathbb{B}_{d+D},\text{ and }\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{B}_{d+D})\subseteq R\cdot\mathbb{B}_{d+2D}

simultaneously hold. Then we take
M\in\mathbb{N}
such that
\forall L\geq M
the following simultaneously hold:

(1)

\displaystyle p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d+D}}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d+D}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d+D}\;\text{ is isomorphism, and}

(2)

\displaystyle\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\right)(\mathbb{B}_{d})\subseteq\frac{\epsilon}{2R}\cdot\mathbb{B}_{d+D},\text{ and }

(3)

\displaystyle\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\right)(\mathbb{B}_{d+D})\subseteq\frac{\epsilon R}{2R^{2}+\epsilon}\cdot\mathbb{B}_{d+2D}.

M
exists due to Proposition
7.7
, and our assumption that
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}
and
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}
, and Lemma
8.18
. We then have

\displaystyle\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\right)(\mathbb{B}_{d})

by (1)

\displaystyle=\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\right)(\mathbb{B}_{d})

\displaystyle=\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r})+(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r})\cdot\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\right)(\mathbb{B}_{d})

\displaystyle\subseteq\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r})(\mathbb{B}_{d})

\displaystyle\quad+(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r})\circ\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\mathbb{B}_{d})

by
(\star)
+(2)

\displaystyle\subseteq\frac{\epsilon}{2R}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}(\mathbb{B}_{d+D})+(R+\frac{\epsilon}{2R})(\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r})(\mathbb{B}_{d+D})

by
(\star)
+(3)

\displaystyle\subseteq\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{B}_{d+2D}+\frac{\epsilon}{2}\mathbb{B}_{d+2D}

\displaystyle\subseteq\epsilon\cdot\mathbb{B}_{d+2D}.

This implies that
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\left(\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\right)=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}
.
∎

Our next result states that the algebraic inverse limit (for operators with bounded degree) is a special case of conformal limit.

Proposition 8.23
.

Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators of uniformly bounded degree, such that the following holds for all
L\in\mathbb{N}:

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p_{nL+r}=p_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{\mathcal{O}}.

Then
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}
exists and it equals to
\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}
, the inverse limit of
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Proof.

Let us denote
\mathcal{O}:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}
. By the definition of inverse limit, the equation
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}=p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ{\mathcal{O}}
holds for all
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
C
uniformly bounds the degrees of
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
. Take arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d+C}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d+C}}
for
L\gg 0
by Proposition
7.7
. For arbitrary
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
, we have

\displaystyle\quad\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\mathcal{O}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert

\displaystyle=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\mathcal{O}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert

\displaystyle=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ(p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\mathcal{O}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r})(v)\rVert

\displaystyle=0.

This finishes the proof.
∎

Corollary 8.24
.

Fix
r\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
. For every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, denote by
\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,m}(E^{a}_{b})
the operator acting on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
which is defined in (
5.5
). Then we have

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left(\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,m}(E^{a}_{b})-\delta_{m=0}\delta^{a}_{b}kL\cdot\mathrm{Id}\right)=J^{a}_{b,-m}.

(8.53)

Proof.

Since
J^{a}_{b,-m}
is by definition the inverse limit of the collection of maps
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,m}(E^{a}_{b})-\delta_{m=0}\delta^{a}_{b}kL\cdot\mathrm{Id}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
(see (
7.11
)), the result follows from Proposition
8.23
.
∎

Corollary 8.25
.

Fix
r\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
. For every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, let
\prescript{\infty}{}{T}^{a}_{b;m}
be the Yangian generators acting on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
which is defined in Definition
8.11
, then we have

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}=\prescript{\infty}{}{T}^{a}_{b;m}.

(8.54)

Proof.

Since
\prescript{\infty}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}
is the inverse limit of
\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}
(see (
8.4
) and Definition
8.11
), the result follows from Proposition
8.23
.
∎

To obtain more conformal limits, we shall use the following criterion.

Theorem 8.26
.

Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators with negative degree, i.e.
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d})\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{<d}
holds for all
L,d\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
\mathcal{O}
is a linear operator on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
with negative degree. Then
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}={\mathcal{O}}
if and only if the followings hold
\forall m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
and
\forall 1\leq b<a\leq n
and
\forall 1\leq c,d\leq n:

\begin{split}\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}},\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})]&=[{\mathcal{O}},J^{a}_{b,-i}],\quad\text{and}\\
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}},\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,1}(E^{c}_{d})]&=[{\mathcal{O}},J^{c}_{d,-1}],\quad\text{and}\\
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}},\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,m}]&=[{\mathcal{O}},\alpha_{-m}/k].\end{split}

(8.55)

Proof.

The “only if” part follows from Lemma
8.22
and Corollary
8.24
. By
7.10
,
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is a highest weight module thus it is generated from a highest weight vector
\omega\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{0}
by the actions of
\{J^{a}_{b,0}\>|\>1\leq b<a\leq n\}\cup\{J^{c}_{d,-m}\>|\>1\leq c,d\leq n,\;m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 1}\}
.
\bar{J}_{<-1}
can be obtained from taking iterative commutators between
\bar{J}_{-1}
, therefore
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
is generated from
\omega
by the actions of operators in the set

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}=\{J^{a}_{b,0}\>|\>1\leq b<a\leq n\}\cup\{J^{c}_{d,-1}\>|\>1\leq c,d\leq n\}\cup\{\alpha_{-m}\>|\>m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}\}

We note that every operator
\mathcal{A}\in\mathcal{G}
is an inverse limit, i.e.
\mathcal{A}=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}
. The condition (
8.55
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}},\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}]

\displaystyle=[{\mathcal{O}},\mathcal{A}],\quad\forall\mathcal{A}\in\mathcal{G}.

(8.56)

Suppose that (
8.56
) holds, then we shall prove that
\forall v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
,
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert{\mathcal{O}}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert=0
. Since
v
is a linear combination of operators of form
\mathcal{A}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(\omega)
and
\mathcal{A}_{i}\in\mathcal{G}
, we can prove the statement for
\mathcal{A}_{1}\cdots\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(\omega)
and the statement for
v
follows from triangle inequality. We proceed by induction on
\ell
. When
\ell=0
, the statement is automatic because
\mathcal{O}(\omega)=0
and
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)
by our assumption that
\mathcal{O}
and
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}
have negative degree. Suppose that the statement has been proven for
v^{\prime}=\mathcal{A}_{2}\cdots\mathcal{A}_{\ell}(\omega)
, then we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert{\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{A}_{1}(v^{\prime}))-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\mathcal{A}_{1}(v^{\prime}))\rVert\\
&=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert{\mathcal{O}}\circ\mathcal{A}_{1}(v^{\prime})-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v^{\prime})\rVert\\
\text{\small by triangle inequality}\;&\leq\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert[{\mathcal{O}},\mathcal{A}_{1}](v^{\prime})-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}},\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}]\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v^{\prime})\rVert\\
&\quad+\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\mathcal{A}_{1}\circ{\mathcal{O}}(v^{\prime})-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v^{\prime})\rVert\\
\text{\small by \eqref{eq:recursion 2}}\;&=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\mathcal{A}_{1}\circ{\mathcal{O}}(v^{\prime})-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v^{\prime})\rVert\\
\text{(!)}\;&=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\lVert\mathcal{A}_{1}\circ{\mathcal{O}}(v^{\prime})-\mathcal{A}_{1}\circ\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v^{\prime})\rVert\\
\text{\small by induction and continuity of $\mathcal{A}_{1}$}\;&=0.\end{split}

(8.57)

Here in the step labelled by (!), we apply the Proposition
7.7
. Namely for
L\gg 0
and fixed
n,k,d
,

\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}=\mathrm{Id},

which implies that
\mathcal{A}_{1}\circ\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}}=\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}_{1}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}}
for
L\gg 0
. Then we take
d
such that
v^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
. This finishes the induction step and therefore concludes the proof.
∎

8.3.1
Order of error terms

Definition 8.14
.

Fix
r\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
and
d\in\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\in\End(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k))\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators of uniformly bounded degree. Let
h>0
, then we say that
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}
converges to
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}
with error term of order
O(L^{-h})
, notation:

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}},

if for all
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
there exists constant
C_{v}>0
such that

\displaystyle\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert\leq C_{v}L^{-h}\text{ holds for all }L.

(8.58)

Here
\rVert\cdot\rVert
is a norm on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
.

Similar argument as in the Remark
8.10
shows that the definition does not depend on the choice of the norm
\rVert\cdot\rVert
. In the below we list some properties which are counterparts of the corresponding statements in the above.

Lemma 8.27

(cf. Lemma
8.21
)
.

Suppose that
\{\alpha_{L}\in\mathbb{C}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of complex numbers, and assume there exists
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
and
C>0
and
h>0
such that
|\alpha_{L}-\alpha|\leq CL^{-h}
holds for all
L
, then

\displaystyle{\alpha_{L}\cdot\mathrm{Id}}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}\alpha\cdot\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)}.

(8.59)

Proof.

Take arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{N}
, then there exists
M>0
such that
\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}
for all
L\geq M
by Proposition
7.7
. For arbitrary
v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
and
L\geq M
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\alpha v-\alpha_{L}\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert=|\alpha_{L}-\alpha|\lVert v\rVert\leq C\lVert v\rVert L^{-h}.

So we can take
C_{v}=\max(\max_{L\leq M}(L^{h}\lVert\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}(v)-\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert),C\lVert v\rVert)
.
∎

Lemma 8.28

(cf. Lemma
8.22
)
.

Suppose that
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
are two collections of uniformly bounded degree linear operators, and assume that
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}
and
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h^{\prime}})}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}
, where
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}
and
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}}
are bounded degree linear operators. Then
\forall\alpha,\beta\in\mathbb{C}
,

\displaystyle\alpha\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}+\beta\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h^{\prime\prime}})}\alpha\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}+\beta\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}},\quad\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{B}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h^{\prime\prime}})}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{A}}\cdot\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{B}},

(8.60)

where
h^{\prime\prime}=\min(h,h^{\prime})
.

Proof.

In the proof of Lemma
8.22
, we replace
\epsilon
by
CL^{-h^{\prime\prime}}
for some
C>0
, the rest remains the same.
∎

Lemma 8.29

(cf. Proposition
8.23
)
.

Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators of uniformly bounded degree, such that the following holds for all
L\in\mathbb{N}:

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p_{nL+r}=p_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L+1}{}{\mathcal{O}}.

Then for all
h>0
, we have
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}
.

Proof.

Let us denote
\mathcal{O}:=\underset{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
L\end{subarray}}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}
. By the definition of inverse limit, the equation
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}=p^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ{\mathcal{O}}
holds for all
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose that
C
uniformly bounds the degrees of
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
. Take arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d+C}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d+C}}
for
L\gg 0
by Proposition
7.7
. Then
\mathcal{O}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d+C}}=\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d+C}}
for
L\gg 0
, whence the lemma follows.
∎

Lemma 8.30

(cf. Theorem
8.26
)
.

Let
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
be a collection of linear operators with negative degree, i.e.
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d})\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{<d}
holds for all
L,d\in\mathbb{N}
. Suppose there exists
h>0
and a linear operator
\mathcal{O}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
with negative degree such that the followings hold
\forall m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
and
\forall 1\leq b<a\leq n
and
\forall 1\leq c,d\leq n:

\begin{split}[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}},\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})]&\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}[{\mathcal{O}},J^{a}_{b,-i}],\quad\text{and}\\
[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}},\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,1}(E^{c}_{d})]&\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}[{\mathcal{O}},J^{c}_{d,-1}],\quad\text{and}\\
[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}},\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,m}]&\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}[{\mathcal{O}},\alpha_{-m}/k].\end{split}

(8.61)

Then
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}{\mathcal{O}}
.

Proof.

We modify the proof of Theorem
8.26
as follows. We notice that in the limit (
8.56
) the error terms are of order
O(L^{-h})
, whence in the line marked with “by (
8.56
)" in (
8.57
), the error terms are of order
O(L^{-h})
. In the last line of (
8.57
) we use the induction and continuity of
\mathcal{A}_{1}
to bound the error term by the order
O(L^{-h})
. The rest of (
8.57
) uses the fact that
\sigma^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ p^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}=\mathrm{Id}
for
L\gg 0
and fixed
n,k,d
, so the error term vanishes for
L\gg 0
. In total, the error term of the convergence
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}{\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\rightarrow}{\mathcal{O}}
is bounded by order
O(L^{-h})
.
∎

8.4
Emergent
\widehat{\gl}(n)
annihilation operators from conformal limit

Theorem 8.31
.

Fix
r\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
. For every
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{>0}
, denote by
\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{m,0}(E^{a}_{b})
the operator acting on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
which is defined in (
5.5
). Then we have

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\frac{1}{(k+n)^{m}L^{m}}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{m,0}(E^{a}_{b})=J^{a}_{b,m}-\frac{\delta^{a}_{b}}{k+n}\alpha_{m},

(8.62)

where
J^{a}_{b,m}
and
\alpha_{m}
are defined in (
8.3
). Moreover the error terms of the convergence (
8.62
) are of order
O(L^{-1})
.

Theorem 8.32
.

Fix
r\in\{0,\cdots,n-1\}
. Denote by
\mathcal{T}(z)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{L}_{m}z^{-m-2}
the Sugawara’s stress-operator of affine vertex algebra associated to
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}\oplus\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0}\right]=-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1},

(8.63)

where
C=-\frac{k(n+2r)}{n(n+k)}
. Moreover the error terms of the convergence (
8.63
) are of order
O(L^{-1})
.

Remark 8.11
.

Individual limits
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}
or
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0}
do not exist.

Our strategy to prove Theorem
8.31
and Theorem
8.32
is to first prove the
m=1
case in (
8.62
), then prove Theorem
8.32
, next we take the adjoint action of stress-operator (
8.63
) on the
m=1
case in (
8.62
) to get
m>1
cases.

We begin with three technical lemma which are crucial in the proof of Theorem
8.32
.

Lemma 8.33
.

For all
m\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 2}
, we have

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,m}(E^{a}_{b})-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,m-1}(E^{a}_{b})\right]=0,

(8.64)

with error terms of order
O(L^{-1})
.

Proof.

Taking the iterated adjoint action of
\frac{1}{k}\alpha_{-1}=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,1}
on two sides of (
8.54
), we get

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\frac{1}{m!}\mathrm{ad}^{m-1}_{\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,1}}\left(\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}\right)=\frac{1}{m!}\mathrm{ad}^{m-1}_{\alpha_{-1}/k}\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}\right),

(8.65)

with error terms of order
O(L^{-h})
for arbitrary
h>0
by Lemma
8.28
and Lemma
8.29
. Let us expand the left-hand-side (before taking
L\rightarrow\infty
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m!}\mathrm{ad}^{m-1}_{\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,1}}\left(\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}\right)=\prescript{L}{}{\left[A^{a}X\cdot\mathrm{Sym}(YX^{m-1})B_{b}\right]}-\left((k+n)L+\frac{kL}{m}\right)\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,m-1}(E^{a}_{b}).

Let us compute the difference between
A^{a}X\cdot\mathrm{Sym}(YX^{m-1})B_{b}
and
\mathsf{T}_{1,m}(E^{a}_{b})
:

\displaystyle A^{a}X\cdot\mathrm{Sym}(YX^{m-1})B_{b}-A^{a}\mathrm{Sym}(YX^{m})B_{b}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}\frac{m-i}{m(m+1)}A^{a}X^{i}[X,Y]X^{m-1-i}B_{b}

by (
C.3
)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}\frac{m-i}{m(m+1)}\left[\mathsf{T}_{0,i}(E^{a}_{c})\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1-i}(E^{c}_{b})-(k+n)\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1}(E^{a}_{b})\right]

\displaystyle=-\frac{k+n}{2}\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1}(E^{a}_{b})+\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}\frac{m-i}{m(m+1)}\mathsf{T}_{0,i}(E^{a}_{c})\mathsf{T}_{0,m-1-i}(E^{c}_{b}).

Therefore (
8.65
) is equivalent to

\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,m}(E^{a}_{b})-\left((k+n)L+\frac{k+n}{2}\right)\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,m-1}(E^{a}_{b})\right]\\
+\sum_{i=0}^{m-1}\frac{m-i}{m(m+1)}{J}^{a}_{c,-i}{J}^{c}_{b,i+1-m}=\frac{1}{m!}\mathrm{ad}^{m-1}_{\alpha_{-1}/k}\left(\prescript{\infty}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;m}\right).

(8.66)

Dividing two sides by
(k+n)L
and applying Lemma
8.21
, Lemma
8.22
, and Proposition
8.23
, we get

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,m}(E^{a}_{b})-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,m-1}(E^{a}_{b})\right]=0.

The error terms of the above convergence are of order
O(L^{-1})
by Lemma
8.28
.
∎

Lemma 8.34
.

For an operator with adjoint
\gl_{n}
indices
\mathcal{O}^{a}_{b}
, denote by
\left(\mathcal{O}^{a}_{b}\right)_{0}=\mathcal{O}^{a}_{b}-\frac{\delta^{a}_{b}}{n}\mathcal{O}^{c}_{c}
the traceless part of
\mathcal{O}^{a}_{b}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,1}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}\right]=0,

(8.67)

with error terms of order
O(L^{-1})
.

Proof.

Expanding (
8.4
) and extracting the traceless part of
u^{-2}
term in (
8.4
), we get

\displaystyle\left(\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;1}\right)_{0}=\prescript{L}{}{\left[A^{a}XYB_{b}\right]}_{0}-(2k+n)L\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}.

(8.68)

Using (
C.3
), we get

\displaystyle A^{a}XYB_{b}-A^{a}\mathrm{Sym}(YX)B_{b}=-\frac{k+n}{2}\mathsf{T}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})+\frac{1}{2}\mathsf{T}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{c})\mathsf{T}_{0,0}(E^{c}_{b}).

Plug the above into (
8.68
) and we get

\begin{split}\left(\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;1}\right)_{0}&=\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,1}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}-\left((2k+n)L+\frac{k+n}{2}\right)\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{c})\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{c}_{b})\right)_{0}\\
&=\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,1}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}-\left((k+n)L+\frac{k+n}{2}\right)\cdot(\prescript{L}{}{J}^{a}_{b,0})_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\left(\prescript{L}{}{J}^{a}_{c,0}\prescript{L}{}{J}^{c}_{b,0}\right)_{0}\end{split}

(8.69)

Dividing two sides by
(k+n)L
and applying Lemma
8.21
, Lemma
8.22
, and Proposition
8.23
, we get

\displaystyle 0=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\left(\prescript{L}{}{\widetilde{T}}^{a}_{b;1}\right)_{0}

\displaystyle=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,1}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}-\left(1+\frac{1}{2L}\right)(\prescript{L}{}{J}^{a}_{b,0})_{0}\right]

\displaystyle\quad+\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\frac{1}{2(k+n)L}\left(\prescript{L}{}{J}^{a}_{c,0}\prescript{L}{}{J}^{c}_{b,0}\right)_{0}

\displaystyle=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,1}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}-(\prescript{L}{}{J}^{a}_{b,0})_{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,1}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}\right].

The error terms of the above convergence are of order
O(L^{-1})
by Lemma
8.28
.
∎

Lemma 8.35
.

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\left[\frac{2}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,0}\right]=\frac{rn-k(n+r)}{n+k},

(8.70)

with error terms of order
O(L^{-1})
.

Proof.

We notice that

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,1}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d}}=d+\frac{knL(L-1)}{2}+knr+\frac{(nL+r)^{2}}{2},\quad\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,0}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)}=nL+r.

Then the result follows from direct computation.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8.32
.

Since both
\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0}
and
-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1}
have negative degree, we can apply Theorem
8.26
to show the convergence and apply Lemma
8.30
to bound the order of error terms. Namely, it is enough to show that

\begin{split}&\left[\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1},\;J^{a}_{b,0}],\\
&\left[\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,1}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1},\;(J^{a}_{b,-1})_{0}],\\
&\left[\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,1}\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1},\;\alpha_{-1}/k],\\
&\left[\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,m}\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1},\;\alpha_{-m}/k]\quad(m\geq 2).\end{split}

(8.71)

The 1st line of (
8.71
) holds because
\left[\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{b})\right]=0
and
[-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1},\;J^{a}_{b,0}]=0
. The 2nd line of (
8.71
) follows from Lemma
8.34
. The 3rd line of (
8.71
) follows from Lemma
8.35
. The 4th line of (
8.71
) follows from Lemma
8.33
. This concludes the proof.
∎

Proof of Theorem
8.31
.

We begin with the
m=1,E^{a}_{b}=\mathrm{Id}
case in (
8.62
), which is equivalent to proving
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\>\frac{1}{(k+n)^{m}L^{m}}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0}=\frac{1}{k+n}\alpha_{1}
. Since both
\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0}
and
\frac{1}{k+n}\alpha_{1}
have negative degree, we can apply Theorem
8.26
to show the convergence and apply Lemma
8.30
to bound the order of error terms. Namely, it suffices to show that

\begin{split}&\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{c}_{d})\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[\frac{1}{k+n}\alpha_{1},\;J^{c}_{d,0}]\quad(1\leq d<c\leq n),\\
&\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,1}(E^{e}_{f})\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[\frac{1}{k+n}\alpha_{1},\;J^{e}_{f,-1}]\quad(1\leq e,f\leq n),\\
&\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,\ell}\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[\frac{1}{k+n}\alpha_{1},\;\alpha_{-\ell}/k]\quad(\ell\geq 2).\end{split}

(8.72)

The 1st line of (
8.72
) holds because
[\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0},\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{c}_{d})]=0
and
[\alpha_{1},J^{c}_{d,0}]=0
. The left-hand-side of the 2nd line of (
8.72
) equals to
\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{e}_{f})
, which converges to
\frac{k}{k+n}\delta^{e}_{f}=[\frac{1}{k+n}\alpha_{1},\;J^{e}_{f,-1}]
with error terms of order
O(L^{-1})
. The left-hand-side of the 3rd line of (
8.72
) equals to
\frac{\ell}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,\ell-1}
, which converges to
0
with error terms of order
O(L^{-1})
. The right-hand-side of the 3rd line of (
8.72
) is constantly zero.

Next we prove the case of
m=1
and
E^{a}_{b}=E^{n}_{1}
. By Theorem
8.26
, it s enough to show that

\begin{split}&\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,0}(E^{n}_{1}),\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{c}_{d})\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[J^{n}_{1,1},\;J^{c}_{d,0}]\quad(1\leq d<c\leq n),\\
&\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,0}(E^{n}_{1}),\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,1}(E^{e}_{f})\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[J^{n}_{1,1},\;J^{e}_{f,-1}]\quad(1\leq e,f\leq n),\\
&\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,0}(E^{n}_{1}),\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{0,\ell}\right]\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}[J^{n}_{1,1},\;\alpha_{-\ell}/k]\quad(\ell\geq 2).\end{split}

(8.73)

The 1st line of (
8.73
) holds because
[\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,0}(E^{n}_{1}),\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{c}_{d})]=0
and
[J^{n}_{1,1},J^{c}_{d,0}]=0
when
c>d
. To prove the 2nd line of (
8.73
), we notice that

\displaystyle[{\mathsf{T}}_{1,0}(E^{n}_{1}),\;{\mathsf{T}}_{0,1}(E^{e}_{f})]=

\displaystyle{\mathsf{T}}_{1,1}([E^{n}_{1},E^{e}_{f}])+\frac{n+k}{2}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(\{E^{n}_{1},E^{e}_{f}\})+{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{n}_{f}){\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{e}_{1})

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\delta^{n}_{f}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{e}_{g}){\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{g}_{1})-\frac{1}{2}\delta^{e}_{1}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{n}_{g}){\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{g}_{f}),

therefore the left-hand-side converges to
\delta^{e}_{1}J^{n}_{f,0}-\delta^{n}_{f}J^{e}_{1,0}+k\delta^{n}_{1}\delta^{e}_{f}
with error terms of order
O(L^{-1})
by Lemma
8.34
.
\delta^{e}_{1}J^{n}_{f,0}-\delta^{n}_{f}J^{e}_{1,0}+k\delta^{n}_{1}\delta^{e}_{f}
equals to the right-hand-side of the 2nd line of (
8.73
). The left-hand-side of the 3rd line of (
8.73
) equals to
\frac{\ell}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,\ell-1}(E^{n}_{1})
which converges to
0
with error terms of order
O(L^{-1})
, and the right-hand-side of the 3rd line of (
8.73
) is constantly zero.

Next we prove the case of
m=1
and general traceless
E^{a}_{b}
. We notice that the adjoint action of
\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(\mathfrak{sl}_{n})
on
\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{n}_{1})
spans the whole vector space
\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(\mathfrak{sl}_{n})
. Thus the result follows from the
E^{a}_{b}=E^{n}_{1}
case and Lemma
8.22
and Corollary
8.24
. We use Lemma
8.28
to bound the order of error terms by
O(L^{-1})
. This finishes the proof of the case of
m=1
and arbitrary
E^{a}_{b}
.

We proceed to the
m>1
cases using Theorem
8.32
and Lemma
8.30
. We notice that

\frac{1}{(k+n)^{m}L^{m}}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{m,0}(E^{a}_{b})\\
=\frac{1}{(m-1)!}\underbrace{\left[\cdots\left[\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{1,0}(E^{a}_{b}),\;\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0}\right],\cdots,\;\frac{1}{(k+n)L}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{2,1}-\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{t}}_{1,0}\right]}_{m-1\text{ times}}

Then according to the
m=1
case that we just proved, and Theorem
8.32
, and Lemma
8.28
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(k+n)^{m}L^{m}}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{m,0}(E^{a}_{b})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}\frac{1}{(m-1)!}\underbrace{\left[\cdots\left[J^{a}_{b,1}-\frac{\delta^{a}_{b}}{k+n}\alpha_{1},\;-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1}\right],\cdots,-\mathcal{L}_{1}+C\cdot\alpha_{1}\right]}_{m-1\text{ times}}

\displaystyle=J^{a}_{b,m}-\frac{\delta^{a}_{b}}{k+n}\alpha_{m}.

This concludes the proof of Theorem
8.31
.
∎

8.5
Comparison with Dorey-Tong-Turner

A different scaling limit was used in the original work of Dorey, Tong, and Turner
[
21
]
, where they consider the following operators

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m}=\left(\frac{n}{(n+k)N}\right)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}\begin{cases}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{m,0}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}&,\text{ if }m\geq 0,\\
\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,-m}(E^{a}_{b})_{0}&,\text{ if }m<0.\end{cases}

(8.74)

To relate their scaling to our result (Theorem
8.31
), we make the following observation.

Consider the linear automorphism
\Phi_{N}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
such that

\displaystyle\Phi_{N}\bigg\rvert_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}}=\left(\frac{n}{(n+k)N}\right)^{d/2}\cdot\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)_{d}}.

(8.75)

Define

\displaystyle\overline{p}_{N}:=\Phi_{N}^{-1}\circ p_{N}\circ\Phi_{N+n}:\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N+n}(n,k)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k).

(8.76)

Then
\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k),\overline{p}_{nL+r}\right\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
forms an inverse system, which is isomorphic to the inverse system

\left\{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k),{p}_{nL+r}\right\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
via the compatible family of isomorphisms
\{\Phi_{nL+r}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
. We use
\{\overline{p}_{nL+r}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
to define the inverse limit

\displaystyle\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)=\bigoplus_{d\geq 0}\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d},\;\text{where }\;\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{d}:=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}\leftarrow\mathrel{\mkern-3.0mu}\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\meno}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\meno}$}}}\\
L\end{subarray}}\>\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{d}.

(8.77)

By construction, we have isomorphism:

\displaystyle\Phi_{\infty}:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)\cong\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k),\;\text{where $\Phi_{\infty}$ is the limit of }\{\Phi_{nL+r}\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}.

(8.78)

The formalism of conformal limit of operators in Section
8.3
works for
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
. Apparently we have

\displaystyle\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}=\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}\text{ in }\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)\>\Longleftrightarrow\>\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\Phi_{nL+r}^{-1}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\>\Phi_{nL+r}=\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}\>\Phi_{\infty}\text{ in }\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k).

(8.79)

Moreover the error terms on two sides are of the same order, i.e.

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}\text{ in }\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)\>\Longleftrightarrow\>\Phi_{nL+r}^{-1}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\>\Phi_{nL+r}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{O}}\>\Phi_{\infty}\text{ in }\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k).

(8.80)

It is straightforward to compute that

\displaystyle\Phi_{N}^{-1}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{m,m^{\prime}}(E^{a}_{b})\>\Phi_{N}=\left(\frac{n}{(n+k)N}\right)^{\frac{m^{\prime}-m}{2}}\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{m,m^{\prime}}(E^{a}_{b}).

(8.81)

Thus we have the following corollary to the Theorem
8.31
.

Corollary 8.36
.

In
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
, we have

\displaystyle\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m}=\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\bar{J}^{a}_{b,m}\Phi_{\infty},\quad\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{m}=\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\alpha_{m}\Phi_{\infty},\quad(m\in\mathbb{Z}),

(8.82)

where
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m}
is defined in (
8.74
) and
\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{m}
is defined as

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{m}=\left(\frac{n}{(n+k)N}\right)^{\frac{|m|}{2}}\begin{cases}(1+\frac{n}{k})\cdot\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{m,0}(E^{a}_{a})&,\text{ if }m>0,\\
\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,0}(E^{a}_{a})-nkL\cdot\mathrm{Id}&,\text{ if }m=0,\\
\prescript{L}{}{\mathsf{T}}_{0,-m}(E^{a}_{a})&,\text{ if }m<0.\end{cases}

(8.83)

Moreover the error terms of the convergence (
8.82
) are of order
O(L^{-1})
. In particular the limit
\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m}=\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\bar{J}^{a}_{b,m}\Phi_{\infty}
and
\prescript{\infty}{}{\beta}_{m}=\Phi_{\infty}^{-1}\alpha_{m}\Phi_{\infty}
satisfy
\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}\oplus\widehat{\mathfrak{gl}}(1)_{kn}
commutation relation:

\displaystyle\begin{split}&[\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m},\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{c}_{d,l}]=\delta^{c}_{b}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{d,m+l}-\delta^{a}_{d}\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{c}_{b,m+l}+km\delta_{m+l,0}\left(\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{b}_{c}-\frac{1}{n}\delta^{a}_{b}\delta^{c}_{d}\right),\\
&[\prescript{\infty}{}{\beta}_{m},\prescript{\infty}{}{\beta}_{l}]=knm\delta_{m+l,0},\\
&[\prescript{\infty}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m},\prescript{\infty}{}{\beta}_{l}]=0.\end{split}

(8.84)

The conjecture by Dorey, Tong, and Turner in
[
21
]
is slightly different from our Corollary
8.36
. They did not define the conformal limit of Hilbert space, instead they stated the expected convergence property as a bound of the error terms of (
8.84
) for finite
L
.

Definition 8.15
.

For an
h>0
, we say a sequence of operators
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\in\End(\widetilde{H}_{nL+r}(n,k))\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
is of order
O(L^{-h})
if for every
d\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C_{d}>0
such that he operator norm of
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}}
is bounded by
C_{d}L^{-h}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}(v)\rVert_{L}\leq C_{d}L^{-h}\rVert v\rVert_{L}\text{ holds for all }v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}.

(8.85)

Here
\rVert\cdot\rVert_{L}
is the norm on the Hilbert space
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
induced by the Hermitian inner product (see Section
4.1
).

Theorem 8.37

(Conjectured in
[
21
, (2.6)]
, see also (
1.3
))
.

The operators

\displaystyle[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m},\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{c}_{d,l}]-\delta^{c}_{b}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{d,m+l}+\delta^{a}_{d}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{c}_{b,m+l}-km\delta_{m+l,0}\left(\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{b}_{c}-\frac{1}{n}\delta^{a}_{b}\delta^{c}_{d}\right).

has order
O(L^{-1})
.

Proof.

Corollary
8.36
implies that

\displaystyle[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m},\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{c}_{d,l}]-\delta^{c}_{b}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{d,m+l}+\delta^{a}_{d}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{c}_{b,m+l}-km\delta_{m+l,0}\left(\delta^{a}_{d}\delta^{b}_{c}-\frac{1}{n}\delta^{a}_{b}\delta^{c}_{d}\right)\xrightarrow{O(L^{-1})}0,

then the Theorem
8.37
follows from Proposition
8.38
below.
∎

Remark 8.12
.

The same argument shows that the operators
[\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{m},\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{l}]-knm\delta_{m+l,0}
and
[\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,m},\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{l}]
have order
O(L^{-1})
. This implies conjecture (
1.6
) as

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{m}=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{n+k}\beta_{m}&,\text{ if }m>0,\\
\frac{1}{k}\beta_{m}&,\text{ if }m<0,\\
\end{cases}\quad\text{ and $\mathcal{B}_{0}$ is central.}

Proposition 8.38
.

For a sequence of operators
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\in\End(\widetilde{H}_{nL+r}(n,k))\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
with uniformly bounded degree, we have

\displaystyle\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}0\in\End(\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty})\;\Longleftrightarrow\;\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\text{ has order }O(L^{-h}).

(8.86)

To prove Proposition
8.38
, we need to compare the norm
\rVert\cdot\rVert_{L}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
with a norm on
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
. There is a natural choice of norm on
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
. Namely, let
(\cdot|\cdot)
be the unique
\widehat{\gl}(n)
-invariant Hermitian form on the irreducible integrable module
L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
which is normalized by requiring

\displaystyle(\omega|\omega)=1,\text{ where $\omega$ is the highest weight vector}.

(8.87)

The
\widehat{\gl}(n)
-invariance means the following equation

\displaystyle(v|J^{a}_{b,m}v^{\prime})=(J^{b}_{a,-m}v|v^{\prime})

(8.88)

holds
\forall v,v^{\prime}\in L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
and
\forall 1\leq a,b\leq n
and
\forall m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Let
\rVert\cdot\rVert
be the norm on
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
induced by
(\cdot|\cdot)
via the isomorphism
\Phi_{\infty}:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)\cong L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
.

Lemma 8.39
.

For arbitrary
d\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
B_{d}>1
such that

\displaystyle B_{d}^{-1}\rVert v\rVert\leq\frac{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert_{L}}\leq B_{d}\rVert v\rVert\text{ holds for all $L$ and all }v\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}.

(8.89)

Proof.

We prove the following more precise statement that will lead to the lemma. For
i=1,2
, let
v_{i}=v_{i}^{\prime}\otimes v_{i}^{\prime\prime}
where

v_{i}^{\prime}\in L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}),\quad v_{i}^{\prime\prime}=\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{i}}(\alpha_{-j})^{m_{i,j}}|0\rangle\in\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}),

then we have

\displaystyle\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{1})|\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{2}))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}=\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}m_{1,j}}(v_{1}|v_{2}).

(8.90)

Here
(\cdot|\cdot)_{L}
is the Hermitian inner product on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
. And we have identified
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
with
L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
using isomorphism
\Phi_{\infty}
.

As a preliminary step, we claim that

\displaystyle\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)|\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}=(\omega|v)\quad\text{for all $v$ and all $L$}.

(8.91)

To prove (
8.91
), we notice that
\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{0}}:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{0}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{0}
is an isomorphism between irreducible
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
modules. Moreover, the inner products
(\cdot|\cdot)
and
(\cdot|\cdot)_{L}
are
\mathfrak{sl}_{n}
-invariant, whence by the Schur lemma there exists a constant
\lambda_{L}\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
for every
L
such that

\displaystyle(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)|\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v))_{L}=\lambda_{L}(\omega|u),\quad\forall u\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{0}.

(8.92)

Write
v=v_{0}+v_{>0}
where
v_{0}\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{0}
and
v_{>0}\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{>0}
, then we have

\displaystyle\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)|\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}=\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)|\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{0}))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}=\frac{\lambda_{L}(\omega|v_{0})}{\lambda_{L}(\omega|\omega)}=(\omega|v_{0})=(\omega|v).

(8.93)

This proves (
8.91
).

Next, we claim that if
\{\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\in\End(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k))\}_{L\in\mathbb{N}}
is a sequence of bounded degree operators such that
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}=\mathcal{O}
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)|\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}=(\omega|\mathcal{O}(v))\quad\text{for all $v$}.

(8.94)

Fix
v
, and let
d
be a natural number such that
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}
for all
L
. Then by Proposition
7.7
there exists
M
such that
\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}}
for all
L\geq M
. Then it follows that

\displaystyle\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)|\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}

\displaystyle=\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)|\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}

by (
8.91
)

\displaystyle=\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}(\omega|\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v))

since
\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{O}}=\mathcal{O}

\displaystyle=(\omega|\mathcal{O}(v)).

This proves (
8.94
).

Next we apply (
8.94
) to deduce (
8.90
). Let us write
v^{\prime}_{i}=f_{i}(\mathcal{G})\ket{\omega}
where
f_{i}\in\mathbb{C}\langle\mathcal{G}\rangle
are non-commutative polynomials in the set of variables
\mathcal{G}=\{\bar{J}^{a}_{b,-m}\>|\>1\leq b,a\leq n,m\in\mathbb{N}\}
. We denote
f_{i}^{\mathrm{op}}(\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}})
to be the polynomial in variables
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{op}}=\{\bar{J}^{b}_{a,m}\>|\>1\leq b,a\leq n,m\in\mathbb{N}\}
obtained from
f_{i}(\mathcal{G})
by reversing the order of all monomials inside
f_{i}
then followed by replacements
\bar{J}^{a}_{b,-m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\bar{J}^{b}_{a,m}
. We also define
f_{i}(\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{G}})
by substituting
\bar{J}^{a}_{b,-m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{a}_{b,-m}
. Similarly,
f_{i}^{\mathrm{op}}(\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{op}})
is defined by substituting
\bar{J}^{b}_{a,m}\mapstochar\rightarrow\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{J}}^{b}_{a,m}
. Then we can rewrite

\displaystyle\begin{split}(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{1})|\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{2}))_{L}&=\left(f_{1}(\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{G}})\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}(\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{-j})^{m_{1,j}}\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\;\bigg\rvert\;\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{2})\right)_{L}\\
&=\left(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\;\bigg\rvert\;f_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}(\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{op}})^{*}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{j}\right)^{m_{1,j}}\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{2})\right)_{L}.\end{split}

(8.95)

Here the superscript
*
on the function
f_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}(\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{op}})
means complex conjugation of all coefficients. By Corollary
8.36
we have:

\displaystyle\underset{L\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}f_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}(\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{op}})^{*}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\prescript{L}{}{\beta}_{j}\right)^{m_{1,j}}=\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}m_{1,j}}f_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}({\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{op}})^{*}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}(\alpha_{j})^{m_{1,j}},

(8.96)

we note that the conjugation by
\Phi_{\infty}
has been absorbed into the operators
\bar{J}^{a}_{b,m}
and
\alpha_{m}
since we have made identification
\Phi_{\infty}:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)\cong L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
. Then (
8.94
) implies that

\displaystyle\begin{split}\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{1})|\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v_{2}))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}&=\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}m_{1,j}}\left(\omega\;\bigg\rvert\;f_{1}^{\mathrm{op}}({\mathcal{G}}^{\mathrm{op}})^{*}\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}({\alpha}_{j})^{m_{1,j}}v_{2}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}m_{1,j}}\left(f_{1}({\mathcal{G}})\prod_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}({\alpha}_{-j})^{m_{1,j}}\omega\;\bigg\rvert\;v_{2}\right)\\
&=\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{\sum_{j=1}^{\ell_{1}}m_{1,j}}(v_{1}|v_{2})\\
\end{split}

(8.97)

This concludes the proof of (
8.90
).

Finally, we demonstrate how to derive the original lemma from (
8.90
). Every
v\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
can be uniquely written as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&v=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\vec{m}=(m_{1},m_{2},\cdots)\in\mathbb{N}^{\infty}\\
\sum_{j}j\cdot m_{j}\leq d\end{subarray}}v_{\vec{m}},\quad v_{\vec{m}}=v^{\prime}_{\vec{m}}\otimes\ket{\vec{m}},\quad\text{ where }\\
&v^{\prime}_{\vec{m}}\in L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k}),\quad\ket{\vec{m}}=\prod_{j}(\alpha_{-j})^{m_{j}}|0\rangle\in\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn}).\end{split}

(8.98)

Then (
8.90
) implies that

\displaystyle\lim_{L\rightarrow\infty}\frac{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v)\rVert^{2}_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}=\sum_{\vec{m}}\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{\sum_{j}m_{j}}\rVert v_{\vec{m}}\rVert^{2}.

(8.99)

By the polarization identity, the quadratic forms
\phi_{L}:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\times\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
defined by

\displaystyle\phi_{L}(u,v):=\frac{(\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\>|\>\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(v))_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert^{2}_{L}}

(8.100)

point-wise converge to a quadratic form
\phi:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\times\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\rightarrow\mathbb{C}
. Choosing a basis of
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
, then quadratic forms
\phi_{L}
and
\phi
are given by their matrix forms, and point-wise convergence implies that every matrix component
(\phi_{L})_{ij}
converge to
\phi_{ij}
. Therefore
\phi_{L}
uniformly converges to
\phi
on any compact subset
K\subset\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\times\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
. Let us take
K
to be the diagonal unit sphere
\mathbb{S}^{\Delta}_{d}=\{(u,u)\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\times\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\>|\>\rVert u\rVert=1\}
, then the uniform convergence implies that there exists
M>0
such that
\forall L\geq M
, the inequality
|\phi_{L}(u,u)-\phi(u,u)|<\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{d}
holds for all
(u,u)\in\mathbb{S}^{\Delta}_{d}
. On the other hand, (
8.99
) implies that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{d}\leq\phi(u,u)\leq 1,\quad\forall(u,u)\in\mathbb{S}^{\Delta}_{d}.

(8.101)

Whence the following inequality holds for all
L\geq M
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{d}\leq\phi_{L}(u,u)\leq 1+\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{k}{k+n}\right)^{d},\quad\forall(u,u)\in\mathbb{S}^{\Delta}_{d}.

(8.102)

Lemma
8.39
follows from (
8.102
). In fact we can take

\displaystyle B_{d}=\max\left[\sqrt{2}\left(1+\frac{n}{k}\right)^{\frac{d}{2}},\;\max_{\ell<M}\sup_{(u,u)\in\mathbb{S}^{\Delta}_{d}}\left(\phi_{\ell}(u,u)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\phi_{\ell}(u,u)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)\right]

(8.103)

to fulfill (
8.89
).
∎

Proof of Proposition
8.38
.

Since
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}
have uniformly bounded degree, there exists
m\in\mathbb{Z}
such that
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}(\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d})\subseteq\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d+m}
for all
d\in\mathbb{N}
. Let us fix a choice of such
m
in the rest of the proof.

“
\Longrightarrow
”. By our assumption, for every
d\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
D_{d}>0
such that

\displaystyle\rVert\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert\leq D_{d}L^{-h}\rVert u\rVert\text{ holds for all }u\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}.

(8.104)

Here
\rVert\cdot\rVert
is the norm on
\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
induced by
(\cdot|\cdot)
via the isomorphism
\Phi_{\infty}:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)\cong L_{k\varpi_{r}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{sl}}(n)_{k})\otimes\mathrm{Fock}_{kr}(\widehat{\gl}(1)_{kn})
. By Proposition
7.7
, there exists
M>0
such that such that

\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d+m}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d+m}}\text{ for all $L\geq M$}.

For
L\geq M
and
u\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert_{L}}&=\frac{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert_{L}}\\
\text{\small by Lemma \ref{lem:bound of norm}}\quad&\leq B_{d+m}\rVert\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert\\
\text{\small by \eqref{A converges to zero}}\quad&\leq B_{d+m}D_{d}L^{-h}\rVert u\rVert\\
\text{\small by Lemma \ref{lem:bound of norm}}\quad&\leq B_{d}B_{d+m}D_{d}L^{-h}\frac{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert_{L}},\end{split}

which implies that
\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert_{L}\leq B_{d}B_{d+m}D_{d}L^{-h}\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert_{L}
. Since

\displaystyle\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}}:\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}\text{ is an isomorphism by our choice of $L$},

it follows that

\displaystyle\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}(v)\rVert_{L}\leq B_{d}B_{d+m}D_{d}L^{-h}\rVert v\rVert_{L}\text{ holds for all }v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}.

(8.105)

(
8.105
) implies that
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}
has order
O(L^{-h})
. In fact we can take

\displaystyle C_{d}=\max\left[B_{d}B_{d+m}D_{d},\;\max_{\ell<M}(\ell^{h}\cdot\rVert\prescript{\ell}{}{\mathcal{A}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n\ell+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}}\rVert_{\ell})\right]\text{ to fulfill \eqref{A is of order L^-h}},

where
\rVert\prescript{\ell}{}{\mathcal{A}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n\ell+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}}\rVert_{\ell}
is the operator norm of the restriction of
\prescript{\ell}{}{\mathcal{A}}
to subspace
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n\ell+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}
, that is

\displaystyle\rVert\prescript{\ell}{}{\mathcal{A}}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n\ell+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}}\rVert_{\ell}=\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{n\ell+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}\\
\rVert u\rVert_{\ell}=1\end{subarray}}\rVert\prescript{\ell}{}{\mathcal{A}}(u)\rVert_{\ell}.

“
\Longleftarrow
”. By our assumption, for every
d\in\mathbb{N}
there exists a constant
C^{\prime}_{d}>0
such that

\displaystyle\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}(v)\rVert_{L}\leq C^{\prime}_{d}L^{-h}\rVert v\rVert_{L}\text{ holds for all }v\in\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d}.

(8.106)

By Proposition
7.7
, there exists
M>0
such that such that

\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}|_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d+m}}=\mathrm{Id}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)_{\leq d+m}}\text{ for all $L\geq M$}.

For
L\geq M
and
u\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
, by Lemma
8.39
we have

\displaystyle\rVert\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert

\displaystyle\leq B_{d+m}\frac{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert_{L}}

\displaystyle=B_{d+m}\frac{\rVert\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert_{L}}

by (
8.106
)

\displaystyle\leq B_{d+m}C_{d}^{\prime}L^{-h}\frac{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert_{L}}{\rVert\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(\omega)\rVert_{L}}

Lemma
8.39

\displaystyle\leq B_{d}B_{d+m}C_{d}^{\prime}L^{-h}\rVert u\rVert.

Let us take

\displaystyle D^{\prime}_{d}=\max\left[B_{d}B_{d+m}C_{d}^{\prime},\;\max_{\ell<M}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}\\
\rVert v\rVert=1\end{subarray}}\rVert\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{n\ell+r}\circ\prescript{\ell}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{n\ell+r}(v)\rVert\right],

then
\rVert\overline{\sigma}^{\infty}_{nL+r}\circ\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\circ\overline{p}^{\infty}_{nL+r}(u)\rVert\leq D^{\prime}_{d}L^{-h}\rVert u\rVert
holds for all
u\in\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)_{\leq d}
and all
L\in\mathbb{N}
. Thus
\prescript{L}{}{\mathcal{A}}\xrightarrow{O(L^{-h})}0
by definition.
∎

9
Applications

9.1
Gelfand-Tsetlin bases of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)

Definition 9.1
.

A GT pattern of height
n
and length
N
is an
(n+1)
-tuple of length-
N
integer arrays
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{n+1})
,
\Lambda_{i}=(\Lambda_{i,1}\geq\cdots\geq\Lambda_{i,N})\in\mathbb{Z}^{N}
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}\overset{1}{\searrow}\Lambda_{2}\overset{1}{\searrow}\cdots\overset{1}{\searrow}\Lambda_{n}\overset{1}{\searrow}\Lambda_{n+1}=\Lambda_{1}^{\shortdownarrow}.

(9.1)

See Definition
8.5
for the meaning of
\Lambda_{1}^{\shortdownarrow}
, and see Definition
8.9
for the meaning of
\overset{1}{\searrow}
. We denote the set of GT patterns of height
n
and length
N
by
\mathrm{GT}(N,n)
. In plain words, the condition (
9.1
) is equivalent to the condition that
\Lambda_{n+1,i}=\Lambda_{1,i}-k
and
\Lambda_{i,j}\geq\Lambda_{i+1,j}\geq\Lambda_{i,j+1}
for all possible
i
and
j
.

We say that a GT pattern
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{n+1})
is non-negative if and only if
\Lambda_{n+1}\in\mathbb{N}^{N}
. We denote the set of non-negative GT patterns of height
n
and length
N
by
\mathrm{GT}(N,n)_{+}
.

Remark 9.1
.

For a non-negative GT pattern
\Lambda\in\mathrm{GT}(N,n)_{+}
, we can associate
\Lambda
to a conventional Gelfand-Tsetlin pattern
[
65
]
by the following arrangement:

\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{n+1})\mapstochar\rightarrow\left(\lambda_{i,j}\in\mathbb{N}\right)_{1\leq i\leq N+n}^{1\leq j\leq N+n+1-i},

(9.2)

where

\displaystyle\lambda_{i,j}=\begin{cases}\Lambda_{i,j}&,\text{ if }i\leq n\text{ and }j\leq N,\\
0&,\text{ if }i\leq n\text{ and }j>N,\\
\Lambda_{1,i+j-(n+1)}-k&,\text{ if }i>n.\end{cases}

(9.3)

One can check that
(\lambda_{i,j})
satisfy the defining condition for a conventional Gelfand-Tsetlin pattern:
\lambda_{i,j}\geq\lambda_{i+1,j}\geq\lambda_{i,j+1}
for all possible
i
and
j
. Conversely, if
\left(\lambda_{i,j}\in\mathbb{N}\right)_{1\leq i\leq N+n}^{1\leq j\leq N+n+1-i}
is a Gelfand-Tsetlin pattern such that
\lambda_{1,j}=0
for all
j>N
, and
\lambda_{i,j}=\lambda_{1,i+j-(n+1)}-k
for all
i>n
and all possible
j
, we can associate
(\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{n+1})\in\mathrm{GT}(N,n)_{+}
by letting
\Lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i}
for all
i\leq n+1
.

Theorem 9.1

(Spectral Decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
)
.

The action of the quantum minors

\{{A}_{m}(u)\}_{1\leq m\leq n}
(see equation (
8.7
)) on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)
has simple spectrum, such that the eigenvectors are labelled by non-negative GT patterns of height
n
and length
N:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{N}(n,k)=\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathrm{GT}(N,n)_{+}}V_{\Lambda},\quad\dim V_{\Lambda}=1,

(9.4)

and the eigenvalue of
A_{m}(u)
on
V_{\Lambda}
,
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{n+1})
is

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{N}\frac{u-\Lambda_{m+1,i}-N+i}{u-\Lambda_{1,i}-N+i}.

(9.5)

Proof.

Recall the decomposition (
8.22
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{C}[X,A]=\bigoplus_{\lambda=(\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{N})\in\mathbb{N}^{N}}V^{N+n}_{\lambda}\otimes(V^{N}_{\lambda})^{*},

It is well-known that
V^{N+n}_{\lambda}
has the Gelfand-Tsetlin bases
[
65
]
:

\displaystyle V^{N+n}_{\lambda}=\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathcal{T}_{\lambda}}V(\Lambda),\quad\dim V(\Lambda)=1,

where
\mathcal{T}_{\lambda}
is the set of arrays
\left(\lambda_{i,j}\in\mathbb{N}\right)_{1\leq i\leq N+n}^{1\leq j\leq N+n+1-i}
such that

\displaystyle\lambda_{1,j}=\begin{cases}\lambda_{j}&,\text{ if }j\leq N,\\
0&,\text{ if }j>N,\end{cases}\quad\text{and $\lambda_{i,j}\geq\lambda_{i+1,j}\geq\lambda_{i,j+1}$ for all possible $i$ and $j$}.

V(\Lambda)
is uniquely characterized by the condition that
V(\Lambda)
belongs to the subspace
\bigoplus_{\nu}M^{\lambda}_{\nu,\lambda_{\ell+1}}\otimes V^{\ell}_{\nu}\otimes V^{N+n-\ell}_{\lambda_{\ell+1}}
in the branching decomposition

\end{remark}\par

9.2
Semi-infinite Gelfand-Tsetlin bases of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)

By the Theorem
8.4
, the transition map
p_{N}
is a Yangian module map with respect to the Yangian action given by
\widetilde{T}(u)
. Therefore
p_{N}
maps common eigenvectors of
\{\widetilde{A}_{m}(u)\}_{1\leq m\leq n}
to common eigenvectors of
\{\widetilde{A}_{m}(u)\}_{1\leq m\leq n}
. It can be read out from the eigenvalue (
) that

\displaystyle p_{N}(V_{\Lambda})=\begin{cases}V_{\Lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow}}&,\text{ if $\Lambda_{1}$ is cuttable},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise},\end{cases}

(9.10)

where
\Lambda^{\natural\shortdownarrow}=(\Lambda_{1}^{\natural\shortdownarrow},\cdots,\Lambda_{n+1}^{\natural\shortdownarrow})
. This motivates our next definition.

Definition 9.2
.

A semi-infinite GT pattern of height
n
and type
r
is an
(n+1)
-tuple of integer arrays
\Lambda=(\Lambda_{1},\cdots,\Lambda_{n+1})
,
\Lambda_{i}=(\Lambda_{i,1}\geq\Lambda_{i,2}\geq\cdots)\in\mathbb{Z}^{\infty}
such that

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}\overset{1}{\searrow}\Lambda_{2}\overset{1}{\searrow}\cdots\overset{1}{\searrow}\Lambda_{n}\overset{1}{\searrow}\Lambda_{n+1}=\Lambda_{1}^{\shortdownarrow},

(9.11)

and that
\Lambda
has the following asymptotic behaviour:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{i,j}=-k\lfloor\frac{j+i-2-r}{n}\rfloor\text{ for }j\gg 0.

(9.12)

We denote the set of semi-infinite GT patterns of height
n
and type
r
by
\mathrm{GT}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
. And we define
\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}
to be the semi-infinite GT pattern which saturates the condition (
9.12
), i.e.

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}_{i,j}=-k\lfloor\frac{j+i-2-r}{n}\rfloor\text{ for all }i,j.

(9.13)

The semi-infinite GT patterns uniformizes non-negative GT patterns in the following sense. For arbitrary
L\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a surjective map
\mathrm{GT}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)\twoheadrightarrow\mathrm{GT}(nL+r,n)
given by:

\displaystyle f_{L}:\mathrm{GT}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)\ni\Lambda\mapstochar\rightarrow(\Lambda_{i,j}+kL)_{1\leq i\leq n+1}^{1\leq j\leq nL+r}\in\mathrm{GT}(nL+r,n).

(9.14)

Moreover, for a fixed element
\Lambda\in\mathrm{GT}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
,
f_{L}(\Lambda)
belongs to
\mathrm{GT}(nL+r,n)_{+}
for sufficiently large
L
, due to the asymptotic behaviour (
9.12
).

Definition 9.3
.

In
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}_{nL+r}(n,k)
, for an element
\Lambda\in\mathrm{GT}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
, we set

\displaystyle V_{\Lambda}:=\begin{cases}V_{f_{L}(\Lambda)}&,\text{ if }f_{L}(\Lambda)\in\mathrm{GT}(nL+r,n)_{+},\\
0&,\text{ otherwise}.\end{cases}

(9.15)

Using the above notation, (
9.10
) can be compactly written as
p_{nL+r}(V_{\Lambda})=V_{\Lambda}
for a
\Lambda\in\mathrm{GT}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
. Taking inverse limit
L\rightarrow\infty
, we get a decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
into one-dimensional subspaces labelled by
\mathrm{GT}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)
.

Theorem 9.2

(Spectral Decomposition of
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
)
.

The action of the quantum minors

\{\widetilde{A}_{m}(u)\}_{1\leq m\leq n}
on
\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)
has simple spectrum, such that the eigenvectors are labelled by semi-infinite GT patterns of height
n
and type
r:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{H}}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n,k)=\bigoplus_{\Lambda\in\mathrm{GT}^{(r)}_{\infty}(n)}V_{\Lambda},\quad\dim V_{\Lambda}=1,

(9.16)

and the eigenvalue of
\widetilde{A}_{m}(u)
on
V_{\Lambda}
is

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{r}\left(\frac{u-r-\Lambda_{m+1,i}+i}{u-r-\Lambda_{1,i}+i}\right)\times\prod_{i=r+1}^{\infty}\left(\frac{u-r-\Lambda_{m+1,i}+i}{u-r-\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}_{m+1,i}+i}\times\frac{u-r-\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}_{1,i}+i}{u-r-\Lambda_{1,i}+i}\right).

(9.17)

Proof.

We have constructed the decomposition (
9.16
) in the above discussions, let us prove (
9.17
). We observe that

\displaystyle\prod_{i=r+1}^{nL+r}\frac{u-r-\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}_{1,i}+i}{u-r-\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}_{m+1,i}+i}=\prod_{j=0}^{m-1}\frac{\left(1+\frac{u-j}{n+k}\right)_{L}}{\left(1+\frac{u-j+k}{n+k}\right)_{L}}.

(9.18)

Therefore, for a sufficiently large
L
, after substituting
\Lambda
in (
) with the truncation
f_{L}(\Lambda)
, the eigenvalue of
\widetilde{A}_{m}(u)
on
V_{\Lambda}
is

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{r}\left(\frac{u-r-\Lambda_{m+1,i}+i}{u-r-\Lambda_{1,i}+i}\right)\times\prod_{i=r+1}^{nL+r}\left(\frac{u-r-\Lambda_{m+1,i}+i}{u-r-\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}_{m+1,i}+i}\times\frac{u-r-\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}_{1,i}+i}{u-r-\Lambda_{1,i}+i}\right).

(9.19)

The eigenvalue (
9.19
) stabilizes for
L\gg 0
because
\Lambda_{i,j}=\Lambda^{\mathrm{vac}}_{i,j}
for
j\gg 0
, therefore we can take
L\rightarrow\infty
and get (
9.17
).
∎

Definition 9.4
.

For two sequences of non-increasing integers
\beta=(\beta_{1}\geq\beta_{2}\geq\cdots)
and
\gamma=(\gamma_{1}\geq\gamma_{2}\geq\cdots)
such that
\beta_{i}\geq\gamma_{i}
for all
i
, we define the skew Young diagram associated to
\beta/\gamma
to be

\displaystyle\mathrm{SYD}(\beta/\gamma):=\left\{(i,j)\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}\>|\>i\geq 1,\beta_{i}\geq j>\gamma_{i}\right\}.

(9.20)

We represent
(i,j)\in\mathrm{SYD}(\beta/\gamma)
pictorially as a unit box in
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with the center
(i,j)
, the coordinates
i
and
j
on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
increasing from top to bottom and from left to right respectively. For example:

\begin{subarray}{c}\mathrm{SYD}(\beta/\gamma)\text{ for }\\
\beta=(6,5,3,3,2,1,\cdots)\\
\gamma=(4,3,3,0,0,-1,\cdots)\end{subarray}\qquad\hbox to33.68pt{\vbox to38.14pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\enskip\lower-30.53308pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}

height=8.4pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-8.4pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 8.00006pt\hrule width=8.79993pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern
